# Verevon Space: audit of Teams, Slack, Grok Bot, Buzz and QM

*Audit T1, as of 2026-10-14. Scope: the Verevon Space (a chat room shared by people and agents) compared with Microsoft Teams, Slack, Grok Bot, Buzz and QM. Status: Read-only audit; nothing in the product or the reference apps was changed.*

## How to read this document

**Tiers.** Every recommendation sits in one of three tiers.

- **PRIORITY** = do first: gaps that block or visibly undercut the vision, table-stakes capabilities every comparable reference has and Verevon lacks or half has, real safety, security or data-governance risks, and high-leverage unique capabilities with acceptable effort.
- **RECOMMENDED** = clear evidenced value that supports the vision but does not block it, larger efforts that need sequencing, and differentiators worth adopting after the priority set.
- **NICE TO HAVE** = polish, niche, speculative or modest value.

**Provenance tags.** A tag in square brackets ends every factual sentence or paragraph about a reference app, repo or product. [REA] = observed through the REA reverse-engineering tool on a shipped app. [SRC] = read directly in source code or shipped files (bundles, manifests, strings, locale files). [DOC] = documentation only, public or internal. [INF] = inference; the basis is stated. Where a claim rests only on documentation or inference, the sentence says so.

**Verevon today.** Each item carries a status for Verevon at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10): **Absent** (nothing exists), **Partial** (part of the capability exists in code), **Stub** (an endpoint or placeholder exists but does not do the work), **Planned** (a design or plan document exists, no code). "Shipped" (used in the baseline counts, the common-ground tables and the strengths) means present in source, not deployed; "Unknown" (one common-ground row) means the capability was not verified either way. Every status was verified by reading the Verevon code; verified data supersedes earlier estimates.

**Effort.** S, M, L, XL are relative sizes after verification; they count the work to close the stated gap, not the whole feature area.

**Ids and anchors.** Recommendations are numbered F-t1-001 to F-t1-096. Each heading carries an anchor of the same name, so a link such as [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) jumps to the item. Verevon claims cite code paths relative to the CoresSystem repository, for example apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:2498.

**Prevalence.** "n of 5 references" counts how many of the five sources show a capability.

**Abbreviations.** SSE = Server-Sent Events; SSRF = server-side request forgery; AG-UI = the agent-UI event stream protocol; MCP = Model Context Protocol; SSO = single sign-on; SCIM = System for Cross-domain Identity Management; SFU = selective forwarding unit (media server for calls); ASR = automatic speech recognition; BFF = backend-for-frontend (the Verevon Rust gateway); NATS = the message bus between planes; HITL = human in the loop; SIEM = security information and event management; TTL = time to live; DM = direct message. Quarry is Verevon's Ingestion Plane crawl and browser executor; Letta is the external agent-memory service that Session Core mirrors memory to. S2.7, S3.1, S3.4 to S3.6, S4.5 and S5.1 are section numbers of the Verevon QM adoption plan (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/docs/VEREVON_QM_COMPARISON_AND_ADOPTION_PLAN_2026-08-13.md).

## 1. Executive summary

### 1.1 Goal

A Verevon Space should work like Teams and Slack for people (rooms, groups, direct messages, many people in one conversation) and like Grok Bot for agents (personal agents that work like a colleague with their own computer and browser), inside Verevon's authority model. This audit compares the five references with the Verevon code, finds what they all share (table stakes), which one does each thing best, what sets each apart, and where Verevon is behind or ahead. The result is 96 recommendations in three tiers, five delivery waves, ten architecture decisions that need an ADR (Architecture Decision Record) first, and the open questions leadership must answer.

### 1.2 Where Verevon stands today

The code-verified baseline holds 227 capability records across 12 categories: 102 shipped, 51 partial, 63 absent, 7 stub and 4 planned. [SRC]

The strongest categories are the ones a chat product does not usually lead with. Governance, security and compliance has 12 of 20 records shipped and only 2 absent. UX patterns and layout has 8 of 11 shipped. Agents in the room has 21 of 42 shipped, the largest shipped count anywhere, and org and membership has 16 of 30. Client shell has 5 of 8 shipped, although Verevon is web-only and has no desktop shell. [SRC]

The weakest categories are the human-chat basics. Calls and meetings has nothing shipped: 3 absent, 3 partial, 1 planned. Messaging core has 16 shipped against 14 absent, 8 partial and 1 stub; the absent records include human-only posts, direct messages, reactions, edit and delete, and attachments, and message search is absent under Search and discovery. Collaborative artifacts has 3 of 9 shipped. Notifications and attention has 5 of 17 shipped with 6 absent, and realtime and sync has 7 of 15 shipped with no room-level push. [SRC]

The honest picture: Verevon has an authority-first agent cockpit with stronger governance than any reference, sitting on a conversation model that is still an AI prompt box rather than a room. Signed Control decisions, agents as service subjects on the roster, audience-bound reads and fail-closed Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) are ahead of all five references. [SRC] What is missing is most of what people expect from Teams or Slack on day one, plus live visibility of agent work for everyone in the room.

### 1.3 Headline findings

1. **Every Space post is a model turn.** The composer always calls streamChat, the gateway has no POST route for a human message, and a non-owner reply is refused twice in Session Core because append authority is checked against the thread owner, although Control already issues a member-capable thread:append decision. [SRC] This is the single largest gap; most human-chat table stakes hang off it ([F-t1-001](#F-t1-001), [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002)).
2. **There is no room-level push.** The Space page polls every 6 seconds while visible, the gateway has no Space stream route, and Session Core's outbox publishes only audit and run-event subjects. [SRC] A sequenced, resumable, authorization-at-delivery substrate is the precondition for live agent work, notifications and calls ([F-t1-003](#F-t1-003)).
3. **Only the sender sees an agent work.** Run events are authorized as run-owner reads and proxied with the caller's own token, so other members get a status row and the approval card; the Work tab is read-only. [SRC] The building blocks (AG-UI stream, step cards, queue and cancel routes, browser live-frame URL) exist outside Spaces and need a room-audience decision ([F-t1-004](#F-t1-004), [F-t1-020](#F-t1-020)).
4. **Approvals resume, but only for three action kinds.** A durable continuation descriptor and delivery worker resume execute_provider_action, book_shipment and tickets.create; shell, browser and MCP tools fail closed, the decide path returns a quarantine placeholder, and there are no once/session/always grant scopes. [SRC] This settles the earlier dispute over resume ([F-t1-010](#F-t1-010)).
5. **Agent memory is not Space-aware.** Session Core extracts memory candidates from every Space post and stores them as user-level memory owned by the thread owner, mirrored to Letta; agent_memory has no space, audience or label column. [SRC] Personal agents must stay out of shared rooms until this is fixed ([F-t1-012](#F-t1-012), [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011)).
6. **The human-chat basics are absent, not partial.** Direct messages, reactions, edit and delete, attachments and message search do not exist in the Space code. [SRC] Reactions and attachments are in 5 of 5 references, and DMs, edit and delete, and message search in 4 of 5; F-t1-013 is rated Partial only because connector imports already carry Space provenance, not because room attachments exist ([F-t1-016](#F-t1-016), [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017), [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013), [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018)).
7. **Shipped governance looks done but is not usable.** The deny-by-default effect policy can only be written through a service-principal PUT and an operator script, with no admin route or UI and plain-string denials; the SCIM (System for Cross-domain Identity Management) endpoint answers success without creating or changing anyone; and any org member can read all org audit events because audit-core never consults the delegated role. [SRC] The audit read gap is confirmed and should be fixed as a bug ([F-t1-006](#F-t1-006), [F-t1-023](#F-t1-023), [F-t1-031](#F-t1-031)).
8. **ZDR is stronger than the first pass assumed.** Session Core already refuses every non-read scope for ZDR callers and gates durable and Letta memory; only the Space decision's own ZDR flag is discarded, and that path is unreachable today. [SRC] The item moved from Priority to Recommended and from M to S ([F-t1-005](#F-t1-005)).
9. **The agent sandbox is confined but uncapped.** Filesystem confinement, seccomp and dropped capabilities are real and fail closed, and no Space agent code path has network at all; what is missing is CPU, memory and pid caps and a per-room egress allowlist through Quarry. [SRC] ([F-t1-021](#F-t1-021))
10. **Attention across rooms does not exist.** Notification-core has preferences and a delivery state machine, but no Space event produces a notification, sidebar unread dots are browser-local, and there is no Home inbox. [SRC] ([F-t1-008](#F-t1-008), [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009))

### 1.5 Decisions leadership must make

Ten topics need an ADR before the related items start, because they stretch plane ownership, ZDR or Space authority:

1. **Ownership of human room messages and shared-thread append authority.** Which plane is the source of truth for human posts, reuse of the existing member-capable Control thread:append decision, a turn_origin field, DM kinds ([F-t1-001](#F-t1-001), [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002), [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003), [F-t1-016](#F-t1-016), [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017)).
2. **Realtime delivery under ZDR and Space authority.** Refs and metadata only on the wire, authority at subscribe and every delivery, content-free wake push, no content cache for ZDR orgs ([F-t1-003](#F-t1-003), [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004), [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009), [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035), [F-t1-080](#F-t1-080)).
3. **Agent memory ownership, audience labels and declassification** ([F-t1-012](#F-t1-012), [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011), [F-t1-072](#F-t1-072)).
4. **Personal agents as service subjects**: owner-derived admission, access lists, cascade on suspension ([F-t1-011](#F-t1-011), [F-t1-043](#F-t1-043), [F-t1-023](#F-t1-023)).
5. **Multi-agent delegation and whose authority an agent acts under** ([F-t1-025](#F-t1-025), [F-t1-019](#F-t1-019), [F-t1-038](#F-t1-038)).
6. **Agent computer network boundary, persistence and local execution** ([F-t1-021](#F-t1-021), [F-t1-028](#F-t1-028), [F-t1-020](#F-t1-020), [F-t1-096](#F-t1-096)).
7. **Calls and meetings**: reverse the documented huddle non-goal, buy the SFU, choose the meeting model ([F-t1-029](#F-t1-029), [F-t1-050](#F-t1-050), [F-t1-066](#F-t1-066), [F-t1-067](#F-t1-067)).
8. **Retention, legal hold and ZDR precedence** ([F-t1-051](#F-t1-051), [F-t1-077](#F-t1-077), [F-t1-005](#F-t1-005)).
9. **Message search index placement and exclusion rules** ([F-t1-018](#F-t1-018), [F-t1-038](#F-t1-038)).
10. **External surfaces and guests**: Channel Plane, linked identities, external audiences ([F-t1-075](#F-t1-075), [F-t1-053](#F-t1-053), [F-t1-093](#F-t1-093), [F-t1-023](#F-t1-023)).

The most important open questions, beyond those ADRs:

- **Product stance.** Does Verevon accept the hybrid brief over the "no chat parity" and "huddles are a non-goal" lines in docs/space-defenition.md? This decides calls, meeting intelligence and screen sharing. [DOC]
- **Default room agent.** Should the always-on default assistant remain once human-only posting exists, or become an explicit binding with mention-only wake?
- **ZDR orgs.** Is a refs-only experience in push, previews, search and digests acceptable to them?
- **Channel Plane and desktop shell.** Is the Channel Plane scheduled, and is a Tauri shell wanted after the PWA (Progressive Web App)?
- **Sandbox in production.** Does execution-core fail closed when bubblewrap is unavailable? Verification confirmed that unsandboxed spawn is refused for the read-only and workspace-write policies; the production behaviour of the fallback path still deserves a focused check. [SRC]

Two earlier open questions are now answered by verification: approvals do resume for three idempotent action kinds and fail closed for the rest ([F-t1-010](#F-t1-010)), and the org audit read path is confirmed open to any org member ([F-t1-031](#F-t1-031)). [SRC]

### 1.6 What changed during verification

Two independent passes followed consolidation: a Verevon-side pass that read the code behind every item, and a reference-side pass that tried to refute the reference claims. All 96 items were checked; none remain unverified and none are low confidence. The Verevon-side pass kept 9 items as written, reworded 78 because the code showed a more precise gap, a better place to build or reusable parts, and gave 9 a demote verdict. Five of those changed tier: [F-t1-005](#F-t1-005) moved from Priority to Recommended, and [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029), [F-t1-066](#F-t1-066), [F-t1-068](#F-t1-068) and [F-t1-069](#F-t1-069) moved from Recommended to Nice to have. The tiers went from 24/45/27 to 23/42/31 (Priority/Recommended/Nice to have). Effort was revised on 29 items, almost all upward, because bundled items span more planes than first estimated; the verified statuses are 68 Partial, 21 Absent, 4 Stub and 3 Planned. The reference-side pass re-examined the 24 items that were Priority before verification (F-t1-001 to F-t1-024; Recommended and Nice-to-have reference claims were not re-checked), confirmed 2 in full and 22 in part, and corrected prevalence counts on 17 items; the corrected counts are used throughout and the refuted claims were removed.

## 2. What every reference shares

Across the five references (Teams, Slack, Grok Bot, Buzz and QM) 72 capabilities were classed as shared by at least 3 of 5 before reference-side verification; after correction a few rows count partial evidence and one (privacy-respecting telemetry) falls to 1 of 5, kept here for traceability. They fall into three groups: the human chat core that Teams, Slack and Buzz ship and that Grok Bot and QM lean on, the agent-in-the-room layer that Buzz, Grok Bot and QM ship, and the client and governance plumbing that every native app carries. Verevon is at parity on five of the 72 (agents as labelled members, server-resolved tenant binding, server-managed chat with zero-data-retention on top, accessibility, and empty states), is partial on 38, lacks 27 outright, has one stub (SCIM) and one unverified (pluggable harnesses); the largest gaps are the human chat core and the realtime push substrate. Prevalence is written as "n of 5 references"; where verification corrected a count, the corrected count is used and the cluster count is not repeated. ZDR means zero-data-retention; SFU means selective forwarding unit; SSO is single sign-on; SCIM is the System for Cross-domain Identity Management; MCP is the Model Context Protocol; PWA is progressive web app; CSP is Content Security Policy.

### 2.1 Messaging core

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shared rooms with human-first posting (agents as ordinary members) | 3 of 5 native (Buzz, Slack, Teams); QM via Slack | Partial | [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) |
| Threads: reply-in-thread, multi-author threads, thread panel | 5 of 5 (Grok Bot threads are bot task threads) | Partial | [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) |
| Direct messages and group DMs | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-016](#F-t1-016) |
| Mentions bound to exact identities, with group and broadcast handles | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-007](#F-t1-007) |
| Emoji reactions | 5 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017) |
| Edit and delete messages (audit, tombstones) | 4 of 5 (QM handles edits made on its Slack surface) | Absent | [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017) |
| Custom emoji, GIFs and shortcode completion | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017) |
| Rich-text composer (formatting, code, lists, Enter behaviour) | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-032](#F-t1-032) |
| Persistent drafts | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-032](#F-t1-032) |
| Attachments and file upload pipeline | 5 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013) |
| Read state: per-thread unread, mark unread, read receipts | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-042](#F-t1-042) |
| Voice notes and audio or video clips with transcripts | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-082](#F-t1-082) |

The baseline is a room where a post is a plain append, agents wake only on mention or subscription, any member can continue a thread, and messages carry reactions, revisions, attachments and a per-thread read frontier; Buzz ships all of this in source and Slack and Teams document it [SRC, DOC]. Verevon is behind on nearly every row: every Space post is a model turn because `SpaceRoomComposer.submitMessage` always calls `streamChat` (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:375-425), a non-owner reply is refused in Session Core by `authorize_thread_owner(Mutate)`, Space kinds are limited to personal and room in the gateway create route (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:2850-2900), and `sessions.proto` offers only `AppendMessage` plus whole-thread deletes, so there is no edit, delete or reaction store (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sessions.proto:18-204) [SRC]. The one point of parity is agent mentions: the browser sends only `mentioned_agent_ref` and the gateway re-resolves it against the Control roster, which is the identity-binding pattern Buzz uses on the client [SRC]. The composer is a two-row textarea cleared before a send starts, so a failed send loses the draft [SRC].

### 2.2 Org and membership

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Role hierarchy, permission checks and role change | 5 of 5 graded roles; 3 of 5 with record-level evidence (Buzz, Slack, QM) | Partial | [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022) |
| Room visibility (open or private), directory, browse and self-join | 3 of 5 (Buzz, Teams, QM) | Absent | [F-t1-048](#F-t1-048) |
| Guests and external users | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-053](#F-t1-053) |
| Admin console and org admin operations | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) |
| Multi-account and multi-workspace switching in one client | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-055](#F-t1-055) |

Every reference grades members, and Buzz adds an owner or admin guard on elevated roles, a sole-owner rule, archive and TTL rooms, all checked under a per-channel advisory lock [SRC]. Control already stores and converges viewer, editor, manager and owner roles with an owner guard (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/repository.go:646-675), but Application can only grant editor and the gateway has only add and remove member routes, so there is no role change, rename, topic, archive or restore [SRC]. Discovery is absent at the schema level: `registered_spaces` has no visibility column and `space_memberships` has no join sequence (apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/017_space_authority.up.sql:10-66) [SRC]. Guests exist only as org-level email invitations with a 48 h default expiry in auth-core [SRC].

### 2.3 Calls and meetings

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Group audio or video calls and huddles started from a room | 3 of 5 (Slack, Teams, Buzz) | Absent | [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029) |
| Meeting transcription and AI recap into a searchable artifact | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-050](#F-t1-050) |
| Calendar, meeting scheduling and calendar-triggered agent work | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-036](#F-t1-036) |

The three references with room calls (Slack, Teams and Buzz) start a call from the room and keep it there; Slack documents the huddle as part of the channel, Buzz models it as a short-lived child channel and Teams carries the deepest engine, while Grok Bot contributes a client call state machine [DOC, SRC, REA]. Verevon has no call, huddle, SFU or WebRTC code in any plane, and `docs/space-defenition.md` lists huddles as a non-goal (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/docs/space-defenition.md:24) [SRC]. Only dictation-sized speech-to-text exists, capped at 25 MB of audio and a 60 s take, with no diarisation [SRC]. A calendar read action exists in integration-corev2, and capability-core's sweeper reauthorizes every scheduled slot through Control, but there is no room UI to create a schedule [SRC].

### 2.4 Search and discovery

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Permission-aware full-text message search (index plus per-hit re-authorization) | 4 of 5 (per-hit re-authorization verified only in Buzz) | Absent | [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018) |
| Scoped in-conversation search and Ctrl+F routing | 3 of 5 (Buzz, Teams, Slack partial) | Absent | [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018) |
| Search operator grammar (from:, in:, has:, before:/after:) | 3 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-069](#F-t1-069) |
| People directory and people search | 5 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-049](#F-t1-049) |
| Room directory: browse and join | 3 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-048](#F-t1-048) |

Buzz's search is the reference mechanism: a stored generated `tsvector` column with a GIN index, a NULL vector for sensitive kinds, `ts_rank_cd` ordering and an access predicate applied to every hit, while QM confirms the same `tsvector` approach over session entries [SRC]. Teams supplies the richest scope list, but only at the level of UI strings, and its search backend cannot be observed [REA]. Verevon has no search over Space messages, threads or runs: the gateway search router serves only web, similar, suggest, images, videos and answer (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/search.rs:30-45), and Ctrl/Cmd+K opens a knowledge search over Data v2 retrieval plus a client-side title filter [SRC]. On people, org-core already serves a member search endpoint that the gateway never proxies [SRC].

### 2.5 Notifications and attention

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Event-to-notification pipeline: typed signal classes, normalised payload, deep links | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008) |
| Per-conversation notification levels and per-event preferences | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008) |
| Personal cross-room activity inbox with typed events | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) |
| In-app unread counts, cross-room aggregates and mark-all-read | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) |
| OS badge, tray and taskbar cues | 3 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035) |
| Per-event notification sounds | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035) |
| Reminders and snooze on messages | 3 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-042](#F-t1-042) |

Slack validates one schema-checked payload (team, channel, message and thread ids plus a trace id) and runs it through client-side display and DND gates, and Teams exposes about 60 titled activity types in its feed [SRC, REA]. No reference ships an implemented notification rate limiter; Grok Bot carries a disabled config schema only [SRC]. Verevon emits only run-completion notifications to users who registered a per-run watcher, `mention.received` exists only as a seeded preference row with no producer, and the sidebar unread and working dots come from a browser-only store cleared when the reader leaves the room (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/space-live-work.ts:1-146) [SRC]. A durable per-member, per-room read marker does exist in Convex (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceReadMarkers.ts:1-153), which is the right base for a server-side aggregate [SRC]. Nothing for out-of-tab attention exists: no manifest, service worker, Notification or Push API, or `setAppBadge` call [SRC].

### 2.6 Apps and extensibility

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Public developer API, SDKs and rate-limit contract | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-076](#F-t1-076) |
| Platform slash commands and app command registry | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-041](#F-t1-041) |
| MCP server registry with shared or per-user credentials | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-037](#F-t1-037) |
| Workflow automation (builder, triggers, steps, run trace) | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-036](#F-t1-036) |

The shared shape is a registry of tools and commands that admins can allow or block, plus an automation builder with a run trace; QM ships the deepest automation model (fire keys, outcomes, loops with gates) and Teams the most explicit allowlisting [SRC, DOC]. Verevon's MCP registry is real, with private, shared and org scope, OAuth 2.1 with token refresh and a three-attempt retry, but tool exposure is resolved per org and requesting user with no Space input (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/runtime_registries.rs:715-765) [SRC]. Org-scoped API keys are minted, listed and revoked end to end, and a typed action registry exists, so the public API and command layer are extensions rather than new builds [SRC].

### 2.7 Agents in the room

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Agent as a first-class, labelled room member with owner provenance | 4 of 5 | Shipped | none needed |
| In-room activity, plan and tool-call cards | 3 of 5 shipped (Buzz, QM, Grok Bot); Slack DOC and Teams streaming flags | Partial | [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004) |
| Agent presence and live working state | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004) |
| In-room approval cards with grant scopes and durable pause and resume | 3 of 5 (Grok Bot, QM, Teams) | Partial | [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) |
| Audience-safe disclosure in shared rooms | 1 shipped (QM) plus 2 partial (Teams, Slack) | Partial | [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012) |
| Context assembly and compaction | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012) |
| Scheduled and event-triggered agent work | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-036](#F-t1-036) |
| Agent cost and usage: per-agent and per-room attribution, budgets | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027) |
| Pluggable agent harnesses (bring your own runtime) | 4 of 5 | Unknown | none needed (does not fit the Model Plane runtime) |

This is Verevon's strongest category. Room agents exist end to end: create-in-room, bind an org agent, pause, resume and revoke, agents held in Control as service subjects, and a binding that already stores `triggerModes`, `allowedTools` and `approvalMode` enforced by the gateway [SRC]. The gap is visibility and scope: inside a Space only the sender streams the turn, because Session Core authorizes run events with `authorize_run_owner(Read)` and the gateway proxies them with the caller's token, so other members see a 6 s poll (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/streams.rs:169-215) [SRC]. QM is the only reference with shipped audience intersection and labelled memory, whereas Verevon's Session Core extracts memory candidates from every Space post into user-level `agent_memory` with no space, audience or label column [SRC]. The cost ledger has tenant, actor, run, request, model and producer dimensions and no Space or agent column (apps/Model Plane/go/services/cost-core/internal/ledger/ledger.go:40-50) [SRC]. Pluggable harnesses were judged not to fit because an outside runtime would bypass ZDR and Model Plane authority [INF].

### 2.8 Collaborative artifacts

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Room canvas: one shared, agent-readable and agent-writable document per Space | 4 of 5 (Teams as meeting notes, a weak analogue) | Absent | [F-t1-015](#F-t1-015) |
| Room file library stored by an owner plane with provenance, grants and quotas | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013) |
| Artifact sharing: per-person and general access, view or edit roles, access requests | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-057](#F-t1-057) |

Slack documents the canvas product model and Buzz ships the write-safety mechanism: a transaction that takes a per-channel advisory lock, compares the head with the expected revision and only then inserts, so a rejected save is rolled back [DOC, SRC]. Verevon has no Space-owned document and no Canvas tab (`SpaceTabId` has six tabs, apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceCockpit.tsx:43); wiki-store has pages, append-only versions and diffs but `CreateVersion` takes no expected-head precondition (apps/Data Plane v2/services/wiki-store-go/internal/repo/wiki_repo.go:359-398) [SRC]. The file library is a read-only Knowledge tab with owner-plane plumbing only for connector imports, and Control `resource_grants` already supports an edit role that the gateway share route hard-codes away [SRC].

### 2.9 Realtime and sync architecture

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Persistent push channel delivering room events to every member | 5 of 5 (Grok Bot's server tail is flag-gated) | Absent | [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) |
| Stream health watchdog with polling safety net | QM in full; Teams and Grok Bot partial | Partial | [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) |
| Local replica or cache for instant paint and restart | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-080](#F-t1-080) |
| Server-managed chat, no E2EE for messages, selective owner-private encryption | 4 of 5 | Shipped | none needed |

Every reference pushes room events to every member; Buzz is the only one with a single fail-closed fan-out chokepoint shared by local and cross-node paths and a subscribe-time access check, and QM is the only one with a verified watchdog that resyncs on reconnect [SRC]. No reference was shown polling room messages while push is healthy [SRC]. Verevon has no room-level push: `SpacePage` polls the thread projection every 6 s while the tab is visible (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpacePage.tsx:273-338), the gateway has no Space stream route (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:649-738), and Convex has no per-Space event table or sequence [SRC]. The run-event SSE with `Last-Event-ID` and the AG-UI stream exist outside Spaces and are the natural base [SRC]. The local replica is deliberately low priority because of ZDR [INF].

### 2.10 Governance, security and compliance

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Server-resolved tenant binding and multi-tenant isolation | 4 of 5 | Shipped | none needed |
| Enterprise SSO, SCIM provisioning and identity federation | 3 of 5 for SSO plus Buzz partial; SCIM reference is Slack | Stub | [F-t1-023](#F-t1-023) |
| Secret and PII redaction in logs, telemetry and agent output | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-045](#F-t1-045) |
| Privacy-respecting telemetry, consent and diagnostics | 1 of 5 consent-deferred client analytics (Grok Bot); QM is a counter-example | Partial | [F-t1-005](#F-t1-005) |

Teams is not a SCIM reference, because it relies on Entra ID provisioning; Slack documents SCIM behind its Business+ and Enterprise plans, and QM's actor gate only filters Slack ingress [DOC, SRC]. QM sends unredacted error context to Sentry when enabled, which is an anti-pattern, not a model [SRC]. Verevon's only SCIM code is a fake-success intake in the Ingestion Plane that stores a webhook event and answers 201 or 204 without creating or changing anyone (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/api/server.go:1225-1290), and SSO sits behind a global flag [SRC]. Tool-result screening and persisted-output scrubbing exist, but `TrustClass` is a pure function of the tool name with no class for room-member text [SRC]. Session Core already enforces ZDR on the caller's token claim; only the Space decision's own `zero_data_retention` flag is decoded and discarded [SRC].

### 2.11 Client shell

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Native desktop shell wrapping the web client | 5 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-073](#F-t1-073) |
| Narrow, typed and validated web-to-native bridge | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-073](#F-t1-073) |
| OS protocol handler with single-instance link forwarding | 5 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-073](#F-t1-073) |
| Auto-update with release channels and safe install timing | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-073](#F-t1-073) |
| Signed packaging and multi-format distribution | 4 of 5 | Absent | [F-t1-073](#F-t1-073) |
| Entity deep-link routing and URL-synced view state | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-039](#F-t1-039) |
| Feature flags, remote config, preview rings and experiments | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) |
| In-app keyboard shortcuts and command palette | 4 of 5 shortcuts; palette 2 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-041](#F-t1-041) |
| Theming, high contrast, org branding and density | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-062](#F-t1-062) |
| Accessibility: screen readers, keyboard regions, reduced motion | 4 of 5 | Shipped | none needed |

All five references are native desktop apps; Teams shows the thinnest model, a native shell around a web UI with a versioned RPC over capability modules, and Buzz is the closest stack fit with Tauri 2 and Rust-owned state [REA, SRC]. Verevon is a Vite SPA with no manifest, service worker, Tauri, Electron or MSIX files and no desktop sign-in or updater endpoint (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/index.html:1-16) [SRC]. Because PWA and content-free push come first ([F-t1-035](#F-t1-035)), the shell is placed in the horizon wave [INF]. Space and tab deep links work but `?thread_id` is ignored, and a Cmd/Ctrl+K overlay exists without focus trap, arrow keys or Enter handling [SRC].

### 2.12 UX patterns and layout

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Primary navigation: rail plus sidebar with Home, Activity, Agents, Files destinations | 4 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) |
| Personal sidebar organisation: star, sections, sort, drag-drop | 4 of 5 (Buzz, Grok Bot, QM, Slack) | Partial | [F-t1-055](#F-t1-055) |
| Contextual right-hand pane with one header contract and resize | 3 of 5 (Grok Bot, Teams, Slack) | Partial | [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) |
| Thread side panel, pop-out thread window, inline replies from inbox | 3 of 5 (Slack DOC, Buzz, Grok Bot; Teams as inline replies) | Absent | [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) |
| Typed transcript rows for mixed human and agent rooms | 3 of 5 | Partial | [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004) |
| Empty states, zero states and suggested starters | 3 of 5 | Shipped | none needed |

The shared layout is a rail, a sidebar the user can reorganise, a main timeline with typed rows and one contextual right pane; Buzz puts Agents, Workflows and the inbox as first-class destinations behind one shared right panel, and Grok Bot's pane shows a close linger, inert-while-closing and focus hand-off, but no keyboard resize or persisted width [SRC]. Verevon's sidebar has fixed groups (Channels with the org room pinned first, Personal Space, All conversations) and a text filter, with no stars, sections, ordering, hide or mute (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/sidebar/CoreSidebarSpacesPanel.tsx:18-21) [SRC]. Buzz's 15 render classes and Grok Bot's telemetry row kinds are the models for typed rows; the approval row and `{agentRef, ownerRef}` union in the recommendation are Verevon synthesis [SRC, INF].

## 3. Best of breed: who does each thing best

| Capability area | Best reference | Why it is best (one sentence) | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Human-first posting and threads | Buzz; QM for the engagement rule | A post is a plain append tagged with the channel id, thread counters are kept in the same transaction at ingest, and QM lets any member continue a thread with a bot-stake rule for un-mentioned replies [SRC]. | [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) |
| Addressing and trigger policy | Buzz | A binding carries respond_to and subscribe fields that fail closed, first-match-wins rule triggers, and a composer address lock [SRC]. | [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002) |
| Ambient participation | QM | A cheap judge returns an act-or-stay-silent decision with a reason, standing orders and a stored ledger, a mechanism no other reference has [SRC]. | [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002) |
| Mentions | Buzz | The composer resolves names to subject ids, qualifies namesakes and blocks an ambiguous send, and the relay indexes mention rows [SRC]. | [F-t1-007](#F-t1-007) |
| Edit, delete and reactions | Buzz; QM for agent-visible revisions | Owner-of-agent retract keeps humans accountable for their agents, and QM appends revision entries and parses reactions for the agent (capped at 5 per reply) [SRC]. | [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017) |
| Attachments | Buzz; Slack for the pipeline | Buzz stores content-addressed media with metadata stripped, and Slack documents a three-step upload with a malware scan [SRC, DOC]. | [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013) |
| DMs and group DMs | Buzz; Teams for history choice | Buzz identifies a DM by a participant hash so a second open returns the existing DM, and Teams lets the adder choose how much history a newcomer sees [SRC, REA]. | [F-t1-016](#F-t1-016) |
| Push channel | Grok Bot | One multiplexed, sequenced stream with updated_seq cursors, cursor_too_old and generation frames, flag-gated and mid-migration in the shipped build [SRC]. | [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) |
| Delivery-time authorization | Buzz | One fail-closed fan-out chokepoint shared by local and cross-node paths, with access checked before subscribe registration [SRC]. | [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) |
| Stream watchdog | QM | SSE open and 30 s silence timeouts with a resync on every reconnect [SRC]. | [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) |
| Live run stream for all viewers | QM | AG-UI-shaped SSE per run with offset-keyed text deltas and a snapshot for late joiners, authorized by run visibility [SRC]. | [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004) |
| Activity and tool cards | Buzz | Fifteen render classes turn tool events into verb-object-outcome cards with a working strip and owner badge [SRC]. | [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004) |
| Cross-room inbox | Teams; Grok Bot for the needs-you queue | Teams exposes about 60 titled activity types with slices, and Grok Bot keeps one queue for approvals, questions and hand-offs [REA, SRC]. | [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) |
| Cross-room state aggregate | QM | One server-filtered session-state stream reports working, awaiting approval or idle per thread with participants [SRC]. | [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) |
| Notification pipeline | Slack | One schema-checked payload with a trace id runs through display and DND gates in the desktop shell [SRC]. | [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008) |
| Auto-follow rules | Teams | Threads are followed when mentioned, authored or participated, stated explicitly [REA]. | [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008) |
| Content-free push | Buzz | The push is only a wake signal; the client fetches the content, which is the only pattern compatible with ZDR [SRC]. | [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035) |
| Message search | Buzz | A stored generated tsvector with a GIN index, NULL vectors for sensitive kinds, and re-authorization of every hit [SRC]. | [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018) |
| Search scope UX | Teams | The richest scope list (channel, chat, community, thread, post, people), evidenced as UI strings only [REA]. | [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018) |
| Search as the asker | Slack; QM for the mechanism | An action token from the triggering event bounds the agent's search to the asker's reach and results are never stored [DOC, SRC]. | [F-t1-038](#F-t1-038) |
| Approvals | QM; Grok Bot for the card UX | QM stores the request, ends generation at the pause, lets only the requester decide with once, session or always scopes and re-runs the turn; Grok Bot orders levels never, ask, always with effective = min(user, team ceiling) [SRC]. | [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) |
| Personal and team agents | Grok Bot | A primary bot, OWNER or TEAM visibility, an access list with USER and MANAGER roles, and clone-to-team [SRC]. | [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011) |
| Private assistant beside the room | Teams | A private pane with an escalate-to-group step, evidenced for meeting chats [REA]. | [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011) |
| Audience-safe context and memory | QM | Write-time labels, an audience fold at read and a context cut when the audience changes are the only shipped implementation; Buzz's version is a draft design [SRC, DOC]. | [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012) |
| Agent browser and takeover | Grok Bot; QM for the grant | Hand-off with an instruction, take control, bot-wall detection and a live preview card, plus QM's scoped browser-model capability token [SRC]. | [F-t1-020](#F-t1-020) |
| Agent computer lifecycle | Grok Bot; QM for tokens and egress | Hibernate and pre-thaw with cost-aware wake, and QM's per-turn capability tokens with an authorising egress proxy [SRC]. | [F-t1-028](#F-t1-028) |
| Multi-agent turn-taking | Grok Bot | Per-member turn requests with nonce and deadline, speak, pass, skip and timeout outcomes, and a loop guard in shadow mode [SRC]. | [F-t1-025](#F-t1-025) |
| Agent self-API | QM | A discoverable API catalogue and a per-turn capability token (48 h default) re-verified by every handler [SRC]. | [F-t1-019](#F-t1-019) |
| Forms and secret drop | QM | A one-time form whose value goes to the keychain while the transcript records only that it was supplied [SRC]. | [F-t1-026](#F-t1-026) |
| Credential custody | QM; Grok Bot for per-person OAuth | A secrets broker with route classification and cross-person asks, and Grok Bot's shared plugin with per-person OAuth and draft cards [SRC]. | [F-t1-044](#F-t1-044) |
| Automations | QM | Crons with destinations, watches, loops with gates, fire keys and outcomes, runAs and recipient consent [SRC]. | [F-t1-036](#F-t1-036) |
| Run queue | QM | A unique one-running-per-session index, SKIP LOCKED claims, lease heartbeat and a reaper with crash-loop parking [SRC]. | [F-t1-040](#F-t1-040) |
| Agent cost | Grok Bot | Cost by flow and by bot with hard limits [SRC]. | [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027) |
| Room canvas | Slack for the product; Buzz for write safety | Rich blocks with comments, and append-only revisions guarded by an advisory-lock compare against the expected head [DOC, SRC]. | [F-t1-015](#F-t1-015) |
| Agent artifacts and viewers | Grok Bot | A lazy viewer registry for PDF, DOCX, XLSX, code and Mermaid is confirmed; the canvas-first rule is flag-gated and inherited [SRC]. | [F-t1-014](#F-t1-014) |
| Room roles and lifecycle | Buzz | Elevated roles granted only by an owner or admin, a sole-owner rule, archive and TTL rooms, all under a per-channel advisory lock [SRC]. | [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022) |
| Room directory and self-join | Buzz | An open channel directory in source, with Teams and QM as the other shipping examples [SRC]. | [F-t1-048](#F-t1-048) |
| Admin policy | Teams | Precedence direct over group over global, policy packages and admin allowlisting of apps, agents and models [DOC]. | [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) |
| Feature flags and rings | Teams | Layered providers resolved by ring, tenant and account with a local override [REA]. | [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) |
| SSO and SCIM | Teams for SSO; Slack for SCIM | Teams ships a native client broker against its own identity cloud, and Slack documents SCIM provisioning on its upper plans [REA, DOC]. | [F-t1-023](#F-t1-023) |
| Guests | Teams; QM for expiry | Teams defines guest tiers, and QM makes a guest expiry mandatory [REA, SRC]. | [F-t1-053](#F-t1-053) |
| Moderation | Buzz | Private reports to a queue, signed actions, tombstones and a ban that also stops the person's agents [SRC]. | [F-t1-043](#F-t1-043) |
| Retention | Slack | Org, workspace, channel and DM rules with an org lock [DOC]. | [F-t1-051](#F-t1-051) |
| Calls | Slack for the product; Grok Bot for the client; Teams for the engine | The huddle belongs to the room, Grok Bot ships explicit call phases, and Teams SlimCore carries the lifecycle depth [DOC, SRC]. | [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029) |
| Meeting recap and consent | Teams | Notes, tasks, topics and chapters with citations back to the recording, AI on and off, a privacy mode and recap visibility derived from transcript access [REA]. | [F-t1-050](#F-t1-050) |
| Command layer | Grok Bot; QM for the palette | Every shell action is a registered command with an id and hotkey consumed by one hotkey layer and a mod+K palette [SRC]. | [F-t1-041](#F-t1-041) |
| Deep links | Teams; QM for paging | One entity-and-action command path serves toasts and links, and QM pages with a ?seq= cursor [REA, SRC]. | [F-t1-039](#F-t1-039) |
| Timeline windows | Buzz | Server-computed windows with reply summaries, composite cursors and a tested scroll-anchoring policy [SRC]. | [F-t1-034](#F-t1-034) |
| Reliable send | Grok Bot | A send journal with queued, prepared, dispatching and accepted-awaiting-echo stages and a stale-while-revalidate resource state machine [SRC]. | [F-t1-033](#F-t1-033) |
| Sidebar organisation | Buzz; Teams for system sections | Per-group sort, synced sections and stars, drag-and-drop and a more-unread pill, with Teams adding computed system sections [SRC, REA]. | [F-t1-055](#F-t1-055) |
| Error recovery | Teams; Buzz for the root boundary | A diagnosis-specific native error curtain with Restart and Repair, and Buzz's root React error boundary with Reload [SRC]. | [F-t1-024](#F-t1-024) |
| Desktop shell | Teams for the model; Buzz for the stack | A thin native shell around a web UI with a versioned RPC over capability modules, and Buzz's Tauri 2 shell with Rust-owned state [REA, SRC]. | [F-t1-073](#F-t1-073) |

Taken together, the references split the work cleanly. Buzz is the best overall model for the human chat core and its substrate: posting, threads, mentions, reactions, DMs, search, delivery-time authorization, timeline windows, roles and moderation, all readable in source [SRC]. QM is the best model for safe agents in shared rooms: audience-bound memory, durable approvals, the self-API, credential custody, automations, the run queue and the live run stream [SRC]. Grok Bot is the best model for the personal agent as a product: the primary bot and access list, the agent's computer and browser with takeover, turn-taking, the send journal, the command registry and cost by bot [SRC]. Slack supplies the product definitions for canvas, huddles, retention, notification engineering and search-as-the-asker, mostly as documentation [DOC], and Teams supplies the enterprise envelope: the activity feed, admin policy and rings, meeting intelligence with consent, deep-link routing and the thin native shell, mostly as reverse-engineered strings [REA]. Verevon's own strengths, Control-signed decisions, ZDR enforcement in Session Core and agents as service subjects, are not matched by any reference and should remain the frame into which these patterns are fitted [SRC, INF].

## 4. Priority recommendations

Priority items are the capabilities a Space needs before it works as a room for people and agents: each is either shared by most of the five references (table stakes) or essential to the agent half of the vision, and each is Absent, Partial or Stub in Verevon code. Items appear in recommended build order within the tier; the rank follows dependencies as much as value, so the human-first room and the realtime substrate come before the features that ride on them. Every card shows the verified status: the "Verevon today" row and gap text come from a code check against the CoresSystem repo at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10), and reference facts carry the corrections that check produced. Where a reference claim rests on public documentation or inference, the sentence says so. Effort is S, M, L or XL for the whole item; cards name smaller slices where verification found them.

<a id="F-t1-001"></a>
### F-t1-001 · Human-first room conversation: human-only posts, member replies in shared threads, and a thread side panel in one contextual right pane
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (Session Core append) with a Control Plane thread:append decision; Frontend Plane (gateway routes, AuxPane, thread panel) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** A Control-authorized append that stores a human post or thread reply in Session Core without starting a model turn. Any member holding a thread:append decision can reply in any room thread, and a per-thread agent-engagement record lets an agent that has spoken in a thread hear follow-ups. Threads render in a generalised right-hand AuxPane (standard header, resize, focus hand-off) bound to the `?thread_id=` links that Activity already emits.

**Why it matters.** Every room post is a model invocation today and non-owners cannot reply although the UI offers Reply. Until people can talk to each other in shared threads, a Space is a shared AI prompt box, not a Teams or Slack room. Mentions ([F-t1-007](#F-t1-007)), notifications ([F-t1-008](#F-t1-008)), search, reactions and edits attach to these entries, and the AuxPane later hosts runs and agent sessions ([F-t1-004](#F-t1-004)).

**What the references do.** Human-first rooms are native in 3 of 5 references (Buzz, Slack, Teams), QM reaches people through Slack, and threads appear in 5 of 5 [SRC][DOC][REA]. Buzz has the best posting model: a message is an event tagged with the channel id, posting is a plain append, and agents receive it only through mention or subscription rules; a reply carries root and parent ids, ingest rejects an unknown parent and upserts thread_metadata (reply_count, descendant_count, last_reply_at) in the same transaction, so clients never count replies [SRC]. QM's engagement rule is unique: any scope member can continue a thread, and a bot-stake check (the agent spoke or was mentioned, cached negative result) decides whether a human reply becomes an ambient agent turn; it lives in QM's Slack surface [SRC]. A thread side panel exists in Slack (DOC only, with pop-out and Threads view), Buzz and Grok Bot; Teams replies inline in an inbox tree-grid [DOC][SRC][REA]. Grok Bot shows the pane mechanics (about 300 ms close linger, inert while closing, focus to the first enabled control, pointer-only role=separator); keyboard resize, persisted width and a docked/overlay breakpoint are Verevon design [REA][INF]. Grok Bot threads are bot task threads, not human threads [REA].

**Verevon today.** SpaceRoomComposer.submitMessage always calls streamChat (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:375-425) and the gateway has no POST route for threads or messages (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:640-745). A non-owner reply is refused twice in Session Core: AppendMessage runs authorize_thread_owner(Mutate) (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:4468-4516, src/auth.rs:470-562) and verify_append_space_decision compares the decision subject with the thread owner (grpc.rs:2432-2530), although Control already mints a member-capable thread:append decision (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/personal_thread_decision.go:143-164) and messages store author_subject_id (session-core migrations/0035_message_author_attribution.sql). The `?thread_id=` links (activity-grammar.ts:190-206) are never consumed and the third column is a read-only rail (SpacePage.tsx:1253-1330). Reuse inject_personal_thread_context (spaces.rs:40-200), append_message_in_tx, spaceReadMarkers and the ChatWorkspaceCanvas resize handle (ChatWorkspaceCanvas.tsx:174-186).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Reuse thread:append rather than a new decision type. In Session Core, for Space-bound threads only, replace the owner check with "caller is the decision subject and the decision verifies", compare the subject with the caller in verify_append_space_decision, and stamp the new message instead of rewriting thread-level audience columns (grpc.rs:2499-2515), so earlier history stays with its revision. Add gateway no-invoke routes that mint the decision through the existing helper with an idempotency key. The Frontend slice (AuxPane, deep link, an explicit "you cannot read these turns" state) can ship first at about M; Session Core plus Control is L. Derive reply counters at read time until volume demands a table. Constraints: Session Core owns transcript truth, agent turns run with the invoker's grants, and Session Core already refuses durable writes from ZDR (Zero-Data-Retention) credentials (auth.rs:276-285). Risks: two write paths drifting, and tests that assume every post is a turn.

<a id="F-t1-002"></a>
### F-t1-002 · Addressed agent wake-up: several agents per message, per-binding respond_to/subscribe trigger policy, offer-to-add, then optional ambient participation
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Frontend Plane (gateway and composer); Application Plane (binding schema); Model Plane (post route, ambient judge, room tool); Control Plane (turn_origin) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** A message wakes an agent only when it is addressed (one or several agents, with an address lock in the composer) or matches a binding trigger rule, governed by per-binding respond_to (owner, allowlist, anyone, nobody) and subscribe (mentions, all, rules) fields, with an offer-to-add dialog for an unbound agent. Later, an optional ambient judge with standing orders and an auditable ledger, and a room tool so an agent may stay silent.

**Why it matters.** A Space should feel like Slack or Teams for people first, with agents joining on demand. Today every post goes through the chat stream, so people cannot simply talk and an agent cannot be silent. This turns a chat with a bot into a room; agent cards, multi-agent work and personal agents build on it.

**What the references do.** An addressing or trigger policy exists in 3 of 5 references (Buzz, Grok Bot mention-to-wake, Slack app mentions) [SRC][REA][DOC]. Buzz is the best model: respond_to OwnerOnly (default), Allowlist, Anyone or Nobody fails closed; subscribe is Mentions, All or Config; rules are first-match-wins with a hard timeout and a 4096-character cap. The composer keeps a persistent addressed-agent set and records implicit mentions so edits can strip them; mentioning an owned agent the room lacks opens an Invite-and-send versus send-as-reference dialog; wake publishes first, then starts the agent with a replay floor. With the default subscribe=mentions Buzz drops unaddressed events [SRC]. Only QM feeds unaddressed messages to an agent: a cheap judge returns {act, reason, asked_by}, every judgment is stored for admins, and a per-channel policy (orders, bot mode ignore/rollup/action/user, ambientEnabled) is updated with an optimistic baseUpdatedAt check that returns 409 [SRC]. QM's surface tool (post, reach, react, edit, read_thread) rejects destination arguments on post, and a turn without a post is silent [SRC]. Grok Bot protos carry a room-member turn outcome with PASS and SKIPPED, a possible third source for allowed silence, mechanism not traced [REA]. Teams has a project-agent "offer help proactively" toggle in strings only, and its agent keywords guide focus rather than trigger [REA]. Buzz docs flag mention-to-invite as needing a security review [DOC].

**Verevon today.** Mention invocation with per-binding policy is enforced at the gateway (apply_mention_binding_policy, apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4399-4573), but every send streams a turn (SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:375-470), one mentionedAgentRef carries one agent (:149), an unbound agent is rejected (spaces.rs:4449) and there is no human-only post route (spaces.rs:649-735; model-gateway has no POST on /v1/threads/:id/messages, apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:177). Binding policy has no human-facing writer beyond active/paused (spaces.rs:3632-3715), the stored "group" trigger mode is never acted on, and respond_to, subscribe, rules, judge, turn_origin and a silence tool do not exist. Reuse apply_mention_binding_policy, agents/bind and agents/available (spaces.rs:3913-4040), session-core AppendMessage (grpc.rs:2386-2470) and spaceAgentBindings (convex schema.ts:606-680).

**How to build it in Verevon.** The gateway reaches Session Core through model-gateway, so add a post-only route there that calls AppendMessage under the existing Control decision and idempotency key ([F-t1-001](#F-t1-001)). Add the binding-policy writer (PATCH route plus a role-gated Convex mutation) before new fields, and ship every field with gateway enforcement in the same change, because the schema comment warns that a stored-but-unenforced field is a false promise. Several agents per message means gateway fan-out into one run per agent, looping apply_mention_binding_policy. Offer-to-add goes through the owner/manager bind route; a mention invokes but never grants. Phase 1 (post plus addressed wake-up for one agent) is M; fan-out, editor, offer-to-add and the default-off judge make it L. The judge must follow ZDR propagation and Control-owned policy floors. Risks: users who miss the always-on assistant (make the default agent an explicit binding) and agents silent when addressed (an addressed human always gets a reply).

<a id="F-t1-003"></a>
### F-t1-003 · Space realtime substrate: typed seq-numbered event projection, resumable per-Space SSE through the gateway, and authorization at subscribe and at every delivery
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | XL | Model Plane (Session Core outbox events); Application Plane (projection); Frontend Plane (gateway SSE, SPA client); Control Plane (stream-read audience) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** One typed, metadata-only Space event envelope with a per-Space monotonic sequence, emitted from Session Core's outbox and projected for fan-out. The gateway terminates one resumable SSE (Server-Sent Events) stream per tab with Last-Event-ID, a cursor_too_old snapshot frame, heartbeats under 15 s and a watchdog, keeping the 6 s poll as fallback. Every frame passes one fail-closed chokepoint holding a live Control read decision for (user, Space, audience revision) that closes the stream on revocation or audience change.

**Why it matters.** All five references push room events; Verevon polls the full thread list every 6 s, so members see posts and agent replies late, and shared run streams ([F-t1-004](#F-t1-004)), unread counts ([F-t1-009](#F-t1-009)), presence and calls have nothing to ride on. Checking authority at subscribe and at every delivery keeps push from becoming a side door around Control decisions and ZDR.

**What the references do.** A push channel exists in 5 of 5 references [SRC][REA][DOC]. Grok Bot's WatchGrokBotTranscripts is the best wire shape: one multiplexed stream with per-room {generation, after_updated_seq} cursors and frames connected, rows, cleared, cursor_too_old, heartbeat, agent_state and roster_changed; it is flag-gated (sand_transcript_server_tail, default off) mid-migration, and bounded replay is inferred from the cursor_too_old frame [REA][INF]. Buzz supplies the authorization pattern, unique among the references: one filter_fanout_by_access chokepoint shared by local and cross-node paths, subscribe checks access then registers, revocation separates a cache-invalidate hint from a connection-kill command, and per-tenant Redis topics use refcounted subscribe with a 500 ms unsubscribe debounce; its 64x figure is a bus-only microbenchmark [SRC]. Buzz hardens WebSockets (auth timeout, ping with missed-pong limit, bounded send queue) and triggers resume from online, focus and visibility events at least 5 s apart [SRC]. QM provides the watchdog: 4 s open timeout, 30 s silence timeout, resync on reconnect [SRC]. Slack's architecture is DOC only, Teams' Trouter push and gap detection are strings and RPC names, and no reference polls room messages while healthy [DOC][REA].

**Verevon today.** SpacePage polls every 6 s while visible (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpacePage.tsx:78-87, 273-338), the gateway has no Space stream route (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:649-738), Convex has no per-Space event table (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:546-604) and Session Core's outbox publishes only audit and mp.v1.run.<id>.event subjects (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/audit_publisher.rs:40-100). Reuse Last-Event-ID resume for the sender's run stream (gateway chat/streams.rs:134-200), Convex leased outbox rows (schema.ts:680-717), Control's read decision carrying the audience revision (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/shared_thread_read_decision.go:1-70), the gateway JetStream consumer precedent (gdpr_nats.rs:1-30) and the run-lifecycle mirror that drops space and thread ids (convex nats.ts:975-1030).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Do not make the Application Plane the first emitter: posts and runs happen in Session Core, so add a thread and message subject family to its outbox (extend classify_outbox_subject and publisher ACLs) and consume it in the gateway or an Application projection; evaluate Session Core's events journal as the sequence source before adding a Convex seq table. Slice it: run-state frames first over mp.v1.run.*.event, with space_id and thread_id added to the envelope, then post frames. Add a stream-read audience to Control's closed set (shared_thread_read_decision.go:44-50) because decisions expire after 2 minutes (personal_thread_decision.go:21), and re-authorize on every heartbeat. Frames and replay buffers carry ids and kinds only so ZDR holds; the BFF already holds a Dragonfly copy of chat content that erasure had to reach, so add no bodies. Shared-bus NATS is best-effort (space_membership_nats.rs:1-60), so the projection must rebuild from a snapshot. Depends on [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) and client store F-t1-033. Risks: decision-minting load, reconnect storms, revocation latency up to the decision TTL.

<a id="F-t1-004"></a>
### F-t1-004 · Agent work visible to the whole room: shared live run stream, activity/plan/tool cards, an ambient activity strip, typed transcript rows and steer/cancel
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Model Plane (run read decision branch, steer framing); Frontend Plane (gateway attach route, cards, strip, rows) | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** Let any member with a thread read decision attach to a running agent turn (offset-keyed deltas, tool steps, a snapshot for late joiners) and render the work as semantic cards (one per tool call, plan states, completed work collapsed) and typed transcript rows with grouping. Add an activity strip near the composer with latch and minimum-display rules and click-through to the run in the AuxPane, and give the working row Stop, Steer and Skip.

**Why it matters.** People cannot cooperate with an agent whose work they cannot see. Verevon renders rich runs in AgentRunConsole and the chat live-run panel, but in a Space the stream reaches only the sender while others see a status line. The strip and typed rows make a mixed room readable once push ([F-t1-003](#F-t1-003)) exposes every step.

**What the references do.** Activity or plan cards ship in 3 of 5 references (Buzz, QM, Grok Bot todos); Slack's chat.startStream, appendStream and stopStream contract is DOC only, and Teams shows only bot-streaming and stop-button flags [SRC][REA][DOC]. Buzz is the best renderer: a classifier maps each tool call to one of 15 render classes over a raw-event floor, one card per call mutated in place with verb-object-outcome summaries, and completed work collapses into one disclosure [SRC]. QM is the best wire: an SSE per run with RUN_STARTED, TOOL_CALL_START and RESULT replay, deltas keyed by character offset, a snapshot fallback on gap and RUN_FINISHED, authorized by the actor's run visibility [SRC]. Grok Bot pushes AgentLiveState with a stale_after rule, batches computer_actions frames on its transcript-watch stream and uses a committed-ids latch in group rooms with several bots; its row kinds are telemetry names with no approval row [REA][INF]. Buzz's steer policy is Queue, Steer, Interrupt or owner-interrupt with explicit prompt framing, and its activity bar rotates headlines every 2.2 s with a "Working" fallback [SRC]. The typed Row union and action frames on one stream are Verevon synthesis [INF].

**Verevon today.** stream_run_events authorizes with authorize_run_owner(Read) (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/orchestration_grpc.rs:3239-3280) and the gateway proxies it with the caller's token (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/streams.rs:169-215), so only the sender streams the turn. Others see a poll-driven status row (SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:340-372), the approval card and a read-only Work tab (SpaceWorkPanel.tsx:1-192); the sender's Stop via cancelInvocation (SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:480-515) ships. Reuse the AgentRunConsole event mapping (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/agents/components/AgentRunConsole.tsx:540-575), model-gateway run_events_sse with event ids (model-gateway sse.rs:7533-7613), the replay buffer with after_event_id (orchestration_grpc.rs:3253-3268), the chat cancel and queue routes (gateway chat.rs:15-83), activity-grammar.ts, space-live-work.ts and the browser payload with liveFrameStreamUrl (gateway browser.rs:2552-2580).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Slice it: (1) member-readable attach with a Control run-read decision verified in Session Core (the thread-read pattern at spaces.rs:2616-2700, never a gateway bypass) and a Space-read branch in stream_run_events emitting an audience-filtered step set, L; (2) step rows in SpaceRoomTimeline from the AgentRunConsole mapping; (3) strip, latch rules and typed rows once push exists, M; (4) steer, skip and an interrupt policy, M, where only owner-interrupt by room managers is new. Drop the Application Plane run-state projection: latest_run_status comes from Session Core (sessions.proto:1006), so push Session Core state through the gateway. Constraints: Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL, so step text and tool output stay owner-private unless labelled room-public (couples to F-t1-012); stream without persisting (ZDR); the room renders Quarry receipts and live frames and never executes browser actions, and human_takeover stays with the run owner. Defer the typed card envelope until a card catalogue exists. Risks: tool output leaking, token fan-out cost (coalesce), cancel racing an in-flight effect.

<a id="F-t1-006"></a>
### F-t1-006 · Space governance admin: effect-policy admin UI with safe presets and typed denials first, then posting rules, agent/model allowlist, locked switches, autonomy posture and rollout flags
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Control Plane (user-core effect policy, reason codes, flags) with Frontend Plane (admin UI) and Model Plane (posture caps) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Phase 1: an org-admin API and UI over the per-org space_effect_policies entitlement booleans, with presets of entitlements, an effective-policy view, and typed denial reasons mapped from Control reason codes in gateway and UI. Phase 2: per-Space posting rules, an agent and model allowlist, org-locked switches with a reason, and a strict/standard/open autonomy posture with tighten-only composition. Phase 3: a registry of all Spaces and Control-owned rollout flags that are never an authorization input.

**Why it matters.** Every shared Space effect is deny-by-default and no human admin can write the policy rows (none exist in dev), so most shipped shared features cannot be switched on or verified live. Admin policy is how Teams and QM make AI rooms governable; without it every new capability adds a hidden switch.

**What the references do.** An admin console exists in 3 of 5 references and feature flags or remote config in 4 of 5 (Buzz, Grok Bot, Slack, Teams) [SRC][REA][DOC]. Teams is the best policy model, from public docs only: every feature area has policies, precedence is direct assignment, then highest-ranked group, then global default, and propagation can take hours [DOC]. QM runs a separate admin service where every mutation and content read is audited, enables a model only after a probe stored as a fingerprint of definition and credential, and ranks posture tiers so the effective posture is the strictest on the scope chain [SRC][DOC]. Grok Bot returns an access state plus one of about ten typed block reasons; one UI component per reason is unverified, and bulk team operations run as jobs with per-item states [REA]. Posting rules appear in at least 2 of 5 (Slack DOC, Teams moderator-only strings) and app or agent allowlisting in at least 3 of 5 (Teams, QM models, Slack admin app approval) [DOC][REA][SRC]. Buzz rate-limits with a Redis fixed window and human and agent tiers; QM per principal; token buckets keyed by org, principal and action are a Verevon proposal [SRC][INF]. Slack's locked settings are desktop-shell IT policy, a weak analogue [SRC].

**Verevon today.** Control stores one deny-by-default row per org (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/authority.go:130-185, migrations 018-028), written only by a service-principal PUT (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/http/server.go:345-360, spaces.go:85-115, service_auth.go:114-140) through an operator script (apps/Control Plane/scripts/set-space-effect-policy.sh:1-40). The gateway and SPA have no admin route or UI (WorkspaceSettingsPage.tsx:30-60) and denials are plain 403 or 503 strings (spaces.go:1455-1465). Per-binding agent policy is enforced in the gateway (spaces.rs:4483-4503), a per-run AutonomyRung is enforced per call (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:95-145) but granted only by a human-approved plan (model-gateway sse.rs:8995-9040), and the limiter keys by org, user or IP (gateway rate_limit.rs:1-40). Reuse the EffectPolicy contract, the gateway orgs domain with require_org_admin, capability-core rollout_state as the kill-switch precedent, and audit-core events.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Phase 1 exposes only the entitlement booleans through a new org-admin-delegated Control route that validates against plan entitlements and emits audit events; the privacy attestations (purpose, lawful basis, retention class, zero_data_retention) stay read-only on the service-only PUT, because the script says they must never be defaulted and a missing row must stay a denial. Add reason codes to Control's denials before building typed denial UI, since the thread-read decline is swallowed today (spaces.rs:1319). Do not present a model allowlist until allowed_models has an owning service. Posting rules, allowlists, posture and flags become separate later items. Phase 1 alone is L. Constraints: entitlements are floors that never authorize an owner-plane resource; Space ZDR and the org interactive ZDR toggle are different flags the UI must show together; flags are rollout controls only. Dependencies: F-t1-022 (room lifecycle), F-t1-011 (agent access list). Risks: approval fatigue under strict posture and a limiter that fails open by design.

<a id="F-t1-007"></a>
### F-t1-007 · Identity-bound @mentions for people and agents with mention records, role-gated group handles, invite-from-mention and ranked @/# pickers
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Frontend Plane (composer, gateway validation) with Model Plane entry metadata and an Application Plane notification-core consumer | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** Send mentions as structured subject refs (mentioned_person_refs beside the existing mentioned_agent_ref), validate each against the Control roster in the gateway, persist mention records on the entry and emit a content-free mention signal. Replace text-only @person insertion with one typeahead for people and agents, later role-gated @room/@here and groups; mentioning an unbound agent offers an invite. Rank the picker by recent interaction plus prefix, scroll past six results, and add # references to threads, runs and documents.

**Why it matters.** A mention record is the prerequisite for notifications ([F-t1-008](#F-t1-008)), inbox slices ([F-t1-009](#F-t1-009)), quotas and audit. Without it people cannot summon each other and @person is visibly broken: it inserts text that does nothing. The agent half already follows the right rule, so extending it to people keeps one identity model inside ACLs.

**What the references do.** Identity-bound mentions appear in 4 of 5 references and scoped pickers in 3 of 5 [SRC][REA][DOC]. Buzz is the best identity model: the composer keeps a label-to-subject map and sends mentions[] of subject ids separate from the body; namesakes get qualified labels, pasted mentions are re-verified and send awaits bindings; the relay indexes mention rows in the event transaction, but roster validation happens client-side (no server-side roster check was found except for workflow-authored messages) [SRC][DOC]. Buzz ranks candidates by recent interaction plus prefix and offers Invite versus send-without-inviting for a non-member owned agent [SRC]. Grok Bot computes candidates with a pure scoping function (group: its members; otherwise other bots and groups containing this bot, unadded team bots hidden) and has reference providers for PRs, skills and MCP items; its groups are bot group chats [REA]. Role-gated broadcast and group handles come from Slack (@everyone restricted by role, SCIM user groups, tier-gated, DOC only) and Teams (team and channel mentions, tags, blocked over 10,000 members; strings plus docs) [DOC][REA]. Buzz has no @channel or @here; "handles checked against a role policy" and "notifications fan out from mention records only" are Verevon design [INF].

**Verevon today.** Agent mentions are identity-bound: the browser sends only mentioned_agent_ref and the gateway re-resolves it against the roster and binding (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4175-4216, 4399-4498). A person mention is text insertion (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:269-312), the picker is prefix-only, capped at six, agents first (:184-211), and an unbound agent is rejected. No producer for a mention notification exists: the gateway has no notification-create route (gateway notifications.rs:20-30) and notification-core accepts the gateway principal only for notification.created to the delegated user (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/http/handlers.go:150-170, 199-218). Reuse resolve_mentioned_space_agent, the AppendMessage metadata Struct (sessions.proto:287-298), the conversation_followed_message.go consumer pattern, the seeded mention.received preference (notification-core migration 005:60-63) and the agents/bind and agents/available routes.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Keep gateway validation of mentioned_person_refs against the Control roster. Persist mention records in entry metadata at append time ([F-t1-001](#F-t1-001)), emit a Space or session event from the owner plane, and add a notification-core consumer in the style of conversation_followed_message.go that creates one content-free in-app notification per mentioned member after a read-entitlement check; do not widen the gateway allowlist. Make invite-from-mention role-aware: owner or manager binds and sends, others get a request, and a mention never grants authority. Do not label a picker section "your agents" until ownership exists beyond createdBy (convex schema.ts:409). Effort stays M; @room/@here and user groups in the same slice make it L. Constraints: ZDR-safe, content-free signals and recipients that pass the room read entitlement. Dependencies: F-t1-032 (draft restore), F-t1-018 (message search behind # thread references).

**Quick win.** The picker and gateway halves extend code that already works for agents, and the composer already carries user:<subject_id> ids, so people mentions with records are a contained M that unblocks notifications and the inbox.

<a id="F-t1-008"></a>
### F-t1-008 · Space notifications: typed signals for mentions, replies, approvals and agent completions, refs-only payload, rate limits, per-room levels and thread follow/mute
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Application Plane (notification-core consumers, per-member state) with producers in Model Plane (Session Core events, capability-core) and Control Plane | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Typed signals (mention, reply, approval_needed, agent_done, agent_awaiting, invite) published into notification-core through a per-service, per-type producer allowlist, with one refs-only payload (signal, space, thread, message or run ref, actor, urgency, actions, trace id, deep link) and a rate limit. Then per-room levels (All, Mentions, Off), a hide-preview flag, and per-user thread follow and mute with auto-follow reasons, applied in a fixed order: mention passes, then mute, then thread rules.

**Why it matters.** No Space event produces a notification today: the producer allowlist admits only run_completed and run_failed, and mention.received is seeded but never produced. People will not notice a mention or an agent waiting on them, which undercuts both the Teams/Slack half and the agent half of the vision.

**What the references do.** Typed signals exist in 4 of 5 references and levels with follow or mute in 3 of 5 [SRC][REA][DOC]. Slack is the best payload model: the desktop shell validates one schema-checked payload (notification id, channel, team, message, user, thread timestamp, trace id), runs client-side display and DND gates, emits received, click and reply telemetry and carries a no-text-in-notifications flag; server-side triggering is not observed [SRC]. Grok Bot's agent-turn notification carries awaiting_user_response, so "done" and "needs you" are distinct [REA]. Teams keeps a closed signal enum (call, mention, chat, channel, other) and auto-follows when mentioned, authored or participated, for threads and posts [REA]. Buzz's shouldNotify runs mention first, then channel mute, then thread rules; after that any top-level message in a non-muted channel notifies, so Buzz is not quiet by default, and its declared agent job slots have no emitter, a lesson to declare only signals with a producer [SRC]. No reference shows an implemented notification rate limiter: Grok Bot ships a disabled config (minIntervalMs 5000, 10 per 300 s, force-persisted isEnabled:false) and its active-device-only suppression is a gate with default off [REA]. Slack's levels and cross-device routing and Buzz's low-noise defaults are DOC only [DOC].
