**Verevon today.** capability-core may send modelplane.run_completed and run_failed to users with a per-run watcher (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/http/handlers.go:130-215, apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/runwatch/consumer.go:1-120), and the watcher button is wired in Chat only (ChatMessages.tsx:3685). mention.received is a seeded row without a producer (notification-core migrations/005_create_channel_configs.up.sql:50-62), @person mentions are composer text (SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:170-215, 295-320) and the gateway may notify only the delegated user (handlers.go:160-168). No deep-link contract, signal taxonomy, per-room level, hide-preview flag or thread follow exists. Reuse the notification-core Accept path and preference rows, the capability-core notifyclient and runwatch JetStream consumer, conversation_followed_message.go as the refs-only consumer template, conversation-core follow routes as the follow pattern, and spaceReadMarkers (convex spaceReadMarkers.ts:1-60) for per-room levels and subscriptions.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Space posts and runs live in Session Core, not conversation-core (apps/Application Plane/conversation-core/conversation-core-go/internal/http/space_activity.go:35-54), so mention and reply records originate with the message owner ([F-t1-007](#F-t1-007)) or an Application projection fed from it, publishing metadata-only events that a notification-core JetStream consumer turns into notifications. Extend capability-core's allowlist for agent_done, agent_awaiting and approval_needed, and auto-register run watchers for runs started in a room. Decide the ZDR delivery rule first: notification-core under ZDR persists no payload, refuses async queueing and never projects to the in-app feed (notification-core service.go:285-345, feed_projection.go:120-145), so a refs-only payload alone yields no in-app signal for ZDR orgs, and Space ZDR and org interactive ZDR are different flags. Slice one (taxonomy, allowlist, producers, deep link) is L; per-room levels and follow/mute add M. Constraints: recipients re-authorized at render; push or chat-app delivery waits on the docs-only Channel Plane. Risks: storms from chatty agents and preference sprawl without documented precedence.

<a id="F-t1-009"></a>
### F-t1-009 · Cross-room attention: a server-side unread/working aggregate stream, then a Home "needs you" inbox with Agents and Activity destinations
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Application Plane (attention projection, aggregate); Model Plane (per-Space latest-activity summary); Frontend Plane (sidebar destinations, inbox route) | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** A per-Space latest-activity aggregate joined with the durable per-member read markers into a per-user {unread, mentions, needs_you} map, first polled, then pushed over the realtime stream, with mark-all-read and defined badge semantics. On top of it, a refs-only per-user inbox with slices (All, Mentions, Threads, Needs action, Agents, Reminders), inline reply or jump, dismiss and snooze, a capped agent "waiting on you" lane, and cross-room Agents and Activity views, named so it is not confused with the support Inbox.

**Why it matters.** Sidebar dots come from a browser store that only the open room fills, and Activity is room-scoped, so a person in several Spaces cannot see what needs them. A cross-room inbox is table stakes in Teams, Slack and Buzz, and the agent needs-you queue (Grok Bot, QM) is its agent-era form.

**What the references do.** A cross-room inbox appears in 3 of 5 references and sidebar destinations in 4 of 5 (Buzz, Slack, QM, Teams) [SRC][REA][DOC]. Teams' UI exposes about 60 titled activity types and slices (Mentions, Threads, VIP, Reminders, Drafts, Saved, Requests) with show rules and a mark-all-read dialog, evidenced by localized string keys; its counter consistency check is inferred from an RPC name [REA][INF]. Buzz is the best re-implementable version: the inbox is assembled fan-out-on-read from a mention join plus a needs-action query capped at 100 rows, grouped by conversation root in a list-plus-detail layout with filter chips and inline reply; presence is a Redis key with EX 180 s [SRC]. A claimed 5-minute per-agent grouping could not be found in Buzz. QM is the best aggregate transport: one SSE session-state stream pushes {threadRef, state working/awaiting_approval/idle, participants} with stale-delta rejection and resync, destinations are permission-checked, and a "99+ waiting on you" badge exists [SRC]. Grok Bot unifies approvals, needs-input tasks and hand-offs in one "Waiting for you" queue, partly inferred from names [REA][INF]. Slack's Home, Activity and Later destinations are DOC only, and its shell unread mirroring is IPC, not a server stream [DOC][SRC].

**Verevon today.** A durable per-member read marker exists (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceReadMarkers.ts:1-153) and the open room derives unread from it, but sidebar dots come from a browser-only store that holds only rooms whose page has published and clears when the reader leaves (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/space-live-work.ts:1-146, SpacePage.tsx:173-175). No server-side aggregate, mark-all-read, mention count, needs-you count, inbox or cross-room Activity view exists; the sidebar "Home" group is the dashboard and the support inbox owns /inbox (src/features/core/lib/sidebar-navigation.ts:100-140, src/app/App.tsx:160-248). Reuse unreadThreadIds and advanceMarker, Control's Space index (listSpacesForSubject) as the visibility filter, the "Needs attention" lens (SpaceActivityPanel.tsx:56-90, activity-grammar.ts:240-270), the agent installations registry and chief-core routing (AgentInstallationsPage.tsx:1-50, chief-core-routing.ts:1-60), the notification feed API (notifications-client.ts:20-70) and the spacePresence heartbeat table.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Phase it: first a polled GET /api/v1/spaces/attention in the gateway that intersects Control's Space index with read markers and a new Session Core per-Space latest-activity summary, driving sidebar counts and mark-all-read (about M); then the inbox; then push through [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003). Back the inbox with an Application Plane attention projection of refs only, next to spaceReadMarkers, because notification-core is not a ZDR-safe store (a ZDR request persists no payload and never reaches the feed, notification-core service.go:285-345, feed_projection.go:120-145); use notification-core only for out-of-app delivery. Extend /agents/installations and chief-core with run and needs-you state instead of a second Agents destination. Constraints: Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL, so the aggregate is metadata-only, filtered by the Space index, with no row for unreadable rooms; per-room thread reads need a Control decision per Space (spaces.rs:1268-1330), so the cross-room read cannot loop over those decisions; items are rehydrated at render under the viewer's current decision. Dependencies: [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008) (mention records, badge semantics), [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004) (run state). Risks: double-counting between badges and the needs-action count, stale counts without resync, per-user fan-out cost in large orgs.

<a id="F-t1-010"></a>
### F-t1-010 · Approvals with grant scopes and durable resume, tool permission policy with ceilings, and quarantine-and-release

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (continuation, screening); Control Plane (grants, ceilings, turn_origin); Frontend Plane (cards, policy editor) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Extend the approve/deny card to scoped decisions (once, this conversation, always for Space plus agent plus action hash) with an explicit addressee (requester, named approver or any member), and make the card honest about which approvals will actually resume the paused run. Add a per-tool allow, ask or block policy with an org floor and a Space ceiling (tighten-only merge) and an editor in the Agent tab. Treat flagged untrusted tool output as an approval kind with a preview and single-use release, and add a Control turn_origin on decisions. Auto-review stays a later, proposal-only phase.

**Why it matters.** Approvals are the main safety valve once agents get a computer and a browser. Scopes and ceilings keep an "always" grant from widening authority; a resume that really continues the work turns an approval from a state change into work done.

**What the references do.** QM is the best source for durable approvals: it stores {requestId, action, purpose, requester, scopes}, returns terminate:true so the model stops at the pause, lets only the requester decide (once, session, always, deny), re-runs the turn with the approval on resume, re-runs retry-safe calls and marks dangling calls interrupted; in thread-only conversations the card goes to the requester's DM [SRC]. QM's quarantine screens tool results, raises a quarantine approval with a preview, and a single-use release resumes with the content framed as data, not authority; it is unique (1 of 5 references) and its classifier can fail open [SRC]. Grok Bot orders levels never, ask, always with effective = min(user setting, team ceiling); its auto-review layers custom and team rules, the classifier returns ALLOW or BLOCK, and ASK_FIRST is a rule effect; the model runs server-side and standing grants exist only for messaging subjects [REA]. Approval cards ship in 3 of 5 references (Grok Bot, QM, Teams via consent strings) [REA][SRC]; Slack offers design guidance only [DOC]; Buzz's harness auto-approves, the negative example [SRC]. QM's tighten-only merge is tested, but an admin setter can replace floors [SRC].

**Verevon today.** The card exists with the decision pinned to the run owner (SpaceApprovalPanel.tsx:1-60). Session Core has durable approvals with an encrypted continuation descriptor and a leaseable outbox (session-core/src/approval_delivery.rs:1-80, continuation_crypto.rs:1-60), and execution-core's approval_delivery_worker.rs:1-120 resumes three idempotent action kinds while shell, browser_agent and MCP (Model Context Protocol) tools fail closed [SRC]. The model-gateway decide path still returns a quarantine placeholder (model-gateway/src/approvals.rs:235-300) and the card claims every approval resumes [SRC]. Missing: grant scopes with revocation, approver routing, a per-tool policy with floor, ceiling and editor (the binding has only approvalMode and allowedTools, spaceAgents.ts:325-395, shown read-only in SpacePage.tsx:945-953), a visible quarantine card (semantic screening is shadow-only, semantic_screening.rs:1-40) and turn_origin [SRC]. Reuse owner_grant_decision.go:1-60 and owner_effect_reservation.go:88-160 (a revocable action-bound grant for tickets.create) and the capability-core policy engine (policy/engine.go:1-180).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Do not rebuild the dispatcher. Widen resumable kinds one at a time with idempotency and postcondition proofs, and surface continuation_delivery on the card. Put grant scopes in Control by generalising the tickets.create owner-grant shape: Control signs, Model only tightens, and an "always" grant skips the prompt but never the owner-plane authorization at effect time. Build the policy from the binding fields and capability-core scope grants, which support only org and global today (engine.go:46-58), so a Space ceiling needs that extension or a Control-side merge. Under ZDR (Zero-Data-Retention) the descriptor is refused (runtime_loop/agent.rs:4440-4600), so ZDR approvals cannot resume by design; browser resume stays with Quarry as executor. Depends on the card surface in [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004). Risks: "always" grants widening authority, model-judged authorization being fooled (proposal only), policy sprawl (keep three levels).

<a id="F-t1-011"></a>
### F-t1-011 · Personal agents with an owner-controlled access list and add policy, a private assistant beside the room, and configurable room bindings

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | XL | Control Plane (owner-derived admission, access list, add policy); Application Plane (binding schema); Model Plane (session keys, model validation); Frontend Plane (private panel) | W3 | High |

**What to build.** Let each person own a personal agent (visibility owner, org or room; a primary-agent setting; per-user sessions) that can be invited into a room with the owner's consent and is revoked with its owner, plus a private assistant panel inside a Space with a reviewed share-to-room step. Give every agent definition an access list (owner, manager, collaborator) and an add policy (owner_only, nobody, anyone) edited with an expected-state token. Make shared room agents configurable per binding: trigger mode, tools, approval, later model and language.

**Why it matters.** The brief asks for Grok-Bot-style personal agents, and a personal Space cannot hold an agent today. Without an owner-controlled access list, a personal agent in a shared room is either unusable or widens its owner's reach, which the Space authority rule forbids.

**What the references do.** Grok Bot is the best source: an agent record carries visibility OWNER or TEAM, a user-level main-agent setting exists, wire roles are USER and MANAGER (Owner and Collaborator are UI labels), principals are USER or GROUP with separate team and link access, edits carry an expected_state_token with a retry message on conflict, and CloneAgentToTeam returns CLONED, UNSUPPORTED or NO_TEAM while excluding personal secret names; team bots share setup but each teammate connects their own accounts [SRC]. Server behaviour was not observed and the primary bot's proactive check-in is UI copy only [SRC]. Personal agents and shared room agents each ship in 3 of 5 references, a private assistant panel in 2 of 5, personal-to-team conversion in 1 of 5, and an owner add policy in 2 of 5 (Grok Bot, Buzz) [SRC][REA]. Buzz enforces owner_only and nobody with a self-add bypass (channel_authz.rs:47-55) and derives SessionScope once at admission; QM resolves scope once per turn [SRC]. Teams' escalate-to-group is meeting-scoped and adds the agent to the meeting chat; its Channel Agent is public preview and Team Agent under construction [REA][DOC]. Slack's personal agent is vendor documentation, plan-gated [DOC].

**Verevon today.** Room agents work end to end (create-in-room, bind, pause, resume, revoke; agents held in Control as service subjects, authority.go:251-300), and a binding stores triggerModes, allowedTools and approvalMode that the gateway enforces (schema.ts:606-678, spaces.rs:4483-4503), but nothing edits them, and model, language, proactivity and thread policy are deliberately not stored [SRC]. The agents table has only an optional createdBy (schema.ts:324-423), listInstallableSpaceAgentsForGateway returns every org definition to any room owner or manager (spaceAgents.ts:480-530), and Control accepts Application's declared service roster with no per-agent check (spaceAgents.ts:700-800) [SRC]. A personal Space is single-subject by construction (repository.go:600-733) [SRC]. Reuse SpaceBindAgentDialog, SpaceCreateAgentDialog and the me/persona route (persona.rs:1-40) as the model for a per-user setting.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Slice it. First (S to M), expose the binding policy through a PATCH route and a settings dialog; add model and language only once an org-approved model list has a Control owner, since allowed_models is refused today (model-gateway/src/grpc.rs:140-165) and only a process-wide env list exists (normalize.rs:80-100), shared with [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006). Second (M to L), make the access list and add policy Control authority enforced at the roster-replace boundary or a new bind decision, and filter the installable list by the caller's add right. Third (XL), personal agents as owner-derived subjects, never a roster widening, plus the private panel; this depends on [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012), [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002) and, for a full personal agent, [F-t1-028](#F-t1-028). Constraints: Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL, so a personal agent in a room may use only what the audience may see and owner connected-account tools still need owner-plane grants; group and link principals resolve in Control. Risks: owner-private context leaking into the room; configuration sprawl (default to mention-only, no tools, require_confirmation).

<a id="F-t1-012"></a>
### F-t1-012 · Audience-safe agent context and memory: source labels, audience-bound memory, owner-signed declassify, review-then-share and explicit room/team knowledge scopes

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (session-core memory, letta-bridge, compaction, labels); Data Plane v2 (retrieval entitlement); Control Plane (declassify decision) | W3 | High |

**What to build.** Stamp a source-scope label on every tool result, retrieval chunk and memory record; tag every memory write with the Space audience (hash, revision) from the signed decision; fold context against that audience; make compaction summaries inherit labels and cut context when the audience changes; evaluate retrieval entitlement for the whole audience. Add an owner-signed declassify action, an explicit opt-in before personal context is used in a shared room, a private draft with Share-to-room, and a per-room knowledge list.

**Why it matters.** Thread storage is audience-bound, but persistent agent memory is not, so what an agent learned in a private room can surface in a wider one. With personal agents entering shared rooms ([F-t1-011](#F-t1-011)) this is a data-governance risk.

**What the references do.** QM is the only reference with shipped, readable audience filtering: every transcript entry carries a scopeLabel and filterHistoryForAudience keeps an entry only if every audience member is entitled (context-filter.ts:1-29); a context boundary fingerprints scope plus sorted audience ids and moves throughSeq forward when the audience changes (context-boundary.ts:35-58); compaction summaries carry the session scope as label plus a securityTainted flag for untrusted input, not audience (compaction.ts:100-170); memory records are classified ordinary, unknown, sensitive or restricted, strongest label wins, with revision compare-and-swap and restore [SRC]. Sharing defaults to Isolated; Open needs owner and room opt-in and a human or live ambient origin [SRC]. Audience-safe disclosure is a differentiator: 1 shipped (QM) plus 2 partial. Teams shows review-then-share strings ("Allow to send to the group", blocked-share text) and knowledge-source removal requiring chat membership; its other knowledge rules are documentation, and it states AI output does not inherit sensitivity labels [REA][DOC]. Slack's model is that agent reach equals requester reach [DOC]. Buzz's audience-labelled memory with declassify is a draft design and its ifc-core crate is unused; Grok Bot's outbound-send labelling is a flag-gated remote-config schema that samples sends rather than blocking them [DOC][SRC]. Per-room knowledge scope is unique to Teams.

**Verevon today.** Decisions bind audience ref, hash and revision, messages carry audience columns (session-core/src/grpc.rs:2565-2580), reads drop rows newer than the decision revision (grpc.rs:5425-5475) and revoked members are denied (migration 0032) [SRC]. But append_message_in_tx extracts memory candidates from every non-conversation-only message, including Space posts, persists them as user-level agent_memory owned by the thread owner (dreaming.rs:739-790, 960-1030) and mirrors them to Letta, while search_memory authorizes only against the thread owner (memory_grpc.rs:350-410); agent_memory has no space, audience or label column (migration 0004:210-235) [SRC]. No declassify, opt-in, private draft or audience-intersected retrieval exists (shared_retrieval_decision.go:1-30 evaluates the acting member; retrieval-engine-rs/src/api/mod.rs:98-279 has no audience concept), and knowledge_scope is deliberately not stored (schema.ts:622-632) [SRC]. Reuse the audience-revision read predicate, MemoryRetention and user_memory_controls (memory_control.rs:25-34), and the Letta bridge's org, user and thread keys.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Start with a containment slice (M): for Space-bound threads stop extracting memory candidates into user-level agent_memory and stop Letta indexing (at minimum for non-personal Spaces), and exclude user and org-scope recall from shared-room turns until a Control opt-in exists; then tag agent_memory rows with space_ref, audience hash and revision and filter at retrieval, with the same filter in letta-bridge or it becomes the bypass. Settle the S3.1 ownership ADR first (Data v2 owns authored memory documents; Model owns learned memory and the resolver), because the scope enum lives in capability-core (workplane_apis.go:66-74). Audience intersection belongs in the Control decision, not in Data v2 inferring membership. Labels, compaction taint, declassify, private drafts and knowledge lists form the later L to XL phase. Never auto-copy room facts into personal memory; ZDR callers already get no durable memory. Depends on [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011) and [F-t1-031](#F-t1-031). Risks: partitioned memory is less useful, so declassify must be cheap; over-filtering needs a visible withheld state.

<a id="F-t1-013"></a>
### F-t1-013 · Room attachments and file library: paste, drop and attach through presigned Data Plane documents with Space provenance, scan and typed refusals, plus a Files view

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Data Plane v2 (documents) with Frontend Plane (gateway routes, composer, Files view) and Control Plane (Space write decision) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Let members paste, drop or attach images and files in a room. Phase 1 reuses the main chat's per-turn attachment flow in the room composer. Phase 2 adds durable room files: the gateway obtains a Control-signed Space decision, the upload carries space_ref, creator (human or agent), mime, size and hash, and the Knowledge tab gains a Files view. Phase 3, only if originals must be retained, adds a Data-owned object store with scan and thumbnails. Refusals are typed (empty, too_large, count, type) throughout.

**Why it matters.** Attachments ship in 5 of 5 references, and a work room without files cannot carry real work. Agents also need the files as input ([F-t1-046](#F-t1-046)).

**What the references do.** The three-step pipeline is a synthesis, not one reference's flow. Slack documents it: get an upload URL, POST the bytes, complete; files are scanned for malware before they become available [DOC]. QM registers a FileArtifact with ownerScopeId, createdBy, optional createdInScope and nullable sha256 (file-artifact-store.ts:15-31), with no expiry or byte reclamation [SRC]. Buzz uploads through the relay, rejects media carrying metadata (validation.rs:503-520), produces thumbnails inline in the upload path, gates reads by relay membership rather than channel membership, and counts storage off-path from a Postgres snapshot (media.rs:381-392, 500-515) [SRC]. Grok Bot types size policy and refusal reasons (TOO_LARGE, AGENT_FULL, UNSUPPORTED_TYPE) with two size tiers [REA]. Teams maps a channel to a SharePoint folder and a chat to OneDrive under one storage authority [REA][DOC]. Caveats: malware scanning is Slack-only and documentation-only; no reference evidences asynchronous thumbnailing; room-ACL-gated reads are Verevon's requirement, not Buzz's behaviour.

**Verevon today.** The room composer send path carries only text, skills and a mentioned agent (SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:375-470) and the Space gateway has no upload route [SRC]. Owner-plane plumbing exists only for connector imports: documents.space_ref is written from a signed import decision (documents-api-go space_import.go:1-90), imports-core persists a space_import_intent only on /import/jobs/source while /import/jobs/upload accepts no Space header (imports-core/app/main.py:205-322), and Control issues import decisions only for personal Spaces (personal_thread_decision.go:111-128, 261-272) [SRC]. The main chat already has per-turn attachments with ephemeral extraction (prepare-chat-attachments.ts:1-45, chat/documents.rs:14-33); the /chat upload is JSON text only with a hard-coded zdr_classification and no space_ref (chat/documents.rs:54-110) [SRC]. No original-bytes store, scan or thumbnailing exists in the upload path; MinIO CAS patterns in workspace_cas.rs:1-25 and cas_store.rs:1-16 are templates. Reuse SpaceKnowledgePanel and the retrieval-engine space_knowledge listing for the Files view.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Phase 1 (S): prepareChatAttachments in the room composer, extracted text inline in the user turn, images inline; no backend change. Phase 2 (L): accept the Space ingress decision on /import/jobs/upload, have the gateway issue it with source_type upload, add a shared-Space import validator in Control and a room-audience grant, because the Space document listing filters by owner, org visibility or explicit grant (retrieval-engine api/mod.rs:1659-1665) and an attached file is otherwise invisible to other members. Phase 3 (XL): split out; do not promise presigned slots until the store exists. Constraints: Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL (coordinate with [F-t1-057](#F-t1-057)); ZDR rooms get only ephemeral extraction since imports-core and Control refuse durable imports under ZDR; the gateway buffers multipart bodies today (knowledge/imports.rs:33-90) under a 100m nginx cap. Risks: malware and decompression bombs, storage abuse. Shares the viewer registry with [F-t1-014](#F-t1-014).

<a id="F-t1-014"></a>
### F-t1-014 · Bring agent-produced artifacts and the shared viewer registry into the Space room

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Frontend Plane (UI, viewer registry); Model Plane Session Core (room-level aggregate read) | W1 | Medium |

**What to build.** Map Space transcript turns to artifact panel items and mount the artifact panel that /chat already ships as a cockpit tab or auxiliary panel, with a room-level Artifacts list across threads. Extract the per-kind viewers into one registry shared by the timeline, the artifacts panel and the Files view of [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013), adding lazy DOCX and XLSX preview with a download-card fallback by size. Put an artifact-first rule in Space instructions so analyses and documents land as artifacts, not long posts.

**Why it matters.** This is the visible proof of the human-plus-agent hybrid: agents deliver work as inspectable, revisioned objects while the shared timeline stays short. Persistence already exists, so this is mostly frontend reuse with high visible impact.

**What the references do.** No reference ships artifacts in the room on by default (0 of 5), so the artifact tool, revision stepper and renderer-gated tool offering are Verevon proposals [INF]. Grok Bot is closest: a remote-config rule routes standalone analyses into a canvas (its fallback text refers to a Cursor Canvas inherited from the platform), EditCanvas, WriteDocument and ReadDocument appear as tool-name enum entries, and the canvas skill, documents UI and files stage flags default to false client-side with server enablement unknown [SRC]. Grok Bot's viewer set is confirmed: pdfjs-dist, mammoth for DOCX, xlsx and mermaid as lazy chunks (package.json:93-105) [SRC]. Inline viewers ship in 2 of 5: Grok Bot [SRC] and Slack file previews [DOC]. QM models playgrounds as typed artifacts, but the miniapp tool is deferred and unavailable on every surface; validation checks only size (512 KB) and emptiness, and isolation comes from a strict CSP and sandbox at render time (playground.ts:1-48, web-ui/server/index.ts:235-247) [SRC].

**Verevon today.** The Model Plane already persists a Space turn's artifacts (latest version per id) in durable assistant message metadata unless ZDR (model-gateway/src/sse.rs:4040-4060, session_flow.rs:1490-1510), rehydrates them for revision turns (sse.rs:5300-5350), and the Space shared-read transcript returns that metadata on each turn (http_routes.rs:7726-7754, spaces.rs:2616-2730) [SRC]; that Space-bound turns take this persistence path is inferred from the shared invoke path [INF]. All Space UI is missing: the timeline keeps only role, content, agentName and authorSubjectId (SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:37-62), the composer passes no onArtifact handler (SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:402-470), SpaceTabId has no artifacts tab (SpaceCockpit.tsx:43), ArtifactsPanel and ArtifactViewer live in the chat feature (ChatArtifactPanel.tsx:60-131, 164), package.json:34-35 has only katex and mermaid, and no room-level aggregate exists [SRC]. Reuse ArtifactsPanel (prop-driven), ArtifactViewer, DiffView, sandboxHtmlDocument, chat-artifacts.ts, and history.rs:575-600 as the turn-mapper pattern.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Frontend: map turns to ArtifactPanelItem, mount the panel, extract the registry, add lazy office previews. Model Plane: a room-level aggregate read across threads from Session Core (or N transcript reads), never from Application Plane projections. Carry the artifact-first rule in Space instructions (editor, manager or owner write role) rather than a new binding field. Constraints: artifacts are thread-owned Model Plane data read under the same Control shared-read decision as the turn; add no second read path. Under ZDR artifacts stream live but are not persisted (sse.rs:4048), so the room list is empty and the UI must say so. HTML and PDF artifacts are untrusted: keep the sandboxed iframe and CSP. Space-bound persistence is inferred from the shared chat invoke path [INF]; confirm with one live Space turn first. A later Promote-to-canvas action writes into [F-t1-015](#F-t1-015).

**Quick win.** The per-thread panel is S: persistence and the read route exist and the panel is prop-driven, so mounting it needs only a turn mapper.

<a id="F-t1-015"></a>
### F-t1-015 · Room canvas: a shared, revisioned Space document that humans and agents co-author

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | L | Data Plane v2 (wiki-store-go versions); Control Plane (shared-write decision); Frontend Plane UI; Application Plane pointer; Model Plane agent tool | W3 | High |

**What to build.** Add a Canvas tab to the Space cockpit backed by a Data Plane versioned page, Markdown first. Every save is an append-only version stamped with a typed principal (human or agent) and an expected-head precondition checked in the same transaction, returning a typed conflict; history, diff and restore follow. Agents read and write it through a Model Plane document tool under the run's delegated Space decision, or through the existing proposal path.

**Why it matters.** 4 of 5 references have a room document and Verevon has none, so agents post plans and minutes as long messages and humans have no durable place to co-author with them. Head CAS (compare-and-swap) stops an agent run from silently overwriting a human edit.

**What the references do.** Buzz is the best source for write safety: insert_canvas_head_checked runs a transaction that takes a per-channel advisory lock, reads the head, compares it with the expected revision and only then inserts, with statuses Duplicate, RevisionMismatch, RevisionMissing and SupersedeFailed (buzz-db event.rs:2014-2075); a rejected save is rolled back, not stored, so only superseded revisions remain in history; the editor is a plain textarea with a history panel for diff and restore [SRC]. Slack canvas is the product model: rich blocks, comments that behave like thread replies with resolve, real-time co-editing; channel canvases were retired for a canvas tab in 2025 [DOC]. Teams' analogue is meeting and chat notes on Loop, a weak match [REA][DOC]. Grok Bot canvases are agent-written through a WriteCanvas tool (full contents, no expected revision) and human-read as a rendered bundle; its lock, renew and release RPCs with a TTL lease belong to the agent file store, not canvases [SRC]. Write safety is 2 of 5 only if those locks count; for room canvases Buzz alone has CAS.

**Verevon today.** No Space-owned document and no Canvas tab: SpaceTabId has six tabs (SpaceCockpit.tsx:43), the gateway proxies wiki-store only for operating-map routes (knowledge/wiki.rs:25-159), and the Studio canvas is gateway memory lost on restart (studio.rs:1-5, 35) [SRC]. wiki-store-go has pages, append-only versions, a diff route and agent proposals, but CreateVersion takes no expected-head precondition (wiki_repo.go:359-398), there is no restore route (handler/wiki.go:86-112), versions record only proposed_by_user (wiki_repo.go:378), pages are keyed by workspace_id, and wiki-store verifies no Space decision [SRC]. The Space-to-workspace binding and a read-only listing of published pages already ship in the Knowledge tab (retrieval-engine api/mod.rs:1659-1807) [SRC]. Reuse wiki_proposals with proposed_by_agent, the wiki_propose_edit and wiki_lint tools (execution-core wiki_agent.rs:1-12), chat DiffView, Convex spacePresence, and Control's ValidateForSharedThread as the template for a shared document-write decision.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Data Plane: add an expected_version_id precondition with typed conflict, a restore operation, a principal-kind column, and verification of a Control-signed Space decision header as documents-api does for imports; canvas pages must be Space-owned, because wiki-store authorizes only by org plus scope and a room editor writing an ordinary workspace page would widen owner-plane authority. Control: a new shared-Space document-write decision, since ValidateForImport is personal-Space only (personal_thread_decision.go:261-272). Gateway: /api/v1/spaces/{ref}/canvas routes. Frontend: Canvas tab, Markdown editor, history, diff, restore, conflict banner; a read-only Pages section can come first. Do not reuse Convex plannerDocuments: its space field is a private/shared/collection label and saveState is last-write-wins (plannerDocuments.ts:423-470, schema.ts:276-303). ZDR callers are refused for every wiki mutation (authctx.go:226-241), so ZDR rooms get no canvas and the UI must say so. Depends on [F-t1-057](#F-t1-057) phase 1 for the edit role. Risks: canvas text becomes prompt content and must be framed as untrusted data; version-per-save bloat needs coalescing; live co-editing would make this XL and stays out of phase 1.

<a id="F-t1-016"></a>
### F-t1-016 · Direct messages and group DMs on Space authority, with conversation kind deciding agent scope and history window

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | L | Control Plane (user-core) with Application Plane projections and Model Plane scope refs | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Add dm and group_dm Space kinds with a fixed roster and a participant_hash so the same set of people always resolves to one conversation, hideable per viewer and resurfaced on new activity. Derive the agent scope from the conversation kind: a conversation with my own agent is my personal scope, a group DM a group scope, a room the room scope. Let the person who adds a member to a group DM choose how much history that member may read.

**Why it matters.** One-to-one and small-group chat is an immediate Teams and Slack expectation and is absent. Modelling DMs as small Spaces keeps one authority path, lets a person message a colleague or an agent, and gives a clear rule for which memory, files and sandbox an agent may use where.

**What the references do.** DMs ship in 4 of 5 references, scope by conversation kind in 2 of 5, the adder-chosen history window in 1 of 5 (Teams). Buzz is the best source for DM identity: open_dm computes participant_hash as sha256 over the sorted, deduped member keys, unique per community, and returns the existing DM if one exists (buzz-db dm.rs:50-60, 358-380; channels.sql:32-46); participants are capped at 9 and the set is immutable, so adding someone opens a new DM with no history (command_executor.rs:470-520); hiding is a per-viewer snapshot that the desktop client clears by re-issuing open-DM, plus a server-side unhide for moderation notices [SRC]. Buzz DMs are server-managed channels, not end-to-end encrypted [SRC]. QM's conversationScope maps dm to personal(actor), group to group:ref and anything else to channel:ref, and memory, files, sandbox and crons attach to that scope; copy the semantics, not the string encoding (resolution-service.ts:15-23) [SRC]. Teams lets the adder choose none, the last N days or all history and narrates it with a system message, inferred from locale strings plus documented limits; Teams requires full history access to add an agent to a chat [REA][DOC].

**Verevon today.** Space kinds are personal, room, project and case in Control's CHECK constraint and validators (017_space_authority.up.sql:10-27, authority.go:14-20, 105-112) and the Convex union (schema.ts:546-556), and the create route refuses all but personal and room (spaces.rs:2850-2900) [SRC]. A personal Space is single-subject and Control refuses to replace its membership (repository.go:646-675) [SRC]. The right primitives exist: the audience-revision ceiling on thread and run reads (run_service_grpc.rs:695-740) and the shared decision path for non-personal kinds [SRC]. There is no participant_hash, per-viewer hide, DM sidebar or history window, and human-to-human posting is not possible today because every post is a model turn and Session Core's append_message and start_run require the thread owner (grpc.rs:4495-4560, auth.rs:480-510) [SRC]. Reuse registered_spaces, space_memberships, the recipient-audience registration outbox, the thread-append decision, and the presence, typing and read-marker paths, which need no change.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Keep the new-kind approach: a Control migration extending the CHECK, two validators, the Convex union and the create route are small. Sequence it behind [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001), because Session Core must accept a Control append decision from a non-owner member; L holds only if that item carries the non-owner append work and the sidebar DM section, otherwise this is XL. Model a conversation with my own agent as the personal scope plus an owner-derived personal agent ([F-t1-011](#F-t1-011)), never as a dm Space containing an agent subject. Enforce the history window where content is read (Session Core list filter and retrieval), carried on the member-add grant as history_from and narrated by a system entry ([F-t1-052](#F-t1-052)). Constraints: Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL, so a DM is a Control-registered Space with a fixed roster and no role escalation; deletion authorization covers personal Spaces only (deletion_authorization.go:1-40), so hide-versus-delete semantics need defining. Risks: hiding must not remove membership; history windows must also reach retrieval indexes.

<a id="F-t1-017"></a>
### F-t1-017 · Message lifecycle and reactions: edit and delete as revision entries (owner-of-agent retract, revisions reach the agent), emoji reactions and a per-message action toolbar

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | L | Model Plane (Session Core revisions) with Application Plane (reactions table), Control Plane (policy and audit) and Frontend Plane (toolbar) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Add edited and deleted revision entries in Session Core, never an in-place overwrite, allowed for the author, the owner of the authoring agent, or a Space admin under policy; render an edited marker or tombstone, write an audit event, and append a message_revision entry when the target is already in model-visible history. Add per-user emoji reactions keyed by (thread, seq) and a hover or focus toolbar with quick reactions, copy, edit, delete, pin, quote and fork. Phase 2 makes reactions typed agent input and lets agents react through a capped typed tool.

**Why it matters.** Reactions (5 of 5) and edit or delete (4 of 5) are among the most basic room behaviours and are absent end to end. Revision entries and owner-of-agent retract make them safe in a room where agents read history.

**What the references do.** QM is the best source for revision propagation: a message_revision system entry (action edited or deleted, keyed by the Slack message ts) is appended outside a turn under the session lease and rendered to the model as edited or deleted markers (message-revisions.ts:1-60); QM has no edit or delete UI of its own and ingests Slack's events, so it counts as handling edits on its Slack surface [SRC]. Buzz is the best source for authority: canManageMessage allows the self-author or the viewer who owns the authoring agent, with no admin branch (canManageMessage.ts:1-30); an edit is a separate event kind (kind.rs:478-492); audit retention of soft-deleted events is a vision-document statement [SRC][DOC]. Admin edit and delete policy flags come from Slack and Teams documentation and Teams strings [DOC][REA]. Buzz keys a reaction on community, event and pubkey plus emoji with removed_at (reactions.sql:1-20) [SRC]. QM surfaces inbound reactions to the agent as a rendered text turn behind a shouldSurfaceReaction gate, and agents react through a reply text directive parsed by regex and capped at 5 per turn (reactions.ts:32-57, 155-195); a typed tool is Verevon's improvement [SRC]. Reactions as agent input or output ship in 2 of 5. The role=toolbar hover-actions surface with a more popover is Grok Bot's, which also has feedback votes UP, DOWN, SUBMIT, REVERT and a ReactToGrokBotMessage RPC at schema level [SRC]; Slack's record covers rich clipboard and the native context menu only [SRC].

**Verevon today.** Session Core offers only AppendMessage plus whole-thread DeleteThread, DeleteThreads and DeleteSpaceThreads (sessions.proto:18-204), the gateway Space router has no message PATCH or DELETE (spaces.rs:640-745), and there is no reaction store in Session Core, Convex or the Space UI [SRC]. The only per-post actions are Reply and an author-only Pin (SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:370-420). Chat has a user-message edit that resends, a MessageAction toolbar (ChatMessages.tsx:891-915, 3445-3460) and a thumbs feedback route (chat.rs:66-72), none used in Spaces [SRC]. The SPA has no Convex client; the gateway proxies Convex over HTTP with a service key (config.rs:50-51) [SRC]. Reuse MessageAction and MessageMenuItem lifted to a shared component, the transcript read with its audience-revision predicate (grpc.rs:5425-5475) for a revision fold, the content-digest-bound thread:append decision and MESSAGE_APPENDED event pattern, spaceReadMarkers.ts:1-40 as the model for a content-free gateway-fronted table, and the chat feedback route for thumbs on agent turns.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Split it. Reactions plus toolbar (M): a content-free Convex spaceReactions table (space_ref, thread, seq, subject, emoji, removed_at) behind a gateway toggle route that checks Control membership and the audience; counts ride with the transcript read, and changes push through a gateway event or the existing poll, not a Convex subscription. Revision entries (L): a revision kind in Session Core appended under the Space decision and folded at read time, Control org and Space policy flags with an audit event, PATCH and DELETE routes, and prompt-builder rendering. Expose message id and seq in the Space transcript DTO, which TranscriptTurn drops today. Make [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) an explicit dependency, since non-owner authorship does not exist yet. Constraints: the transcript is append-only; under ZDR no durable original exists, and elsewhere keeping the original conflicts with privacy deletion and needs a per-org rule; a reaction must never stand in for a Control approval; the agent wake gate defaults off. Risks: deleting an entry an agent already acted on does not undo its effects, so the tombstone must say so; no third-party GIF proxy because of data egress and ZDR.

<a id="F-t1-018"></a>
### F-t1-018 · Permission-checked message search: Session Core index with per-hit re-authorization and ZDR exclusion, in-room scoped search on Ctrl/Cmd+F and open-in-context

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | L | Model Plane (Session Core index) with Frontend Plane (gateway route, UI) and Control Plane (read decision) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Add a keyword index over Space thread messages in Session Core, a `SearchSpaceMessages` call and a gateway route `GET /api/v1/spaces/{ref}/search`. The index only proposes candidates: every hit is re-authorized against the Control room-read decision and audience revision, and turns stamped Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) or privacy-restricted are never indexed. In the room, Ctrl/Cmd+F opens a search with a removable scope chip (room, thread, person, global); hits open in context through `?thread_id=&seq=` deep links and cursor paging of the thread list.

**Why it matters.** There is no message, thread or room search, and a room used for months is unusable without one. Full-text search is table stakes in 4 of 5 references (per-hit re-authorization is verified only in Buzz), and a room agent asked "what did we decide about X" needs the same permission-checked candidate source.

**What the references do.** Buzz is the best implementation: a STORED generated `tsvector` column (simple config) with a GIN (Generalized Inverted Index) index, where eight private event kinds produce NULL so they never match, and a community id is required at the type level [SRC]. Queries use `websearch_to_tsquery` ranked by `ts_rank_cd`; prefix mode appends `:*` to the trailing token only [SRC]. The relay reads candidates in pages of 100 and applies a per-hit predicate (filter match, accessible channel, reader authorized), so a page can be shorter than the limit [SRC]. Full-text search is 4 of 5 (buzz, teams, qm, slack); qm has its own tsvector over session entries [SRC]; Slack's place in the count rests on product knowledge, not a record [INF]. In-room search on the native Find command is 3 of 5: Buzz maps Mod+F to the current channel and Mod+K to everything, and Backspace on an empty query clears the scope chip [SRC]; Slack's shell routes Find to in-app search without showing whether it is pre-scoped [REA]; Teams' scopes are locale strings only [REA]. Caveats: Teams search runs on the unobservable Substrate service, so it must not be cited for per-hit re-authorization, which is verified only in Buzz; Grok Bot's search is agent-side [SRC].

**Verevon today.** The gateway search router serves web, similar, suggest, images, videos, suggestions and answer only (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/search.rs:30-45); the sidebar filter matches room names and thread titles only (CoreSidebarSpacesPanel.tsx:80-110). Session Core has no tsvector index and no search RPC (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:5425-5475); the thread list takes only a limit (spaces.rs:945-960) and `?thread_id=` links are never consumed (src/features/spaces/lib/activity-grammar.ts:190-206). Reuse the audience-revision predicate (grpc.rs:5455-5461), the Space-scoped read decision of `space_thread_transcript` (spaces.rs:2606-2700) and `idx_messages_author_subject` (migration 0035) for a person filter.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Index in Session Core, which owns message text; never copy transcripts into Data v2 or Convex. The ZDR prerequisite is already met because Session Core refuses durable writes from ZDR callers (auth.rs:276-285). Use one Space-scoped read decision per query, re-filter every hit, and check on every query because revocations ride plain NATS. Ship the thread-list cursor and deep-link consumer in the same slice. Start keyword-only; the simple config has no Norwegian stemming. Risks: treating the index as an access boundary; a deep link the viewer can no longer read must fail closed; keep the browser find bar as a fallback.

<a id="F-t1-019"></a>
### F-t1-019 · Let room agents call Space actions themselves (agent self-API with run-bound grants)

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (catalogue and dispatch) with Control Plane (run-action decisions) and Frontend Plane (action registry) | W3 | High |

**What to build.** Generalize the existing one-action agent-to-owner pipeline so a room agent can perform a vetted subset of Space actions (rename or pin a thread, post a card, pause itself, create a thread, schedule a follow-up). Each admitted action gets an owner-side endpoint that verifies a Control run-action decision and re-checks its own access control list (ACL), a pinned server-owned schema in Capability Core and an execution-core adapter. Risky calls route to the existing in-room approval card.

**Why it matters.** This is the core of "agents that work like a human": today a room agent can only talk. Verevon already has a typed registry with risk level and owner plane per action and a run-bound decision for one action, but verification raised the effort from M to L because that one-action pipeline must first be generalized; qm and buzz show agents organising threads, scheduling follow-ups and pinning.

**What the references do.** qm is best (2 of 5 references; a differentiator absent from the human-chat incumbents). It serves a discoverable `GET /v1/apis` catalogue of API families with guidance text and eligibility predicates on `isAdmin` and `swarmsEnabled` [SRC]. The runtime signs a sandbox turn token (48 h default, configurable including no-expiry) with claims `actorId`, `scopeId`, `orgId` and optional session, run, attempt and lease fields, sent as `x-agent-capability`; only swarm operations require a durable run [SRC] [DOC]. The admin tool wraps the `/v1/admin` routes, so agent calls pass through the handlers an administrator uses; the authority recheck is inferred from that routing [INF]. Buzz exposes about 25 CLI (command-line interface) command groups as JSON-in/JSON-out subcommands authenticated with NIP-98 signed events [SRC]; "everything a human can do" is a VISION.md intent [DOC]. Caveat: qm families are feature-gated.

**Verevon today.** A governed pipeline exists for exactly one action, `tickets.create`: Control's run-action decision and owner-effect reservation (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/run_action_decision.go:340-347), Capability Core advertising (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/api/model_action_view.go:21-32), the adapter in execution-core/src/ticket_tools.rs and approval continuation, all hard-coded to that action. `MODEL_EXECUTABLE_ACTION_IDS` has 8 entries and none of the 14 `spaces.*` actions (src/shared/actions/model-eligibility.ts:126-135); the Space catalogue is human-only (spaces.rs:4880-4925). Room agents are born tool-less and confirmation-only (convex/spaceAgents.ts:540-565). Reuse `RunActionDecision`, the tickets.create pipeline as template, the agent-action-grant routes (spaces.rs:665-675) and `SpaceApprovalPanel`.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Do not mint a new grant type and do not let the Model Plane consume the Frontend registry: Capability Core deliberately holds server-owned schemas (model_action_view.go:29-32). Add one `runActionTarget` entry per action, generalize model-action-view and Capability Core to a server-owned list, and add an owner endpoint per owning plane. Phase it: first Model-Plane-owned tools on the run's own thread (rename, pin, post a card), which need no cross-plane decision; then Application-owned (pause self) and orchestrator-owned (schedule follow-up). Make binding policy editable, or no room agent can use tools. Constraints: Model proposes, owner executes; a run's thread authorization is not authority over a target (run_action_decision.go:19-22); membership, role, deletion and credential changes stay human-only; ZDR forbids durable owner actions (personal_thread_decision.go:292-294). Depends on [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010). Risk: prompt injection, so the grant must intersect with the delegating human's authority on every call and never widen the owner-plane ACL.

**First phase.** The first phase reuses the tickets.create machinery and needs no cross-plane decision; L covers the full generalization. It is not counted as a quick win (see section 9.2).

<a id="F-t1-020"></a>
### F-t1-020 · Room-scoped agent browser: space_ref on browser runs, a live preview card that expands to an interactive view, hand-off and take-over with a control indicator

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | L | Ingestion Plane (Quarry live view, challenge signal) with Model Plane (space_ref, hand-off), Control Plane (room decision) and Frontend Plane (card, modal) | W3 | High for the Verevon gap, Medium for reference mechanisms |

**What to build.** Add `space_ref` to the browser run start request, authorize it with a Control decision bound to the room audience hash and revision, and show browser steps and evidence as room cards. Let the room watch the session as a preview card that expands to an interactive view in the AuxPane with a "control: agent | you" indicator, Take control and I'm done. Turn Quarry's challenge classification into a hand-off request (id plus instruction) instead of a retry. Quarry executes every action; the Model only proposes.

**Why it matters.** The brief asks for agents that work like a person with their own browser. Browser runs and live frames with takeover exist, but nothing is Space-scoped, so the room cannot see, attribute or govern the agent's browsing.

**What the references do.** An agent browser exists in 2 of 5 (qm, grokbot). qm mints a browser-model capability token; the runner calls a completions endpoint at core so it never holds a provider key, gets 403 without a grant and 409 when the account or model changed, and sign-in profiles are used only in direct messages [SRC]. Live view with takeover is 2 of 5, Grok Bot best in class: an embedded noVNC (browser VNC, Virtual Network Computing) view, a `LiveState` hand-off request id plus instruction, and an `EndHandoff` RPC with triggers BUTTON, VIEWER_CLOSED or DISMISSED [SRC]; qm's browse skill hands the person a live-view link for sign-in walls, then re-runs [SRC]. Grok Bot classifies bot walls from URL and title as non-retryable `botBlock` [SRC], but the link to the hand-off flow was not traced [INF]; a periodic-frame preview mode was not verified [SRC]. Teach by demonstration is 1 of 5, protos and strings only [SRC]. Caveats: qm's browser is a prompt-level skill needing an org provider key; a space_ref and room-scoped preview card are Verevon proposals, as no reference ties browser runs to a shared room [INF].

**Verevon today.** `BrowserRunStartRequest` has no Space field and the step always sends an empty `space_id` (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/browser_run.rs:87-122, 425-441; the comment names this as the follow-up). Building blocks exist outside Spaces: takeover with a control chip and frame stream routes on the dashboard (src/features/dashboard/home/BrowserChrome.tsx:497, 1228-1236; apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:371-445), per-action human-in-the-loop (HITL) gates (execution-core/src/browser_agent/browser_risk.rs:1-60), and Quarry challenge classification that nothing consumes (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:154-200). Run-level `require_approval` is rejected (browser_run.rs:70-80). Reuse these plus `space_capability_decision.go` as the decision template and `BrowserProfileScope` with its ZDR guard.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Four pieces: plumb `space_ref` into `ExecuteStepRequest.space_id` with a Control Space browser decision; make execution-core turn a Quarry `ChallengeSignal` into a hand-off request; add a Space-audience-authorized frame stream; mount a room card and expandable view reusing `BrowserChrome`. Constraints: broker-signed grants cannot widen the domain policy (browser_run.rs:128-200); force ephemeral or run-scoped profiles so a member's sign-in is never shared into a room; restrict takeover to the requester or an owner; use per-action HITL, not run-level approval; no frame retention under ZDR. Depends on [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004), [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012), [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) and [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001). Teach by demonstration is a separate XL. Risk: frames carry sensitive data, so honour privacy tier and read entitlement.

<a id="F-t1-021"></a>
### F-t1-021 · Agent computer confinement: real network enforcement and resource caps, audience-bound egress, one SSRF-safe connector

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Model Plane (execution-core, sandbox-manager) with Ingestion Plane (Quarry) and Control Plane (egress decision) | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** Close the gap that bites today: per-spawn CPU, memory and pid caps (prlimit or rlimit in the spawn wrapper, or cgroup limits in compose), plus one rule that every Space agent code path holds a sandbox-manager lease. Defer a proxy-only network namespace and per-room egress allowlists until a feature needs sandbox network. Keep server-side request forgery (SSRF) protection where it lives: new unfurls, webhooks and agent fetches go through a Quarry endpoint and the gateway's forward-don't-fetch test.

**Why it matters.** Agent computers are part of the Space vision, and confinement is the real security boundary under every softer control. A room-aware egress rule fits "Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL": an agent acting for a room must not reach a destination any member's org forbids.

**What the references do.** Sandbox confinement is at least 2 of 5 (grokbot, qm). Grok Bot's exec daemon takes a declarative policy (readonly or workspace read-write, writable roots, network none or proxy), runs a Linux kernel preflight, and on Windows is proxy-only with no filesystem isolation [SRC]. Its enforcement binary is not shipped and no bubblewrap, Landlock or seccomp string exists, so the mechanism is unknown [INF]; its cgroup step only attaches processes to an external workload cgroup and sets no caps [SRC]. qm runs agents in hosted backends (E2B, Modal, Sprites, DO, smolmachines) with an optional egress proxy [SRC]. Egress policy is 2 of 5: qm's `audience-floor.ts` intersects members' allowlists and unions denies [SRC], but enforcement is fail-open when a backend proxy URL is unset [SRC] [DOC]. SSRF-safe fetch is 2 of 5 with differing mechanisms: Grok Bot link previews pin IP plus SNI and re-validate up to 5 redirects; Buzz pins IP but disables webhook redirects and uses an enumerated deny list, so unlisted special ranges pass [SRC].

**Verevon today.** Filesystem confinement fails closed: bubblewrap read-only root, cleared environment, pid, uts and ipc unshare, unsandboxed spawn refused (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/sandbox.rs:1-60, 150-230; executor.rs:60-200), plus a seccomp profile and a capability-dropping production overlay (apps/Model Plane/deploy/seccomp-bwrap.json:1-30; docker-compose.production.yml:1-20). `code_interpreter` and the process host hard-code `MpNetworkPolicy::Disabled` (process_host.rs:417-431, code_interpreter.rs:575-581), so no Space agent path has network. Missing: CPU, memory and pid caps (only a timeout and a 64 KiB output cap; sandbox-manager store.go:255-290 counts processes), and the lease is mandatory only for Space code_interpreter (sandbox_lease.rs:106-151). Control has a coarse `space:egress` permission (space_capability_decision.go:120-175). SSRF is already Quarry: dns_guard.rs, a DNS-pinned egress proxy, redirect re-validation (quarry-tls/src/redirect_policy.rs) and gateway forwarding (public_url.rs; tests/ssrf_forward_not_fetch.rs).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Add caps in the execution-core spawn wrapper, which every path already runs through `require_requested_isolation_for`, or at container level; extend the lease rule in sandbox_lease.rs. When network is needed, extend the Control `space:egress` decision rather than inventing `effective_egress`; the audience intersection needs Control's snapshot, and Quarry or the proxy enforces it with audited allow and deny. A shared SSRF crate was deliberately rejected (dns_guard.rs module doc). Constraints: Model proposes, Quarry executes; a proxy-only netns needs capabilities the container lacks (sandbox.rs:24-45), so it is a spike on the production image; ZDR is compatible because scratch execution is ephemeral. Depends on [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) and [F-t1-031](#F-t1-031). Risk: production kernels differ from dev hosts.

<a id="F-t1-022"></a>
### F-t1-022 · Room administration basics: change and choose member roles with an owner guard, rename, topic, archive and restore, and keep the organization room roster in sync

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Control Plane (role and lifecycle) with Application Plane (grant list, org-room sync) and Frontend Plane (Members tab) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Two quick wins: run `syncAllOrganizationRooms` on a schedule and from the existing org member_changed handler, and let managers rename a room and edit its topic. Then the Control-contract changes: a per-member role change and role choice on add (viewer, editor, manager, owner) with pure guard functions (elevated roles granted only by owner or admin; the sole owner cannot be demoted, removed or leave) and table tests, plus an archived registration state that denies writes and allows reads. Drop "delete with a restore window" until the deletion saga covers shared rooms.

**Why it matters.** Control already stores graded roles and lifecycle states, but no route or UI changes a role, shared rooms cannot be renamed or archived, and the organization room's roster is declared once and never re-synced. Graded roles are in all five references, and rename, topic and archive are in at least three.

**What the references do.** Graded roles exist in 5 of 5 references. Buzz is best: a closed `MemberRole` enum where `is_elevated` is Owner or Admin; elevated roles are granted or changed only by an owner or admin, and `is_sole_owner` blocks demoting, removing or departing as the last owner [SRC]. The time-of-check-to-time-of-use (TOCTOU) guard is a per-channel Postgres advisory lock across the transactional check-then-write [SRC]. Slack has the richest role set (primary owner, owner, admin, member, two guest tiers) [DOC]; qm keeps separate read, write and manage predicates per scope kind [SRC]. Rename, topic and archive are at least 3 of 5 (buzz, teams, slack): Buzz lets owners or admins change the name, any member the topic, and archives expired TTL (time-to-live) rooms with a sliding deadline and reaper, which is Buzz-unique [SRC]; Teams has a 30-day restore window [DOC] [REA]; Slack's behaviour is public product knowledge [INF]. Starter rooms with a welcome agent are 2 of 5, but in Buzz they are desktop-client choreography, not a server-side on-join hook [SRC].

**Verevon today.** Control converges viewer, editor, manager and owner with an owner guard (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/repository.go:600-733), but Application grants only "editor" (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:821-836, spaceMembers.ts:85), the gateway has only `POST /members` and `DELETE /members/{id}` (apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:649-738), and `SpaceMemberControls` has no role control. The spaces row has no topic, no lifecycle has an archived value (schema.ts:546-604; migrations/017_space_authority.up.sql:5-30), and deletion admits personal Spaces only (deletion_authorization.go:1-40). `syncAllOrganizationRooms` has no caller and there is no crons file (spaceMembershipSync.ts:1-150; spaces.ts:1425-1452), although a durable member_changed consumer runs (nats-subscriber.js:300-330). Reuse `ReplaceMemberships`, `roomMembersForControl`, `transitionLifecycle` and `require_space_agent_grant_role`.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Application Plane: add `convex/crons.ts` calling `syncAllOrganizationRooms` and call `syncOrganizationRoom` from the member_changed handler; add `spaces.topic` plus rename and topic mutations gated by manager or owner. Control Plane: the replace-memberships request carries no acting subject, so rank rules cannot be enforced through the declarative PUT; add a per-member change-role endpoint that carries the actor, and an archived registration_state with decision gating. Control stays the final check; Application's list is intent only. Exclude the organization room from role editing because its roster derives from org-core. Depends on [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) for org-admin authority over orphaned rooms. Risks: Convex edit plus Control declaration are two steps, so keep the declaration idempotent and retried; ZDR orgs need immediate purge rather than a restore window when deletion lands.

**Quick win.** The reconcile and rename or topic slices are S each and reuse existing code; L covers the role and archive contract changes.

<a id="F-t1-023"></a>
### F-t1-023 · Enterprise identity: stop the fake-success SCIM stub, real SCIM 2.0 with deprovisioning that suspends a person's agents, per-org SSO enforcement and domain verification; linked identities later

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Stub | XL (split: S, M, L, L) | Control Plane (auth-core, org-core, user-core) | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** First, a one-line safety fix: make the SCIM (System for Cross-domain Identity Management) data routes in integration-corev2 answer 501 so no identity provider (IdP) believes a deprovision succeeded. Then build on the org-core domain substrate: a real DNS or IdP verification flow, an enforce-SSO (single sign-on) flag with a break-glass owner, allowlist or auto-join, and a typed session state in the UI. Then SCIM Users in org-core mapping deactivate to member status suspended. Groups, the cascade that suspends a person's agents, and principal links are later slices.

**Why it matters.** SSO and SCIM are table stakes for enterprise buyers. Today SSO sits behind a global flag and the SCIM stub returns success without applying anything, so a deprovisioned employee silently keeps access and their agents keep acting.

**What the references do.** SSO and identity federation is 4 of 5 (teams, qm, slack, buzz as partial). Teams is best on the client: a native OneAuth/WAM broker, home-realm discovery against Microsoft's identity cloud and a typed `UserAuthState` (Up, InteractionRequired, LicenseError, SignInRestriction, UnRedeemed) known from strings and RPC names [REA]; it relies on Entra ID provisioning and is not a SCIM reference. Slack is the SCIM reference: create and deactivate members and IdP group sync, plan-gated to Business+ and Enterprise, with no SCIM version stated, so "SCIM 2.0" is Verevon's choice [DOC]. qm's principal links are the best linked-identity design: a link never creates an admin, records evidence and is reversible [SRC] [DOC]. Buzz's federation is relay-side only, with a planned client flow and no SAML [SRC]. Allowlists are 2 of 5, but qm's actor gate only filters Slack ingress and Buzz's is a relay pubkey allowlist, so checking at sign-up and agent invocation is an extrapolation [INF]. Suspending a person's agents on deprovision is Verevon design.

**Verevon today.** The only SCIM code is a fake-success intake in the Ingestion Plane: `/api/v1/scim/v2/*` authenticates a per-org bearer token, stores a webhook event, publishes `scim_event_received` and answers 201, 204 or an empty list without changing anyone (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/api/server.go:1225-1290, 2713-2790); no Control service consumes the event. SSO is the better-auth plugin behind the global `SSO_ENABLED` flag (apps/Control Plane/auth-core/src/auth/auth.ts:995-1040). Unconnected substrate exists: `sso_provider` rows with organization_id and domain (auth-schema.ts:101-110), org-core `organization_domains` with pending, verified and revoked states but no verification handler (org-core/migrations/011_verified_organization_domains.up.sql:1-30; domain_invitation_policy.go:1-35), a suspended member status (org/types.go:164-173) and `RevokeSession` plus `RevokeAllSessions` (auth-core/src/grpc/auth/v1/auth.ts:188-200). Reuse all of it, and the `scim_tokens` store as a model to re-home.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Identity lifecycle is Control-owned, so re-home the SCIM endpoint and token store to org-core rather than bridging an Ingestion event over NATS. Space access already requires an active org membership at Control (user-core/internal/spaces/repository.go:828-852), so suspending a member cuts new Space decisions at once; remaining gaps are in-flight runs (revocation rides plain NATS), the organization room's roster ([F-t1-022](#F-t1-022)) and agent owner links, which Control does not hold because `agents.createdBy` is optional and Convex-side. The agent-suspension cascade depends on [F-t1-043](#F-t1-043); durable membership delivery is [F-t1-048](#F-t1-048); Groups wait for [F-t1-054](#F-t1-054); principal links wait for the docs-only Channel Plane. Risks: enforced SSO can lock owners out; domain verification must be real because the org-core table says a Brreg match is not proof; IdPs expect idempotent PATCH and precise error codes; a wrong principal link is an impersonation bug.

<a id="F-t1-024"></a>
### F-t1-024 · Client resilience: root and per-region error boundaries, lazy-chunk recovery, a boot watchdog, and a build id with stale-tab reload and contract guard

| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | M | Frontend Plane | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Wrap the app in a root error boundary plus per-region boundaries (navbar, Space cockpit, side panels, run console) showing a classified failure card (render error, stale-deploy chunk failure, session expired, gateway unreachable) with Reload and Retry. Reload once on a Vite chunk-preload failure with a sessionStorage loop guard. Expose a build id through a Vite define and a no-store `version.json`, add a minimum-client or contract version from the gateway, show a non-blocking "new version, reload" banner, and force reload only on a contract mismatch after drafts are saved.

**Why it matters.** The code itself states there is no Suspense or ErrorBoundary, so one render error blanks a Space people keep open all day, and a tab open across a deploy keeps an old bundle whose lazy chunks can 404. Both are cheap to fix and visible to users and to the agent run consoles in those regions.

**What the references do.** Crash and render recovery is 4 of 5 (slack, teams, grokbot, buzz), so it is table stakes. Teams has the richest native recovery: an error curtain with diagnosis-specific strings for certificate, proxy, policy, corrupt package, resource, OS and runtime-version failures, and an unresponsive-renderer toast with Wait, Trace and Restart [REA]; the curtain is a native-shell screen for when the web client fails to start, not a render boundary, and much of it is flag-controlled. Grok Bot has one client restore lifecycle with a 30 s stuck detector reporting the stage reached [SRC]. Slack shows fatal-exception and unresponsive-window dialogs with telemetry flush [SRC]. Buzz has a root React boundary with a Reload button [SRC]; qm has none. Version gating is 2 of 5 (grokbot, teams): Grok Bot's remote `sand_min_client_version` has fixed fields, only `min_version` and `max_age_days` have client consumers, and a `SAND_CLIENT_UPDATE_REQUIRED` header starts an auto-updater check; no banner or forced reload was observed [SRC]. Teams' nag-then-block policy is documentation only [DOC]. Per-region boundaries, chunk recovery and a build-id stale-tab reload appear in no reference and are Verevon proposals [INF].

**Verevon today.** No ErrorBoundary, Suspense, `vite:preloadError`, `unhandledrejection` or watchdog code exists: `index.tsx` renders App directly, about 30 routes are `lazy()` imports (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/app/App.tsx:1-60), and the only trace is the shim comment (src/shared/lib/create-resource-compat.ts:3-9). nginx answers a missing hashed asset with 404 (nginx.conf:103-114) and serves index.html and json with no cache header (nginx.conf:132-133); vite.config.ts defines no build id; the gateway `/version` reports only the server revision (apps/gateway/src/main.rs:205-221). Reuse Solid 2 rc.0's `Errored`, `Loading` and `createErrorBoundary` (node_modules/solid-js/dist/solid.js:1117), the code-keyed copy in shared/i18n/errors.ts, and the SSE (server-sent events) reader's Last-Event-ID resume (src/shared/api/sse.ts:25-60) as the reconnect hook.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Frontend Plane only. Put `Errored` boundaries at the root and around each region with a failure card keyed by error code; add a preload-error listener that reloads once; emit `/version.json` with no-store through an explicit nginx location, because json currently falls through to the SPA (single-page application) fallback, and give index.html no-cache; add a gateway min-client header or `/api/v1/client-config`; compare on visibilitychange and SSE reconnect. One correction: `apps/gateway/src/domains/monitoring.rs` is Quarry change-monitoring and `observability.rs` exposes only Prometheus metrics, so there is no client-diagnostics endpoint or request id; add a small ids-only route under `/api/v1`, or drop the boot-stage recorder. Depends on persistent drafts ([F-t1-032](#F-t1-032)) before any forced reload and on [F-t1-079](#F-t1-079) for the report action. Risks: boundaries that swallow errors silently; telemetry must be ids-only under ZDR; the contract version must be deliberate, not the build hash.

**Quick win.** Boundaries, one-time chunk recovery and the build-id banner are S and use primitives already in the bundle; the contract header and draft-safe reload make the full item M.

## 5. Recommended

The Recommended tier holds work that makes a Space a credible place for humans and agents to work together but that is either large, staged behind other items, or cheaper than its original framing suggested. Each card below states the verified gap in CoresSystem code, the reference mechanism it borrows, and the approach after verification. Where verification found more shipped than the original item assumed, the card says so and re-scopes to the real gap. Prevalence counts are "n of 5 references" (Teams, Slack, Grok Bot, Buzz, QM).

<a id="F-t1-025"></a>
### F-t1-025 · Multi-agent rooms: turn-taking, governed delegation, agent-to-agent messages and background child work

*Verevon today: Planned · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Let several agents live in one room and cooperate: a coordinator fans out per-member turn requests with a nonce, deadline and parent ids, a hop limit and loop detector, and an agent-to-agent message contract with typed delivery outcomes. A delegate re-resolves its own authority through Control, and the whole chain renders as one collapsed unit with an openable transcript. This is the core of the Space vision and the main gap relative to Grok Bot.

**Reference practice.** Multi-agent turn-taking appears in 2 of 5 references and agent-to-agent messaging in 3 of 5. Grok Bot's coordinator sends each member a turn request carrying nonce, peers, new messages, deadline and root parent id; members answer SENT, PASS, SKIPPED, TIMEOUT or CANCELLED, with idempotency by nonce, a depth-based hop limit and a loop detector that runs off, shadow or on [REA]. QM's orchestrator cannot run tools, posts an early acknowledgement and delegates to child sessions that inherit scope and audience, with tree caps and cycle checks; Buzz shows the failure mode without a breaker [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** A room message addresses exactly one agent, held as a single mentionedAgentRef (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:149) and applied by the gateway as a persona and binding-policy overlay on the human's own turn (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4399-4482). execution-core has generic subagent delegation at depth 1 with the same identity (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/subagent/mod.rs:1-70), and StartManagedRunRequest carries no space_id or audience (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sessions.proto:493-509), although child runs are registered on the parent's thread_id, so they are not Space-less in effect. UI-2c is designed but not started [SRC]. Stage it as the plan decides: first a narrow delegate_to_agent capability in execution-core's governed loop (same room, one hop, human present) that re-enters a new internal gateway endpoint under the original human's bearer and the existing persona pipeline, plus a collapsed card; then multi-mention fan-out in the gateway; only then the coordinator with the nonce, deadline and outcome protocol and shadow loop detection. The coordinator cannot be driven by Application room events, because Application publishes none; that feed is part of the work.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002), [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004), [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) and [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027) caps first; loops and cost blow-up are the Buzz failure, and every hop must record the initiating human. Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) runs register no child lineage, so the collapsed chain cannot rely on durable lineage for them.

<a id="F-t1-026"></a>
### F-t1-026 · Typed cards and agent input requests (forms and secret drop)

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Frontend Plane, Model Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give agents a small typed card vocabulary (sections, buttons, select, fields, chart, link, with a plain-text fallback) that rooms render natively, and a request_input tool that shows a form and resumes work on submit. Structured input makes multi-human rooms readable and replaces long prose questions with compact, actionable UI.

**Reference practice.** Forms and secret capture ship in 2 of 5 references and cards or blocks in 2 of 5. QM's secret drop mints a one-time URL with a seven-day time-to-live and records in the transcript only that a secret was provided [SRC]. Grok Bot's request_input tool takes fields, a secure flag and multi-step, creates a pending widget in the thread, and Respond, Submit or Dismiss resume the run with a typed message [REA]. Slack Block Kit is the best-specified card schema, server-validated with action round-trips and a required top-level text [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Rooms render user turns as plain text and assistant turns as Markdown plus one built-in approval card decided only by the run owner (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:322-348; SpaceApprovalPanel.tsx:23-130).
