Agents ask today by ending the turn with a prose question (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:166-186). The AG-UI client-tool path cannot carry a pause and resume, and approval-gated resume is quarantined until a descriptor-backed dispatcher exists (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/approvals.rs:254-272). No credential broker exists in apps/Control Plane [SRC]. Split into two slices: extend the existing shared/chat-nodes ConversationNode union with form, choice and link nodes, render them in SpaceRoomTimeline and validate server-side in the gateway; then make request_input an end-turn tool whose result carries a typed form node, with the submit posted as a typed user turn under the existing append decision that starts a new run. Drop the secret-drop half: S3.4 says to reuse existing secret stores, not add a keychain, so secure fields wait for S3.4.

**Dependencies and risks.** Runs are owner-bound, so only the member who started the run can answer; card actions are an injection path and need server-side validation under a Control decision; nothing may be stored for ZDR threads.

<a id="F-t1-027"></a>
### F-t1-027 · Agent operations: per-room and per-agent cost and budgets, admin allow/block, answer-quality surface, and room-behaviour evals

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Attribute usage to Space and agent binding by flow, add a per-Space allowance with warnings and a room spend panel, let admins allow or block agent definitions with a reason, bring thumbs-plus-reason feedback and citations to rooms, and run room-behaviour evals. Per-room cost is the safeguard that makes multi-agent rooms, browsers and computers affordable to ship.

**Reference practice.** Cost and usage attribution ships in 4 of 5 references; room-behaviour evals in 2 of 5. Grok Bot keeps cost series by flow and by bot with hard limits and usage warnings [REA]. Teams labels agent messages, shows citation chips and collects feedback with a reason and explicit allow or block per agent app [REA]. Buzz's evals run the real product process tree with scripted human actors and assert thread placement, notifications and silence, used as release regression [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** cost-core's ledger dimensions are tenant, actor, run, request, model and producer, with no Space or agent (apps/Model Plane/go/services/cost-core/internal/ledger/ledger.go:40-50), and session-core reports run tokens as 0 so the room cost line prints nothing (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/activity-grammar.ts:690-725). model-gateway budget.rs checks only request and org ceilings, and the room surfaces only the org budget_exceeded refusal (SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:530-575). Already present: the Agent chip on agent turns, thumbs-plus-reason feedback in /chat, a closed failure-code allowlist in session-core, and the eval-lab-py live-stack case runner [SRC]. Drop the AI label (the chip exists) and build evals on eval-lab-py. Slice one is "attribute and show": carry space_ref and binding ref through USAGE_ENVELOPE and run records, fix the zero token fields, then a room spend panel. Add allowance enforcement afterwards, token-based so it survives ZDR, which refuses max_cost_usd on ZDR turns. Treat allow/block (Control, M) and evals (M-L) as separate items.

**Dependencies and risks.** Feedback is owner-bound, so rating another member's turn needs a Space-aware feedback authority first; keep ZDR usage aggregate-only.

<a id="F-t1-028"></a>
### F-t1-028 · Persistent per-agent computer with lifecycle UX and a governed egress boundary

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Grow the run-scoped sandbox into a per-(Space, agent) computer: a resource record with lifecycle states, a durable home that survives runs, hibernate and pre-thaw on presence intent, a room view of files and processes with stop and kill, and network egress through an authorising proxy with an audience floor. An agent that works like a colleague with its own PC is the other half of the Grok Bot vision.

**Reference practice.** A persistent agent computer ships in 3 of 5 references; a sandbox security boundary in 2 of 5. QM keeps a sandbox resource record with owning scope, provider and lifecycle, snapshots the home to object storage, and issues a signed egress token whose allowed hosts are the intersection over the audience and denied hosts the union, enforced by a proxy that rejects private ranges [SRC]. Grok Bot reports client presence intents (app foreground, composer focus, typing) to pre-thaw, never wakes for background-tagged calls, and batches pattern-triggered wake-ups [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** More ships than the original item assumed: a Control-signed, backend-pinned Space capability profile and lease, a durable Space workspace with PromoteWorkspace (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/workspace_promote.rs:1-20), a process registry with agent-callable signal and kill tools (apps/Model Plane/go/services/sandbox-manager/internal/process/store.go:1-40), and a Watch core in capability-core [SRC]. Still missing: lifecycle states beyond SCRATCH, ACTIVE, SNAPSHOTTING and DESTROYED (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sandboxes.proto:14-47), any room route to stop a process or create watches (the gateway reports watches_not_built, apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:1618-1628), any sandbox network path (AllowDomains is refused without a proxy, execution-core/src/executor.rs:160-181), and per-turn egress tokens. Stage it: a room stop/kill route under a Control effect decision (S-M); watches surfaced through capability-core /api/v1/watches (M); a per-(Space, agent) home with new lifecycle states, snapshots and an explicit ephemeral rule for ZDR (L); an authorising egress proxy on the Model Plane side that reuses Quarry's dns_guard and the signed space:egress permission, not Quarry's loopback browser proxy (L). Drop the user's own machine from the recommendation.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027) and [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012); authenticated egress waits for the S3.4 broker ([F-t1-044](#F-t1-044)); no ZDR handling was found in sandbox-manager or the lease code [INF].

<a id="F-t1-030"></a>
### F-t1-030 · Room content hardening: read authorization for attachments and transcripts, stricter CSP, anti-spoofing, link and download safety

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Frontend Plane · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** A room renders other people's and agents' content, so an XSS or exfiltration channel acts with a user's agent authority. Render an explicit restricted state instead of swallowing a 403, add link scheme rules and destination display, and roll out a tighter Content Security Policy (CSP) through a report-only candidate.

**Reference practice.** A strict CSP with Trusted Types ships in 2 of 5 references (Teams, Slack) and link and download safety in 1 of 5. Teams uses a default-src 'self' policy with nonce-based scripts, Trusted Types, object-src 'none' and a stricter report-only candidate [REA]. Buzz consults a kind-level read policy on every read path and returns not-found for hidden kinds [SRC]. Slack applies a scheme allowlist and warns on unusual protocols [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** The server is already deny-by-default: every Space read takes a Control shared-read decision and the transcript route returns 403 space_read_not_authorized (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:2616-2680), but SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:197-205 swallows that 403 and falls back to the thread preview, so a room without shared reads silently shows only your own posts. nginx.conf:46-70 enforces a CSP with connect-src 'self' https: and img-src 'self' data: https: blob: and no report-only policy; the markdown renderer builds anchors with no scheme check (src/features/chat/components/chat-media-markdown.tsx:1273-1281). Spaces have no attachments or download route yet [SRC]. Re-scope: render a room-level restricted state (S); add an https and mailto allowlist with the destination host shown for external links (S); add a Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only candidate with a gateway report endpoint, then narrow connect-src and img-src behind a media proxy (M). Demote Trusted Types (three innerHTML sinks, all sanitized), drop SRI, and move attachment ACL work to the attachments feature.

**Dependencies and risks.** connect-src and img-src are deliberately broad for Nexi, telemetry and web-search thumbnails, so narrowing needs a host inventory; link rules matter more once human posts render through ChatMarkdown ([F-t1-032](#F-t1-032)).

<a id="F-t1-031"></a>
### F-t1-031 · Org audit trail for admins: role-gated view, Space decisions and agent invocations, SIEM export, usage ledger export

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Control Plane · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Expose audit-core to org admins and auditors through a role-gated, cursor-paginated API and view with an audited export token, record Space decisions and agent invocations as metadata, and add Space and agent attribution to the usage ledger. Enterprises adopting an agent workspace must answer which agent did what on whose authority.

**Reference practice.** SIEM-grade audit ships in 2 of 5 references and spend export in 1 of 5. Slack's audit log streams JSON events (actor, action, entity, context) to an org-admin token, never logs message bodies and audits each export [DOC]. QM keeps an append-only table with a unique idempotency key, exactly-once recordOnce, audited admin reads, and a spend ledger by day, scope, origin and model with a bounded export [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** audit-core already ingests JetStream events from several planes and serves GET /v1/audit and /v1/usage with org, time, event and user_id filters, limit default 100 and no cursor (apps/Control Plane/audit-core/internal/api/api.go:106-116,197-222). The access defect is confirmed by trace: the gateway list_audit needs only a session (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/audit.rs:28-106) and audit-core verifies the delegation binds the org but never consults the delegated role (audit-core/internal/api/service_auth.go:203-213), so any org member can read all org events. Space decision issuance emits nothing, and decisions are stateless signed tokens (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/decision.go:1-40) [SRC]. Ship the access fix first as an S security item: the gateway requires admin or owner for org-wide reads, audit-core honours claims.UserRole and forces user_id to the caller for non-admins, and the settings panel is gated. Then add cursor pagination, filters and the export token. Emit denials and effect-bound decisions from user-core through its durable audit outbox, reuse session-core audit events for agent invocations, and add space_ref and agent_ref to cost-core entries through the producer.

**Dependencies and risks.** An export is a mass-exfiltration path and must itself be audited and bounded; details must never hold content or the decision token; retention is a single global value (see [F-t1-051](#F-t1-051)).

<a id="F-t1-032"></a>
### F-t1-032 · Composer for real work: rich text, persistent drafts, queue and steer while a turn runs, attachments and paste chips, scheduled send, and ref-based context chips with a pre-send preview

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Frontend Plane · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** The composer is where people and agents meet. Keep drafts across navigation and failed sends, render human posts with the same Markdown as agent output, wire the existing queue and steer backend into the room, and add attachments and long-paste chips. Scheduled send and "Ask the agent about this" context chips follow as separate items.

**Reference practice.** A rich composer and drafts ship in 4 of 5 references; queue and steer in 1 of 5. Grok Bot uses a restricted-schema editor serialised to markdown with a send journal and typed delivery states [REA]. Buzz keys drafts by room and thread with a revision tombstone, and its context hand-off builds a capped, JSON-quoted block with a treat-as-data notice and a byte-identical preview before sending [SRC]. QM shows editable queue chips while a run streams and promotes one to a steer through a durable run signal [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The Space composer is a two-row textarea disabled while a turn streams and cleared before the send starts, so a failed send loses the draft (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:147,376-402,745-746); human posts render as plain text (SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:329-336). The queue and steer backend is already Space-aware and re-mints a content-bound Control append decision (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/json_handlers.rs:176-205), but the composer imports only streamChat and cancelInvocation. Home already has a ZDR-gated draft hook and paste-to-attachment chips (src/features/dashboard/home/home-draft.ts:1-60) [SRC]. Drop the Tiptap editor in favour of a markdown textarea with a small toolbar and preview. Reuse useHomeDraft instead of building tombstones, wire queueInvocationInput into the composer, restore the draft on failure, render human posts through ChatMarkdown, and extract Home's paste chips and attachment preparation. Attachments need a new Space-bound Data Plane upload path, since chat upload is user-scoped (gateway/src/domains/chat/documents.rs:54). Scheduled send belongs to capability-core cron_schedules and is deferred.

**Dependencies and risks.** Steer by a non-owner needs [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001); every send needs a fresh content-bound append decision; link rules from [F-t1-030](#F-t1-030) should land before human markdown rendering.

<a id="F-t1-033"></a>
### F-t1-033 · SPA realtime client layer: client-supplied idempotency key, send states with in-place optimistic row, normalized store with version guards, offline vs service-down banner

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane, Frontend Plane · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Make each send carry a client idempotency key reused on retry, dedupe it end to end so a retry after a timeout cannot create a second append and a second billed AI turn, expose queued, sending, accepted and failed states, and tell offline from service-down with a banner and last-fresh time.

**Reference practice.** Idempotent sends ship in 3 of 5 references and connectivity awareness in 3 of 5. Grok Bot keeps a send journal with stages queued, prepared, dispatching and accepted-awaiting-echo, and marks accepted only when the echo with the same id arrives [REA]. Buzz inserts content-addressed event ids with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING and swaps an optimistic row in place [SRC]. Slack distinguishes online, service-down and offline through an unauthenticated probe [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** The gateway already honours a client idempotency_key and generates one when absent (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:120-195), and Control binds it into the decision digest, but the composer never sends one and nothing dedupes: Control decisions are stateless tokens, and Session Core append_message_in_tx assigns a fresh ULID and derives the event hash from message id and sequence (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:2598-2640). The composer posts through the SSE chat stream with one in-flight exchange and does not use the existing resume route; the timeline is a 6 s poll with an ordering guard (src/features/spaces/lib/space-live-work.ts:1-145). An unauthenticated /health route exists (gateway/src/main.rs:91) [SRC]. Re-scope into three slices: idempotency end to end, where Session Core derives a namespaced dedupe hash (org, thread, author, key plus content digest) for append and run start, and the client reconnects with resumeStream when it already holds a request id; a connectivity banner (S); and descoping the normalized store until push lands.

**Dependencies and risks.** The dedupe must live in Session Core, so the Model Plane is an owner; the events unique index is per org, so a raw key would collide; coordinate send states with [F-t1-032](#F-t1-032) and echo confirmation with [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003).

<a id="F-t1-034"></a>
### F-t1-034 · Scalable room timeline: server-computed windows with exact cursors and reply summaries, virtualised rows with scroll anchoring, jump-to-latest and history paging

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane, Frontend Plane · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add a Session Core query and gateway route that returns windows of top-level threads with turn summaries and a keyset cursor, and give the room a tested anchoring policy, jump-to-latest and history paging. Once push and streaming land, an unwindowed list that re-reads every 6 s will jump under the reader.

**Reference practice.** Server timeline windows appear in 1 of 5 references and virtualised anchoring in 2 of 5. Buzz keeps thread metadata (reply count, last reply, participants) at write time, pages with a composite cursor and a limit+1 probe, and ships a written, tested anchoring policy: hold the anchor on prepend, auto-scroll on append only while pinned, otherwise show jump-to-latest [SRC]. Grok Bot animates rows that shift under late content with an epoch-keyed layout cache [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** No cursor, windowing or summaries exist on the path: the gateway threads route takes only limit, default 80 and clamped to 200 (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:1157), ListThreadsRequest has no cursor (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sessions.proto:958-985), so posts beyond the newest 80 are unreachable in the room, and normalizeTranscriptTurns keeps only the first 30 turns of a post (src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:50), silently dropping the newest. The sort key is derived at read time through lateral joins (session-core/src/grpc.rs:5503-5596), and the N+1 transcript fetch is real only on first load [SRC]. Ship the client slice first (S): page the transcript from the newest turn backwards, add jump-to-latest and pinned-to-bottom anchoring on poll using the chat controller precedent and a pure policy module, keep aria-setsize and posinset, and try content-visibility before adding a virtual-list dependency. Then the server slice (M): a persisted last_activity_at plus turn_count, last_turn_at and participant ids maintained at append time, a before/limit+1 query in Session Core, a gateway route, and lazy transcript fetch per visible post.

**Dependencies and risks.** Cursors carry no authority, so every page re-runs the Control shared-read decision and summaries must be filtered per reader; drop the dependency on [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) server seq unless it ships first.

<a id="F-t1-035"></a>
### F-t1-035 · Attention outside the open tab: installable PWA, content-free Web Push with service-worker Open/Reply, in-tab toasts, tab-title and app badge

*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: XL · Plane: Frontend Plane, Application Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** An agent that finishes or needs approval while the tab is closed cannot reach anyone today. Add a tab-title count, favicon dot and app badge, an in-tab toast, a web app manifest and service worker, and later content-free Web Push whose payload is only an opaque wake and a notification id.

**Reference practice.** Unread badge or tray state ships in 3 of 5 references and push with native toasts in 4 of 5; an installable Progressive Web App (PWA) in 2 of 5. Buzz's device registers an expiring, installation-scoped lease; a blind gateway sends a constant payload and the client fetches pending items through the authenticated API, so neither provider nor gateway sees content [SRC]. Grok Bot computes the badge from the same roster as the sidebar and rejects stale snapshots by epoch and sequence so the count never moves backwards [REA]. Slack toasts carry an inline reply and action buttons and log only non-content fields [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** index.html links only an icon and the static title "verevonv3", public/ has no manifest or service worker, and no Notification, Push or setAppBadge use exists outside the demo guard; the only cues are navbar unread counts and sidebar dots (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/CoreNavbar.tsx:86-90). notification-core has no web-push path: push is Novu vocabulary behind a disabled adapter (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/runtime/client.go:201-219), every ZDR delivery is marked failed (delivery_worker.go:70-96), and no consumer covers Space posts, run completion or approvals. The Watch primitive stops at "recorded" (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/watch/watch.go:6-9) [SRC]. Slice A (S, Frontend only): title count, favicon dot, setAppBadge and an in-tab toast fed from the space-live-work unread projection, honouring per-room mute. Slice B (M): manifest, icons, install affordance and a shell-only service worker with its own no-cache nginx location, since nginx.conf:103-114 gives every .js file a one-year immutable header. Slice C (L): Web Push, after notification-core gains Space, run and approval producers and the S4.4 delivery ledger. Prompt for browser permission from an explicit setting, never on load.

**Dependencies and risks.** Push depends on [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008), [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009), [F-t1-024](#F-t1-024) and [F-t1-060](#F-t1-060); the ZDR refusal needs a Control-decided carve-out for the content-free wake class; approvals are never decided from a toast.

<a id="F-t1-036"></a>
### F-t1-036 · Room automations: create schedules, watches, calendar and event triggers from the room, with fire outcomes, recipient consent, goals and run traces

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Control Plane, Model Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** The backend already re-authorizes every schedule fire through Control; finish the product around it. Let members create, pause and test-run schedules from the Work tab, show a fire log with an outcome vocabulary, store recipient acceptance, expose watches so process output wakes the room, and add a calendar-event trigger beside cron. Scheduled and event-triggered agent work is table stakes among the agent references.

**Reference practice.** Scheduled or event-triggered work ships in 4 of 5 references; per-fire reauthorization and consent in 2 of 5. QM derives a deterministic fire key per schedule and slot, opens a thread per fire with outcomes running, ok, silent, deferred, failed or refused, runs as owner, scope floor or shared scope with membership skip notes at fire time, and stores recipient acceptance [SRC]. Grok Bot pauses routines whose output is unread and asks on return, and uses the calendar as an agent trigger source with approval-gated scheduling [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** The Work tab lists a room's schedules and fired runs (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceWorkPanel.tsx:171-189) and capability-core's Sweeper reauthorizes every slot with a fire key and idempotency key (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/cron/sweeper.go:148-160). Creation exists only in Settings and only for the caller's personal Space, because Control's schedule create, fire, run and step decisions and the watch decisions all call validatePersonalAuthority, and ZDR is refused outright (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/schedule_create_decision.go:62-97; schedule_fire_decision.go:84-96). cron_fires holds only pending or failed, watch routes require a human caller with process output as the only source, and calendar.events is read-only with live proof pending (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/actions/calendar_read.go:1-70) [SRC]. Lead with a Control slice: shared-audience variants of schedule create, fire, run and step, then watch create and observe. Then ship independently: a fire-log and outcome surface plus pause and resume from the Work tab (S-M); room-bound create with stored recipient acceptance; Watch routes through the gateway once S4.4 delivery exists; a calendar poller trigger and approval-gated calendar.create_event after live proof and the extra OAuth scope. Goals, loops and reviewer reports move to a later wave.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) turn_origin, [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027) caps for unattended runs and [F-t1-047](#F-t1-047) for webhooks; ZDR rooms must hide or refuse automation with a typed reason; a calendar trigger runs under one member's provider connection, which the room must name.

<a id="F-t1-037"></a>
### F-t1-037 · Space tool cockpit and reliable tool connections: per-room MCP enablement, per-binding allowlist, credential mode and scoped consent, health states and person-bound sign-in links

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (registry and runtime) with Control, Ingestion and Frontend · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Let a Space manager enable org-approved MCP (Model Context Protocol) servers for one room, with a per-binding tool allowlist, a per-user or shared credential choice and a consent card bound to the exact Space, agent and tool. Make tool use fail visibly and recover with per-server health, backoff, call budgets and a room-visible "needs sign-in" card.

**Reference practice.** An MCP registry is table stakes among the agent references (3 of 5); consent links for connected apps appear in 2 of 5. QM's server record carries credentialScope shared or per_user, a toolAllowlist and a readOnly flag, and a consent link completes only for the signed-in principal [SRC]. Grok Bot probes health every 10 s, bounds calls at 2 min idle and 60 min total, and returns a typed error union with needs_auth and retry_after hints [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** The registry, OAuth 2.1 refresh, three-attempt retry and argument validation exist (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:327-343, tool_retry.rs:1-60), but tool exposure is resolved per org and requesting user with no Space input (runtime_registries.rs:715-765), so a room turn is offered that member's private and shared tools. A per-binding allowed_tools list is stored, enforced and displayed (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4500-4570) but nothing writes it. Slice 1 (M): default-deny MCP tools in Space turns, enable-per-room as a human-only Control decision, and a write path for allowed_tools. Then add Retry-After, per-server state and idle versus total budgets to the existing retry and refresh. Consent cards and person-bound links follow once cards exist; the connect-session callback completes by state, so a principal check must be added.

**Dependencies and risks.** S3.4 broker and S3.2 sandbox lease; a shared credential lets any member act as its owner, so default read-only with a warning; never widen a private server by Space linkage.

<a id="F-t1-038"></a>
### F-t1-038 · Agents and AI answers over room history under the asker's authority: search_space_history and open_space_entry tools, cited answers over messages and Space knowledge

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (tools, index, answer composition) with Control Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Give room agents search_space_history and open_space_entry tools over a Session Core message index, run under the authority of the human who triggered the turn, then cited answers over messages and documents and an "Ask agent about this" action. Agents today cannot read room history beyond the current transcript.

**Reference practice.** A history tool appears in 3 of 5 references, delegated-authority search and cited answers in 2 of 5 each. QM's sessions.search returns session and entry references without widening read access, and the history tool reopens one entry by sequence even after compaction [SRC]. Slack mints an action token from the triggering event and limits results to what the user can access; this rests on public docs [DOC]. Grok Bot adds scopes and a rule to trust the newest result [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** Grounded document retrieval is shipped: knowledge_search maps to cap.retrieval.query (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/capability_policy.rs:780-800) and the gateway carries a Control retrieval decision in space_context (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:215-300). Messages are not covered: Session Core scans with no full-text index (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:5503-5596) and the gateway search routes cover only web, similar, suggest, images, videos, suggestions and answer, none over Space messages (search.rs:39-45). Stage it: (a) "Ask agent about this" as a composer action that quotes the selected turn into a reply plus mention (S); (b) a tsvector index and SearchSpaceMessages RPC filtered by the audience ceiling; (c) the two tools in capability_policy.rs, scoped to the current Space, using a model.thread.read decision added to space_context; (d) cited answers last. Current-Space scope makes every hit readable by all members, which removes the private-quote problem.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018) index and deep links; ZDR (Zero-Data-Retention) threads have nothing to index; retrieved messages are untrusted input.

<a id="F-t1-039"></a>
### F-t1-039 · Entry references and permalinks: one entity router for message, thread and run links, deep links honoured, Space-role pins, visibility-preserving fork, quote and forward

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Frontend Plane (route table) with Model Plane (entry refs, fork) and Application Plane (pins) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Define one reference to a room entry (space_ref, thread_id, seq) resolved server-side under the viewer's audience revision, and one route table that notifications, push, agent-posted links and share handlers all use. Build Space-role pins, a fork that copies only what the forking viewer can see, quoted replies with tombstones, and forward last.

**Reference practice.** Deep links are table stakes (4 of 5); pins appear in 3 of 5; fork, quote and forward in 2 of 5 each. Teams normalises toast clicks, deep links and lifecycle events into one (entity, action) command and fails closed on membership [REA]. QM pages older history until the target sequence loads, then highlights it, and records forkedFrom and forkBoundarySeq on fork [SRC]. Buzz renders known hosts as entity chips [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Activity rows already emit /spaces/{id}?thread_id=...#chat (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/activity-grammar.ts:186-212) but only the hash tab is read (components/SpaceCockpit.tsx:120-125) and App.tsx registers just /spaces/:spaceId (src/app/App.tsx:181-182). Pin is an author-only thread flag (apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:2183-2310) and the timeline sorts by time only (SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:137-141). Every message carries a durable message_id and audience snapshot (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/migrations/0025_message_space_audience_context.sql:1-30) that the Space client drops (src/shared/api/spaces-client.ts:606-660). Slice by cost: make the existing links scroll and highlight, validated like chat-thread-deep-link.ts (S); pass message_id through and add /spaces/:id/m/:entry (S to M); Space-role pins in an Application table with timeline lifting (M); fork as a Session Core RPC denied for ZDR (L); quote with a server-resolved tombstone (M). Demote forward.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) shared threads, [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035) typed notification refs; reveal no titles to non-members; an agent pin tool first needs a model-eligibility.ts entry.

<a id="F-t1-040"></a>
### F-t1-040 · Per-thread serialized, leased agent run queue and member-safe mid-run steering for multi-member rooms

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Several people can post to one thread while an agent turn runs. Characterise what happens today, add a per-thread active-run guard with a visible "agent is busy" state, and let members steer a running turn at the next tool-round boundary instead of being locked out.

**Reference practice.** A durable per-conversation run queue appears in 2 of 5 references, mid-run steering in 1 of 5. QM allows one running run per session via a unique partial index, claims with SKIP LOCKED, heartbeats the lease, parks a run after repeated crashes, and classifies an incoming message as engage, steer or drop with steers read at step boundaries [SRC]. Grok Bot uses a Temporal-based durable harness instead [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** Steering is already built and Space-aware except in the room composer: model-gateway queues input under a fresh Control append decision (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:7397-7540), the BFF route mints it (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/json_handlers.rs:164-215), but SpaceRoomComposer only refuses to send while submitting (src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:372-378). The registry is in-memory, single-replica and bound to the requesting user (queued_input.rs:28-32). No per-thread gate exists: start_run_inner inserts with ON CONFLICT (id) only (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:3071-3140). Reduce to: (a) a two-member concurrency test, including whether the assistant-reply slot rule (grpc.rs:2487-2490) refuses a second reply; (b) a per-thread guard at run start or a gateway 409 plus a queued-turn state; (c) wire the composer to the existing queue route, then member steering by explicit policy with [F-t1-032](#F-t1-032); (d) reuse managed-run recovery as the reaper. Drop the tool ledger: interactive runs cannot restart.

**Dependencies and risks.** ZDR Spaces cannot steer (queue route refuses ZDR tokens); member steering must not widen any owner-plane ACL; multi-replica steering needs request-id routing over NATS.

<a id="F-t1-041"></a>
### F-t1-041 · One command layer on the typed action registry: Cmd/Ctrl+K palette and quick switcher, platform slash commands, shortcut sheet, then a unified 'To:' picker

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Frontend Plane · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Wrap the typed ActionDescriptor registry and navigation commands in one command registry with hotkeys, one key handler, Ctrl/Cmd+1-9 Space switching and a shortcut sheet. Rebuild Cmd/Ctrl+K as a palette over rooms, threads, agents, people and actions, and add platform slash commands beside skills. A registry shared by human clicks and model-initiated actions is a base no reference has.

**Reference practice.** A command registry with hotkeys is table stakes (4 of 5); slash commands 3 of 5; a quick switcher is recorded in 2 of 5 and under-counted. Grok Bot registers every shell action as a command with id and hotkey consumed by one hotkey layer and a mod+K palette, and reveals numeric shortcuts on a held modifier [REA]. QM uses the same registry shape [SRC]. Slack defines the keyboard model and the ephemeral versus in-channel slash contract [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** The Cmd/Ctrl+K overlay is a knowledge search plus local chat-history filter, not web search (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/CoreNavbar.tsx:122-132), with no focus trap, arrow keys or Enter handling (docs/UI_UX_AUDIT_2026-09-29.md:71). The room "/" opens a skill picker only (src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:213-275), and the gateway accepts only personal and room Spaces (apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:2871-2873), so a "To:" picker has nothing to find. Ship the switcher (rooms, loaded threads, agents, roster people) and the keyboard model first, keeping knowledge and local history as palette providers. ActionDescriptor has no hotkey or when field, so add them in a thin wrapper; run actions only through ActionCommand preview and the gateway dispatcher, where input is derivable or after a schema-driven prompt. Split the "To:" picker into its own XL item blocked on a DM kind and people directory.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-055](#F-t1-055) org switcher keys, [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) cross-room lists; requiresApproval actions never run from the palette; recent items obey isLocalRetentionAllowed under ZDR.

<a id="F-t1-042"></a>
### F-t1-042 · Read state, presence and personal follow-ups: per-thread read frontier, mark unread, global presence and agent availability, saved items, reminders and snooze

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Application Plane (convex-core) with Model Plane (run-state feed) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add mark-unread, a first-unread divider and jump-to-unread to the room read marker; a global status with text and expiry plus agent availability; and refs-only saved items and reminders that fire into the Inbox. Per-thread frontiers and receipts wait for shared threads.

**Reference practice.** Read state is table stakes (4 of 5), presence 3 of 5, reminders 3 of 5. Buzz keeps a server-side read frontier with manual-unread overrides and encrypts a reminder's target and note to its author so the server schedules on not_before alone [SRC]. Teams marks read optimistically, has the richest availability model with DND suppressing notifications, and offers shared room reminders [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** A durable, cross-device room-level marker ships (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceReadMarkers.ts:1-30), designed per room on purpose, and advanceMarker never moves backwards (spaceReadMarkers.ts:78-84); there is no mark-unread, divider or receipts. Presence is room-scoped online and typing with a 30 s TTL (convex/spacePresence.ts:1-60); the only global status is a user-core profile availability value with no text or expiry (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/http/handlers.go:225-262) that no Space surface reads; agent "working" is inferred in the browser (src/features/spaces/lib/space-live-work.ts:100-140). Reminders exist only as a 24 h ticket snooze (src/features/inbox/components/InboxPage.tsx:1057-1073). Three slices: (1) a mark-unread mutation plus divider and jump from the SpacePage arrival snapshot (S to M); (2) extend the availability value with text and expiry and project agent working or waiting from a Model Plane feed, not a cross-plane read; (3) refs-only reminders fired by the Convex scheduler into the Inbox (M). Drop priority re-notify; notification-core has no such concept.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) before per-thread frontiers, [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) Inbox Reminders slice; rows stay content-free; global status must not leak across organisations; reminder targets are rechecked at fire time.

<a id="F-t1-043"></a>
### F-t1-043 · Room moderation: report, queue, tombstones, user block, and timeout or ban that also stops the person's agents

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Application Plane (reports, sanctions) with Control Plane (suspension) and Model Plane (tombstone write) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add Report on every room message, a private queue for owners and managers, signed actions (dismiss, delete with a visible tombstone, timeout, remove, ban) and a per-user block list. A ban must also stop the person's agents, or it becomes a bypass. Without this, Spaces cannot open to larger teams or guests.

**Reference practice.** A moderation workflow appears in 2 of 5 references, a ban cascade to owned agents in 1 of 5. Buzz keeps a reports table with the reporter hidden from the author, writes a room-visible tombstone, filters delivery through a block list, and checks the ban verdict at authentication for the principal and its owner, failing closed [SRC]. Teams has the best end-user side: report a concern, block, chat-request expiry [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** Only coarse removal exists: remove_space_member (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:3433-3546) reaches Control, which publishes membership_changed (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/nats/shared_publisher.go:196-215), and session-core records the revocation (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/migrations/0032_space_membership_revocations.sql:1-20); no in-flight run is cancelled. space_memberships has no suspended_until (apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/017_space_authority.up.sql:53-64) and agents carry no owner link in Control. Room posts are Session Core threads with owner-bound presentation writes, so the tombstone cannot live in convex-core: put the hide and tombstone write in Session Core behind a Space-authorised moderation decision, keep Application records content-free (refs and hash), and implement timeout and ban as Control membership state on the membership_changed path made durable (today plain NATS that tolerates missed events). Every post today is an AI turn, so the first reports target agent output and prompts.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022) room roles, [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011) agent ownership, [F-t1-031](#F-t1-031) audit; removal must propagate to Data Plane indexes and notification payloads; a fail-closed ban check must alert rather than silently lock people out.

<a id="F-t1-044"></a>
### F-t1-044 · Credential custody and outward actions: secrets broker, per-person connectors, draft-first sends, bounded cross-room reach

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Ingestion Plane (broker) with Control (grants), Application (drafts) and Frontend · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Let room agents use credentials without seeing them: a broker that attaches secrets server-side with host, method and path pinning, a one-time secret-drop form that bypasses chat and the model, and Control grants (once, session, standing) bound to the Space audience. Add per-person connector accounts, draft-first outbound messages where the human stays the sender, and bounded cross-room posts.

**Reference practice.** Secrets custody and room-agent connectors each appear in 3 of 5 references, draft-first sends in 2 of 5, cross-room reach in 1 of 5. QM's broker checks capability claims, host, method and path prefix, uses manual redirects, caps responses at 5 MB and writes a receipt; secret drop is a one-time form with 7-day expiry; reach is capped at 5 cross-conversation posts per turn [SRC]. Grok Bot ships plugins with per-person OAuth accounts and draft cards with always, needs-approval or disabled settings [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** Absent: the broker, secret drop and Space-bound grants (session-core migration 0028 adds a credential_grant_ref column no code reads), per-room connector bindings (bindings carry only allowed_tools, trigger_modes and approval_mode; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4483-4580), and cross-Space posts (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:2495 refuses them). Already shipped: the human-only versus model-executable classification with a pinned test (src/shared/actions/catalog-manifest.test.ts:23-49), the personal-connection owner rule (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/api/connection_access.go:22-73), the human_approved_ai_action attestation (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/integration_tools.rs:347-430) and the Inbox draft and outbound-intent flow. Split into four: A broker, secret drop and grants in integration-corev2 on egress.SafeClient (L); B connector cockpit and bindings (M); C draft-first sends on conversation-core ai actions, not a new Draft object (M); D cross-room posts under a Control decision for the target Space (M). Drop the route-classification sub-item.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-028](#F-t1-028) egress boundary, [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) grant records; recheck grants at use and every scheduled fire; durable decisions refuse ZDR, so standing grants need an explicit rule; only three action kinds resume after approval.

<a id="F-t1-045"></a>
### F-t1-045 · Agent runtime guardrails for shared rooms: member-text screening, shell command policy, room approvals, secret masking, DLP

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane (execution-core, model-gateway, capability-core) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** In a multi-human room, colleagues' messages are untrusted input to someone's personal agent, and agent output posted to the room can leak credentials and personal data. Extend the existing screening and redaction to both. Shell parsing, browser approvals in rooms and value-based credential masking follow once rooms can reach those capabilities.

**Reference practice.** Secret and PII (personally identifiable information) redaction is table stakes (4 of 5); command policy 2 of 5; provenance-tagged screening and inline DLP (data loss prevention) 1 of 5 each. QM tags every chunk with provenance, screens external chunks under a deadline, parses commands with tree-sitter-bash against an org floor returning allow, ask or deny with a reason code, and masks injected secrets in raw, URL-encoded and base64 forms [SRC]. Grok Bot pairs a shell AST with typed block reasons [REA]. Teams offers inline DLP with report or override [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** TrustClass is a function of the tool name with no class for room-member text (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/provenance.rs:20-58); shell is gated by a tool-name keyword with no parser (execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:195-262); pii_filter redacts provider-bound input, not agent posts (model-gateway/src/moderation.rs:570-605); scrub.rs is pattern-based (execution-core/src/scrub.rs:40-70). The room composer never requests agentic or browser features and the sandbox is credential_free with egress disabled (execution-core/src/sandbox.rs:355-368), so there is nothing to mask yet. Ship now: (a) a RoomMember trust class from author subject versus invoking subject at prompt assembly, since Session Core records each turn's author (session-core/src/grpc.rs:2386-2395); (e) pii_filter and masking on agent replies in the model-gateway output path or Session Core append, not an Application path. Hold (b), (c), (d) until S3.2, S3.4 and room agentic access land.

**Dependencies and risks.** S2.7 provenance envelope, [F-t1-005](#F-t1-005) typed refusals; false positives on colleagues' text; stateless screening under ZDR; room approvals stay bound to the initiating human.

<a id="F-t1-046"></a>
### F-t1-046 · Files become agent input and room knowledge: per-turn re-authorized attachments as model input, extraction with summaries and typed refusals, and agent-proposed pinned knowledge

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (prompt builder, routing) with Data Plane v2 (extraction, proposals) and Control (import contract) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Make an uploaded file usable by a room agent: extract and index it into the Space retrieval binding with typed refusals, then pass attachments to providers per turn after re-authorising each against the invoking member's current ACL, storing references only. Later let an agent propose a summary that a human approves before it is pinned.

**Reference practice.** Files as agent input and as room knowledge each appear in 2 of 5 references; crystallisation in 1 of 5, planned only. QM lists attachments newest-first under caps (10 documents, 8 MB), authorises each against the current ACL, dedupes by sha256, extracts with heap and time caps, picks native file input or text per provider, emits truncation notices and stores only references [SRC]. Grok Bot stages uploads with background captions and deletes on discard [REA]. Slack's auto-summary rests on public docs [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The room composer has no attach control (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx); Chat's ephemeral extract and durable document routes are not wired to Spaces (apps/gateway/src/domains/chat/documents.rs:1-60). Room agents read only through the retrieval binding (apps/Data Plane v2/infra/postgres/migrations/20260813130000_space_retrieval_bindings.sql:1-30), and Control's import decision is personal-Space only (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/personal_thread_decision.go:261-272), so no durable room upload can be authorised today. wiki-store SubmitProposal and ReviewProposal exist (apps/Data Plane v2/services/wiki-store-go/internal/grpcserver/server.go:343-380) but no room offers them. Sequence: first a Control shared-import contract plus a Data Plane upload into the retrieval binding, so files become knowledge agents can already read; then per-turn native attachment input; then propose-and-review pinning on wiki proposals. Do not start with per-turn references in session-core.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013) owns the composer attach pipeline (confirm to avoid double counting), [F-t1-015](#F-t1-015) canvas; ZDR refuses the attachments field (model-gateway/src/zdr_features.rs:286-298) and durable import, so ZDR rooms need the ephemeral route or a typed refusal; attachment text is untrusted.

<a id="F-t1-047"></a>
### F-t1-047 · Webhooks that wake room agents (Space-bound inbound endpoints)

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane (receive, verify) with Model Plane capability-core (trigger) and Control (fire decision) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Let a Space manager create a secret webhook endpoint bound to a room and an agent, so a pull request, a payment dispute or an alert starts work in the room. Ingestion verifies, dedups, filters and caps the delivery; a Control-decided unattended turn consumes the payload as untrusted data. The same plumbing unblocks per-agent email ([F-t1-075](#F-t1-075)).

**Reference practice.** 2 of 5 references have it. QM's WebhookReceiver runs per-provider verifiers (GitHub, Slack, Stripe, Linear, generic HMAC) with constant-time compare, derives a delivery id for dedup, filters by JSON path, caps the payload at 16,000 characters in a wake envelope wrapped as untrusted content, and dispatches it like a cron fire under the endpoint owner's authority; the secret is shown once [SRC]. Buzz adds outbound calls with SSRF protection [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** integration-corev2 already receives org-level provider webhooks with signature checks for a fixed catalogue, dedup, storage and a NATS reference publish (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/api/server.go:981-1100, 2408); conversation-core's inbox is the only subscriber. Nothing is Space-bound, and the wake path is not ready: Control's schedule-fire decision requires a personal Space and refuses ZDR (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/schedule_fire_decision.go:83-101), the scheduled step is one tool-free 1024-token call (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/grpc.rs:498-528), and the gateway reports watches_not_built (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:1622-1627). Model the endpoint as a webhook source kind of the S4.3 watch primitive (Space and member authority, bounded predicate, cursor, unscreened trust level) rather than a new trigger beside schedules, keeping an endpoint-id route family in integration-corev2 with one-time secrets. Add the Control shared-room fire decision first. Ship notify-and-summarise through the tool-free lane and gate tool use on S4.1 effects closing. Not a quick win.

**Dependencies and risks.** Control shared-room fire decision (critical path), S4.4 delivery; payloads are attacker-controlled and never get tool access in the same turn; ZDR rooms drop payloads; at-least-once delivery needs idempotent fire keys.

<a id="F-t1-048"></a>
### F-t1-048 · Room visibility, directory and self-join (org-public or private), with a late-joiner history rule

*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: L · Plane: Control Plane (visibility, join, tenure), Model Plane (read filter), Frontend Plane (directory) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give named rooms an open or private visibility, a paged directory of org-public rooms the caller is not in, and a self-join that grants exactly room membership with the default role. Record join and leave sequence per membership so history can be limited to a tenure window, and make membership-change delivery durable. Browse and join is how people find work in Slack and Teams; today a person sees only rooms someone added them to.

**Reference practice.** Discovery and self-join ship in 3 of 5 references (Buzz, Teams, QM); a tenure window ships in 1 of 5 (QM). Buzz lists open channels in a directory and self-adds membership only for open rooms, while private rooms return not-found to non-members [SRC]. QM stores a participant window (valid-from and valid-to sequence) on membership rows and filters transcript reads by it, denying when membership is unknown [SRC]. Slack's public and private channels rest on public docs only [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** registered_spaces has no visibility column, only a privacy_revision counter, and space_memberships carries no join sequence (apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/017_space_authority.up.sql:10-66). The gateway has no browse or join route (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:649-735), and Session Core applies only an audience-revision ceiling to shared-thread reads, never a floor (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:5438-5465) [SRC]. membership_changed already goes out over JetStream; the non-durable part is the plain-subscribe consumer and the missing publisher outbox. Decide the membership writer first, because the Application declarative sync (ReplaceMemberships) would revoke a Control-native self-join at the next sync. Keep rooms private by default with org-public opt-in, default open rooms to full history, and split the item: visibility, directory and join (M), then tenure window and durable delivery (L).

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs the default join role from [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022); the strict tenure floor belongs with guests in [F-t1-053](#F-t1-053). Directory names must respect residency and Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR), and self-join must never widen an owner-plane ACL.

<a id="F-t1-049"></a>
### F-t1-049 · Searchable people-and-agent directory with an agent-readable resolver

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Control Plane (people), Application Plane (agents), Frontend Plane gateway (composition) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add a directory search over org members and registered agents that returns only subjects the caller may see, expose it to the add-member picker and later DM pickers, and give agents a resolve tool that answers ok, ambiguous (with candidates) or none (with a reason). Without it people cannot find each other past a flat list, and agents cannot address the right human.

**Reference practice.** All 5 of 5 references have a searchable directory. Teams resolves org directory, saved contacts and external users in one picker, finds externals only by exact email to prevent enumeration, and ranks by recent interaction [REA]. QM adds resolver vocabulary for agents: ok, ambiguous, none, not-in-channel and unlinked login [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** org-core already serves a member search by name or email substring (apps/Control Plane/org-core/internal/http/handlers.go:955-1060), but the gateway never proxies it and the add-member picker lists every active member with no search box (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceMemberControls.tsx:86-135). An org-wide agent registry exists in the Application Plane (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceAgents.ts:477-512), but nothing merges people and agents and no agent-callable resolver exists [SRC]. Slice 1 (S): expose the org-core search through a gateway route that forces the session org and filters to active members, because the raw handler also returns invited emails. Slice 2 (M): compose people and agents in the gateway and expose the resolver as an agent tool with presence-not-authority semantics, since the registry answers who exists, never who may be invoked.

**Dependencies and risks.** The agent half is Application-owned under ADR-0002, so Control must not own the combined directory. External and guest lookup waits for [F-t1-053](#F-t1-053); DM and group-room pickers have no consumer until those Space kinds exist.

<a id="F-t1-050"></a>
### F-t1-050 · Meeting intelligence with consent: long-form transcription and an authority-scoped recap saved as a Space document, under a deny-by-default per-meeting AI policy

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane (ASR, recap), Data Plane v2 (transcript units), Control Plane (meeting AI policy) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Transcribe uploaded recordings, then live calls, with speaker labels and timestamps; store segments as knowledge units rather than room posts; and write a recap (decisions, action items, chapters, citations) back as an editable Space document. Gate all of it with a Control-owned per-meeting policy (AI off, transcript only or full; privacy mode; ephemeral or retained answers; locked fields) that is checked before any automatic speech recognition (ASR) or agent reads audio.

**Reference practice.** Transcript and recap ship in 3 of 5 references (Buzz, Slack, Teams); a per-meeting AI policy in 2 of 5 (Teams, Slack). Teams produces notes, tasks, chapters and a speaker timeline with citations back to the recording, derives recap visibility from transcript access, and offers visible AI on/off, a privacy mode that blocks all agents, and admin-locked options rendered as locked [REA]. Slack announces to all when AI notes start and defaults off with external guests [DOC]. Grok Bot adds a consent card for agent attendance [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Only dictation-sized speech-to-text exists: a 25 MB cap and a ZDR gate in apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:3952-4060, a 60 s take in the frontend, and no diarisation code anywhere. There is no meeting object, recap document or AI policy, and Control import decisions require a personal Space (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/personal_thread_decision.go:111-128) [SRC]. Resequence: first the shared-room import decision and policy effects in Control; then an upload flow with a long-form ASR job extending the existing transcribe chain (self-hosted for ZDR tenants); then the recap as a Data Plane document with action items as approval cards; last the Meetings tab and policy chip. Split into an ASR and recap MVP (L) plus the policy and authority work.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs the meeting record [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029) and a manager role, since membership adds carry no role. Transcript units must carry the meeting audience and policy revision so retrieval cannot leak them; ZDR must fail closed until a speech deployment is attested.

<a id="F-t1-051"></a>
### F-t1-051 · Retention policies for rooms, files and agent sessions, plus shared-Space deletion on the existing saga

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Control Plane (policy), Application Plane (sweeper, shared deletion), Model Plane (thread purge) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add org and per-Space retention (keep, N days, ZDR) carried in Control's signed decision and enforced by per-plane sweepers that return the same honest receipts the deletion saga uses, then extend that saga from personal to shared Spaces with owner approval and write fencing. Retention is a gating enterprise question and a GDPR storage-limitation duty, and today there is nothing between keep-forever and ZDR.

**Reference practice.** Retention appears in 3 of 5 references (Slack, Teams, and Buzz in its docs); durable shared-space deletion in 1 of 5 (Buzz). Slack layers policy objects at org, workspace, channel and DM scope, most specific wins unless the org locks it, and a scheduled sweeper deletes across stores with audited policy changes [DOC]. Buzz runs deletion as request, frozen inventory digest, owner approval, write fence, resumable checkpointed executor and final receipt [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Control signs retention_class, residency and deletion_scope into every decision as opaque strings (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/authority.go:144-176), but no plane has a time-based sweeper; the only running one is audit-core's global purge (apps/Control Plane/audit-core/cmd/server/main.go:150-185). The deletion saga with per-owner receipts and legal hold is personal-Space only, and Session Core delete_space_threads is bound to one owner principal (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:6058-6130) [SRC]. Phase it: add a numeric TTL to space_effect_policies and one Session Core sweeper for threads and messages plus the existing memory expiry (L); generalise owner adapters to "purge before timestamp for this Space" under a scheduled-purge scope; shared-Space deletion last, with purge re-keyed from owner-plus-Space to Space id.

**Dependencies and risks.** Legal hold outranks any sweep and ZDR must never weaken. Application may only request purges, never delete another plane's data. Search and graph index purge and copies in notification payloads stay open; whether saga migrations are applied in staging was not verified.

<a id="F-t1-052"></a>
### F-t1-052 · Typed timeline entries: server-signed system messages, a card registry with a safe fallback, link previews through Quarry, and pointer embeds for artifacts

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (entry schema), Application Plane (system rows), Ingestion Plane (preview), Frontend Plane (card registry) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Give entries a content type and payload rendered through a card registry with a neutral fallback for unknown kinds, let the server emit typed system notices (member added, agent bound or paused, rename) that clients cannot forge, snapshot link previews at send through Quarry, and embed artifacts as pointers rendered under the viewer's own grant. Rooms then show what changed without a separate tab and never copy artifact content into messages.

**Reference practice.** Typed content and system messages each ship in 2 of 5 references, link previews in 3 of 5, pointer embeds in 1 of 5. Teams has 87+ typed system notices including agent added or removed [REA]; Buzz signs notices with the relay key and snapshots previews sender-side so recipients never contact the site [SRC]; Grok Bot renders unknown and retired preview types as fallback cards [SRC]; Teams Loop embeds carry their own ACL and warn when shared to a wider audience [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** AppendMessageRequest has role, content and an opaque metadata struct but no content_type (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sessions.proto:287-298); the timeline normaliser drops every role except user and assistant (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:21-67); the activity grammar has no membership class; Quarry has an SSRF guard and OpenGraph extraction but no metadata-only fetch mode [SRC]. Do not append notices into Session Core threads: the room is a list of thread-posts and each append needs a per-human Control decision. Emit notices as room-level feed rows from an Application projection of Control events, reserve content_type or an interim metadata.kind for in-thread cards, keep unknown kinds as a fallback, and add a light preview mode to Quarry.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs the append path [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) and canvas and lists ([F-t1-015](#F-t1-015), [F-t1-058](#F-t1-058)) for embeds. ZDR rooms disable snapshots; request-access stubs must not leak titles.

<a id="F-t1-053"></a>
### F-t1-053 · Guests and link invitations with mandatory expiry

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Control Plane (auth-core identity, user-core membership) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Introduce a guest principal with memberships only in explicitly granted rooms, no directory, roster or agent invocation by default, invited through room-scoped invitations that must expire. Add shareable links or codes with TTL, max uses, optional admin approval and stored terms acceptance. Today a customer or contractor cannot be pulled into one room at all.

**Reference practice.** Guests ship in 4 of 5 references; link invitations in 2 of 5. Teams tiers guests [REA]; QM makes guest expiry mandatory and removes memberships through the normal change path [SRC]; Buzz encodes org, room, role, expiry, max uses and a nonce in an HMAC-signed invite code and rate-limits claims to 10 per minute [SRC]; Slack distinguishes single- and multi-channel guests with an admin approval queue [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Only org-level email invitations exist, through the Better Auth organization plugin with a 48 h default expiry and a rate-limited accept route (apps/Control Plane/auth-core/src/auth/auth.ts:942-1013). space_memberships allows only user and service subjects (apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/017_space_authority.up.sql:51-66), room grants require an active org member and are hard-coded to editor, and ReplaceMemberships revokes any managed member not in the declared list (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/repository.go:693-775) [SRC]. Gate the build on two decisions: the guest identity model (separate principal versus restricted org role) and the cross-org identity and privacy contract the UI research doc requires. Reuse the Better Auth invitation for the email path with a room target, give guests a subject type excluded from the declarative sync, and ship link invites as a later slice.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs room roles [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022) and the tenure window from [F-t1-048](#F-t1-048); a guest must never become a plain org member or enter org-visible documents. Whether the Data Plane honours the room audience for org documents was not traced; billing seats were not verified.

<a id="F-t1-054"></a>
### F-t1-054 · Teams and sub-groups with source-tracked membership; mentionable groups

*Verevon today: Stub · Effort: L · Plane: Control Plane (auth-core team store, user-core expansion) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add a team concept (owners, members, visibility) whose grant expands into room memberships tagged with their source, so leaving a team removes only team-derived grants. Later, team mentions expanded server-side at send. Teams are the organising unit of the Teams half of the vision and the prerequisite for SCIM group sync and group-based agent access.

**Reference practice.** Teams ship in 2 of 5 references (Teams, QM); mentionable groups in 2 of 5 (Teams, Slack). Teams links team, team_members and team_rooms so joining a team grants its standard rooms while private rooms keep their own roster, and the server expands a group mention at send with per-recipient permission checks [REA]. QM includes a team layer in a turn only when the actor is on the team and the room is configured for it [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Dormant scaffolding exists: auth-core defines team and team_member tables and an invitation team_id that nothing reads (apps/Control Plane/auth-core/src/db/schema.ts:225-262), and resource_grants reserves a team subject in its CHECK while the ACL repository rejects non-user subjects (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/users/acl_repository.go:58-85). Memberships carry only a free-text granted_by that the Application full-replace sync overwrites (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/repository.go:693-775), and no Space mention delivery exists [SRC]. Start from the auth-core tables by enabling the Better Auth teams option, since Auth Core is the membership authority and org-core deliberately mounts no membership mutations. Decide the single writer of room membership first: carry a source per member in the Application declaration, or let Control own writes with a source column and scope the sync to source=manual. Use the reserved team subject for team-level sharing.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022) and [F-t1-048](#F-t1-048); group mentions wait for Space notifications. Team membership never widens an owner-plane ACL, and agents in a mentionable group must be opt-in because one broadcast can trigger many paid turns.

<a id="F-t1-055"></a>
### F-t1-055 · Personal sidebar organisation: stars, custom sections, per-group sort, hide, mute, drag-and-drop, computed system sections and cross-org badges

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Application Plane (preferences), Frontend Plane (sidebar) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give each user a synced sidebar preference document (stars, custom sections, per-group sort, hidden refs, mute) edited with drag-and-drop, plus computed system sections (Unread, Waiting for approval, Agent runs, Muted) over the same summaries, and later cross-org badges with keyboard switching. The need grows as soon as directory, DMs and team rooms add conversations per person.

**Reference practice.** Sidebar organisation ships in 4 of 5 references (Buzz, Grok Bot, QM, Slack); custom sections in 2 of 5 (Teams, Slack); computed system sections in 1 of 5 (Teams). Buzz stores a per-user blob of stars, sections, sort and hidden refs server-side, renders fixed groups then user sections, and drives a "More unread" pill from rows outside the viewport [SRC]. Teams computes Recent, Muted and Meeting-chats folders toggled in settings and offers a switch-back that keeps custom sections [REA]. Slack's workspace rail badges each tenant from its own summary [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** The Spaces sidebar has fixed groups and a text filter with no stars, sections, hide, mute or room-level badge (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/sidebar/CoreSidebarSpacesPanel.tsx:30-232), and the WorkspaceSwitcher has no cross-org badge (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/CoreNavbar.tsx:672-729). A reusable organiser already exists for chat threads, with native drag, an accessible Move menu and a zod-typed reducer, but it is browser-local (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/sidebar/ChatSidebarOrganizer.tsx:14-130) [SRC]. Unread is server-backed per room; the missing piece is a per-user, per-org attention summary. First slice: promote the chat organiser's pattern to a synced Convex preference document of refs only, then computed sections on the attention summary; cross-org badges and Ctrl+1..9 as a later item needing a multi-org read path.

**Dependencies and risks.** Hide, mute and sort are presentation only and must never change Space access; mute is a notification preference distinct from agent pause. Each gateway request resolves one org, so badges must never mix two orgs' data.

<a id="F-t1-056"></a>
### F-t1-056 · Agent task tracker: Needs-input state, per-task threads and unread in the Work tab

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (task state), Application Plane (projection) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Give each agent task a visible state (working, needs input, done) with a note, link it to its run and Space thread, and project it with per-task unread into the Work tab and the activity inbox. A human should see exactly which agent task is waiting on them, which is the Grok Bot behaviour the vision asks for; today only runs awaiting approval surface.

**Reference practice.** Ships in 1 of 5 references (Grok Bot). Each task carries state working, needs_input or done, a note, done and seen-done timestamps and a sub-thread id; a message sent with a task id routes to the agent about that task, and a panel shows unread replies and done-but-unseen tasks [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** A durable task registry exists in capability-core with run_id, title, assignee and a free-text status that PATCH accepts (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/api/workplane_apis.go:618-700), proxied by model-gateway /v1/tasks, but it is org-scoped with no space_ref, no Control decision on read or write, no agent producer and no caller in verevonv3. The Work tab lists runs and schedules with awaiting_approval as the only needs-a-human state (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/activity-grammar.ts:118-137), and no needs-input pause exists anywhere in the Model Plane [SRC]. Link tasks to runs and threads through run_id plus a Control-bound space_ref used only as a filter, following the Space cron pattern; add needs_input as a status value together with a Model-side ask-the-human pause; reuse the run's Space thread and the existing todos rather than inventing per-task threads. This is not a frontend-only change and is no longer a quick win.

**Dependencies and risks.** Keep agent-owned tasks separate from human Lists ([F-t1-058](#F-t1-058)) and link by ref; a free space_ref readable by any org caller would leak private rooms; ZDR runs must not persist task content.

<a id="F-t1-057"></a>
### F-t1-057 · Artifact sharing: edit role, Space-as-subject grants, general access and access requests

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Control Plane (user-core) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Extend the single resource_grants authority with an edit role exposed to the UI, a Space subject evaluated as the Space decision intersected with the grant, a general-access setting off by default per org, and an access-request flow with a human-only approve action, across documents, canvases and files. Without an edit role other members cannot co-author, and without access requests the decision never reaches an owner.

**Reference practice.** Ships in 4 of 5 references. QM offers a Google-Docs-style dialog where request access creates a pending item for the owner and the decide endpoint checks the decider manages the resource's home scope before granting, never downgrading a higher role [SRC]. Slack canvas grants are independent of conversation membership and mentions notify only people with access [DOC]; Grok Bot uses private, team-view and public tiers [SRC]; Teams delegates to SharePoint permissions [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** Control already supports role edit and reserves a team subject (apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/012_resource_grants.up.sql:15-40); only the gateway hard-codes document, user and view (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/shares.rs:111-115). The real blocker is that POST and DELETE /authz/grant sit behind requireVerifiedAuthzDelegation, which admits only visible and check calls under v2 delegation while the gateway signs v1, so the share write path is fenced off today (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/http/server.go:384-400) [SRC]. First unblock that path with a verified resource-owner delegation or a dedicated grant-admin scope; then expose edit, add subject_type space with a CHECK migration, classify new resource types in resource_taxonomy.json with its Rust mirror, and build requests on the activity inbox. For documents reuse documents.space_ref instead of duplicating it with Space-subject grants.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t1-015](#F-t1-015) and [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013) cannot ship safely before phase 1. A Space subject may only narrow or co-require, never add an independent path; agents must never gain access through it; general access conflicts with ZDR and residency.

<a id="F-t1-058"></a>
### F-t1-058 · Typed room records and Lists: one revisioned work-object substrate with a table view, assignees and item threads

*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: L · Plane: Application Plane (records), Frontend Plane gateway (Control decisions) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Build a generic Space record primitive (stable id, namespaced type, body, previous-revision compare-and-swap, idempotent resubmit) and ship Lists on it: typed columns, a table view first, assignees and saved views. A list is where humans and agents assign work and see status, and one substrate avoids building tasks, decisions and checklists separately while keeping edits out of chat unread counts.

**Reference practice.** Lists ship in 3 of 5 references; a typed-record substrate in 1 of 5. Slack Lists define columns of text, select, people, date and checkbox, store views with filter, sort and group, and emit an activity item on assignment [DOC]. Buzz's record substrate keeps heads and revisions, advances the head by an atomic CAS on the previous revision, treats resubmits as idempotent, and excludes revisions from unread and notifications [SRC]. Teams offers Lists, Planner and Wiki tabs [REA].

**Verevon today and approach.** convex-core has no space_records tables (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:546-604), the gateway has no records route (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:649-731) and the cockpit tabs are chat, work, knowledge, activity, agent and members. Adjacent scaffolding exists: workspaceId-keyed plannerDocuments with no consumer, thread-scoped Model Plane todos, org-scoped capability-core tasks and support tickets with saved views. Control resolves exactly one agent owner action today, tickets.create (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/model_action_view.go:12-26) [SRC]. Ship human-edited records first with gateway-side Control decisions for new read and write effects, because Convex does not evaluate Space roles. Reuse the Todo status vocabulary, register the table in the Space deletion flow, and take the agent write path out: it is a new owner-action vertical with schema hash, run-bound decision and approval continuation.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs [F-t1-057](#F-t1-057) phase 1 and the activity inbox. Records must hang off spaceRef, never plannerDocuments or the Convex projects table (ADR rows 84-85); linked tickets and documents are refs rechecked by the owner plane; bundling the agent path makes this XL.

<a id="F-t1-059"></a>
### F-t1-059 · Do not disturb, quiet-hours schedules and urgent break-through, visible to agents

*Verevon today: Stub · Effort: M · Plane: Application Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give each person a do-not-disturb (DND) state: a manual pause, a recurring schedule with time zone, a VIP list and a rate-limited urgent break-through. While DND is on, non-critical signals from non-VIP actors go to the inbox only and flush when it ends. Agents work around the clock and will page people at night unless this gate ships before push ([F-t1-035](#F-t1-035)); an agent that can ask "may I interrupt now" can defer a non-urgent ask instead of blocking a run.

**Reference practice.** 2 of 5 references have DND. Slack combines a manual pause, a schedule, admin default hours, a VIP override and a once-a-day "notify anyway" break-through [DOC]. Teams adds urgent messages and priority contacts that pass DND and automatic DND in meetings [REA]. Buzz and QM have none [SRC].
