**Verevon today and approach.** Control user-core already stores a per-user QuietHours setting (enabled, start, end; default off) and a manual availability status, but nothing enforces or exposes them (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/http/handlers.go:999-1014, 225-262). notification-core's only gate is a per-event opt-out with no time window, and a suppressed request creates no inbox row (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/notification/service.go:326-360) [SRC]. Extend the user-core setting with time zone, days, dnd_until and VIP, project it into notification-core by event (identity_sync pattern), and add a tri-state gate (deliver, inbox-only, defer) after the preference check. Urgency is set by the trusted producer with a per-agent daily cap; agents get only a narrow "interruptible now" read bound to the run's subject.

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs Space producers from [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008); the deferred flush rides on the S4.4 ledger ([F-t1-060](#F-t1-060)). Never disclose the schedule to agents or members.

<a id="F-t1-060"></a>
### F-t1-060 · Finish the S4.4 delivery ledger and add a content-free missed-activity digest

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Application Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Finish notification-core's delivery ledger (bounded retries, max-age expiry, Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) proof, delivery errors reported back to the producing agent run) and add a per-user hourly or daily digest that pulls absent humans back when agents are blocked on them. The ledger is the base for push, email and scheduled output without lost or duplicated messages.

**Reference practice.** Outbox 2 of 5 references, digest 2 of 5. QM's DeliveryStore does enqueue, claim-pending and ack with idempotency keys, max-age expiry and five retries, reports undeliverable errors to the source session, and its morning digest auto-pauses when the person is inactive [SRC]. Buzz has a content-free mention outbox [SRC]; Teams offers a missed-activity email [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** A leased delivery-attempt state machine with request-level idempotency keys exists, but the worker is off by default, has no bounded-retry or max-age policy, and rejects ZDR requests outright (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/notification/service.go:296-302, delivery_worker.go:91-94). The Space ledger is a named gap (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:1616-1627), and insight-core's daily-brief scheduler is disabled because an organisation is not a recipient [SRC]. Treat finishing the ledger as planned S4.4 work and build no parallel Space ledger. Make the digest a separate Application Plane item: a per-user scheduler modelled on the briefs scheduler that composes counts and links from Application-owned state, not a capability-core schedule. A ZDR-org digest needs an explicit content-free request class first; links must resolve through normal membership checks.

**Dependencies and risks.** Signals from [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008) and the inbox from [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009); email through Novu is a content-persisting boundary whose provider proof is still open.

<a id="F-t1-061"></a>
### F-t1-061 · 'Catch me up' recap of what I missed, scoped to my own read access

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane (recap call); Frontend Plane (entry point) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Add a "Catch me up" action on the room header and the Spaces list that summarises unread messages and agent runs since the viewer arrived, prioritises mentions and items needing action, offers scopes (unread, last 7 days, custom) and mark-as-read on completion. For a returning person in an agent-heavy room this answers "what did the agents do and what needs me".

**Reference practice.** 2 of 5 references. Teams does a cross-thread recap prioritised by relevance and marks chats read when dismissed [REA]. Slack offers unread, last-7-days and custom-range scopes plus a recurring-summary workflow step [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The feature is absent; the only related thing is the Meeting Scribe template, which summarises when mentioned and posts to the whole room (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceCreateAgentDialog.tsx:35-60). The substrate is shipped: a per-member room read marker (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceReadMarkers.ts:1-60), a client arrival snapshot and unread set (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/space-unread.ts:1-45), and viewer-scoped transcript and work reads under Control decisions (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:2607-2640) [SRC]. Build it as a gateway-composed stateless summarise call over threads read under the viewer's own decisions, bounded in thread count, using the arrival snapshot as the window start. Keep the result viewer-private and ephemeral; never route it through a room agent, which would post it. Do not put it in the Inbox, which is the support-ticket inbox.

**Dependencies and risks.** Read markers from [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) already exist at room level. A ZDR turn is a single stateless call, which fits; a persisted private result raises effort to L.

<a id="F-t1-062"></a>
### F-t1-062 · Space UI quality baseline: locale-correct activity grammar and dates, server-held preferences, shared announcer, reduced motion, density and high contrast, failure screens

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Frontend Plane (with Control Plane for preferences) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Move the hard-coded Norwegian activity labels and forced nb-NO dates onto the i18n catalogue and viewer locale, pass the locale to Model Plane turns, read the server-held preferences at bootstrap, add a shared screen-reader announcer with row narration, forced-colors and prefers-contrast rules, a density attribute, and a root error boundary with a cannot-reach page. These are the places where an otherwise strong bilingual, accessible Space UI would regress as rooms grow.

**Reference practice.** Localisation 3 of 5 references, theming and high contrast 4 of 5, friendly activity copy 2 of 5. Grok Bot keys two hidden live regions by a counter so repeated messages announce and makes outgoing panes inert until animation ends [REA]. Teams ships 63 locales with a regional fallback map and start-up milestones; Slack has a cannot-connect page with log download [REA]. Buzz gates motion and offers three density levels [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** RUN_STATUS labels and verbs are Norwegian literals (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/activity-grammar.ts:118-137) and both date formatters force nb-NO (lib/space-thread-presentation.ts:73,90); there is no forced-colors rule in global.css and no error boundary (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/lib/create-resource-compat.ts:5) [SRC]. Two sub-items already exist: every Space animation is gated by prefers-reduced-motion, and user-core serves language, timezone, date format and compactMode through /api/v1/preferences (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/http/server.go:228) [SRC]. Drop those; make the frontend read the existing preferences (localStorage only as cache), route labels and dates through useI18n().tr and localeIntl(), wire compactMode as the density attribute, and spike Solid 2 boundary semantics before the fallback page.

**Dependencies and risks.** The timezone-to-region mapping in user-core handlers.go may be a bug (low confidence); check it before Space code depends on it. Diagnostic exports must exclude secrets and ZDR content.

<a id="F-t1-063"></a>
### F-t1-063 · Agent identity in the room: surface the persisted avatar colour (then shape) and add a hover card that leads with blocking state

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: S · Plane: Frontend Plane (gateway passthrough); Application Plane for shape later · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Render the avatar colour that the Application Plane already stores on every agent post, card, roster row, sidebar entry and mention picker, and add a hover or focus card for agents and members that leads with what blocks a human (awaiting approval), then a draft, then the last entry. Agents must be instantly distinguishable from people and never look like a colleague.

**Reference practice.** Avatars and hover card 1 of 5 references each. Grok Bot builds a deterministic non-human character from 14+ shapes by 11 colours, reuses the colour as an accent in agent rows, and orders its hover card as awaiting reason, draft, last entry, opened on delay and on keyboard focus [REA]. Buzz has a BotIdenticon and an agent badge naming the owner [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The create dialog collects one of ten colours, the gateway forwards it and Convex stores and projects it (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceAgents.ts:194, 441), but the gateway composition field map omits avatarColor (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4096-4160), the SpaceAgent type has no field (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/spaces-client.ts:704-733) and cards render an initial (SpacePage.tsx:911-915) [SRC]. Slice 1 (S, no plane work): add the field to the map and type, and render one shared AgentAvatar with the current initial or Sparkles glyph as fallback. The hover card is S to M on @kobalte/core hover-card, which the pinned alpha ships, reading the live-run projection and AWAITING_APPROVAL state. Shapes and uploads are a later Application Plane follow-up.

**Dependencies and risks.** Colour is presentation only and must never enter an authority decision; the card shows other members only "waiting on owner", never approval details or controls. An agent Control authorises but Application has not described keeps a neutral avatar.

<a id="F-t1-064"></a>
### F-t1-064 · Room creation flows: templates with an agent roster and seed artifacts, clone, project and case Spaces bound to work objects, invite-on-mention

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Application Plane (templates, bound Spaces) with Control Plane (kinds, decisions) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Let a room be created from a server-side template (instructions plus an agent roster with binding policy) or cloned, offer to add a non-member or bind an unbound agent when someone mentions them, and later create case and project Spaces bound to tickets and projects. Templates with an agent roster turn recurring work into ready-made human-plus-agent rooms, something neither Slack nor Teams has.

**Reference practice.** Templates 2 of 5 references (Buzz, Teams), resource-bound rooms 1 of 5 (QM), mention-to-invite 1 of 5 (Buzz). Buzz instantiates a template by creating the room and binding each agent through the normal decision, and on send returns non-member mentions so the client can offer an invite through the normal add-member path [SRC]. QM keeps channel-derived project members separate so unlinking removes only them [SRC]. Slack channel templates that pre-seed lists rest on docs only [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Named room creation takes a name only and refuses project and case kinds (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:2873) although Control and the Convex schema accept them; the only templates are three client-side prompt presets, and the composer offers only bound agents and roster people as mention candidates (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:184-203) [SRC]. A room is born pending_registration, so "one transaction" is really a resumable saga: create, await registration, set instructions, bind each agent through its own Control confirmation. Split into templates (M), invite-on-mention (M), case and project kinds after an ADR defines owner and lifecycle, and seed artifacts after [F-t1-058](#F-t1-058).

**Dependencies and risks.** Templates must never pre-grant tools, a mention never grants, and the offer must not reveal whether a private candidate exists. Owner-plane ACLs stay authoritative for bound Spaces.

<a id="F-t1-065"></a>
### F-t1-065 · Governed Space skills and plugin bundles (ecosystem-compatible manifests)

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane (skill store, plugin registry); Control Plane (publish decisions); Ingestion Plane (git pack import) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Give skills a Space scope and a review lifecycle (draft, reviewed, published, archived; any edit invalidates review), with a Control decision on who may publish. Later, import skills from Claude-style and Cursor-style plugin bundles. A team can then curate room-specific know-how safely, which is how agents improve in a shared room.

**Reference practice.** Governed skills 2 of 5 references, plugin bundles 2 of 5. QM runs draft to reviewed to published to archived with signed manifests verified on resolve, visibility filtering per principal and scope, and git packs pinned to a ref [SRC]. Grok Bot reads both .cursor-plugin and .claude-plugin marketplace manifests, pins git sources to a 40-hex sha, declares a variables schema and registers each component with its subsystem [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Skills exist with org and user scope only (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/migrations/0031_agent_skills_ownership.sql:1-25), are resolved server-side and picked per turn with the composer's / picker, but have no Space scope, lifecycle or signing. The plugin registry is a record store: rows default disabled, the wire type carries a manifest URL and nothing loads one (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/api/plugins.go:1-70) [SRC]. Split it. Governed Space skills (L): extend session-core agent_skills, the live store the resolver reads, with scope space, review-state columns and immutable reviewed versions, plus a new Control publish decision. Ecosystem plugins: there is no plugin host, so defer the fan-out; start only with importing static skill markdown as draft Space skills requiring review, sha-pinned, refusing hooks and MCP components.

**Dependencies and risks.** S3.5 must first pick the canonical skill record. A skill body is prompt text, so the review gate must cover the exact stored body; Space-level plugin activation waits for a record to write to.

<a id="F-t1-067"></a>
### F-t1-067 · Duplex voice for agents: a voice front that delegates to the Space agent, then agents as attributed participants in room calls

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Phase A: inside a Space, a person holds a realtime voice conversation with a fast voice persona whose only tools delegate, send_task handing real work to the existing agent run (still behind approval gates) and a history search over Space threads, with per-agent voice, speed and language. Phase B: a server-side participant per invited agent hears a room call and answers with an attributed track. This is the defining hybrid moment: humans and agents in one voice room, and an agent tasked hands-free.

**Reference practice.** 1 of 5 references each. Grok Bot's realtime session exposes send_task, recall and search_conversations tools, announces worker results as async updates, allows one active voice chat per user and keeps per-agent voice settings [SRC]. Buzz publishes agent audio under the agent identity, flushes on about 300 ms silence, cancels agent speech on any human speech and speaks only when addressed [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Model Plane already serves POST /v1/ai/realtime/session with server VAD and a ZDR fail-closed route (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:4900-4945), but the request has no tools field (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/inference.proto:1156-1175), the BFF has no route and the shipped voice mode is half-duplex and mounted only in the dashboard composer [SRC]. No call substrate exists anywhere. Add a BFF route that mints a session with Space-scoped instructions and a tool schema; because the browser holds the provider socket, send_task and search execute in the browser through the existing composer and read routes, and per-minute cost stays unmetered until a Model-side relay exists. Phase A is L; Phase B is XL and waits for [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029).

**Dependencies and risks.** ZDR Spaces get no duplex voice today; a personal Space cannot hold an agent; consent from [F-t1-050](#F-t1-050) before an agent hears a room. Always label agent voices.

<a id="F-t1-005"></a>
### F-t1-005 · Make zero-data-retention binding in every plane, with typed refusals and content-free telemetry

*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: S · Plane: Model Plane (session-core, model-gateway, sandbox-manager) with Application Plane and Frontend Plane · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High · moved down from Priority by verification*

**What and why.** Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) is a Verevon promise and an architecture rule. The remaining work is hardening: commit the untracked refusal catalogue and matrix test, type two refusal sites, cross-check the Space decision flag, record the per-Space ZDR product decision and guard telemetry labels. Regulated buyers look for exactly these seams.

**Reference practice.** ZDR blocking persistence: 1 of 5 references. Grok Bot blocks its persistent computer (sandbox) under privacy mode with a typed no-storage error that the UI maps to a visible block state; that is the one confirmed feature, not a general pattern [SRC]. Consent-deferred client analytics 1 of 5 (Grok Bot, 256-event cap, disabled on opt-out) [SRC]; Teams has a diagnostic data viewer [REA]; QM sends error context unredacted when enabled, an anti-pattern [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Session Core already enforces ZDR on the verified token claim: authorize_operation refuses non-read scopes on CreateThread, AppendMessage and StartRun, and memory preflight gates durable and Letta memory (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/auth.rs:262-285, memory_grpc.rs:105-122). Only the Space decision's own flag is decoded and discarded (grpc.rs:1735-1775), unreachable today. A typed catalogue (apps/Model Plane/rust/crates/mp-contracts/src/zdr_refusal.rs:1-140), posture matrix and SPA module exist but are untracked in git; Session Core refusals are plain strings; per-Space ZDR is "not applied in v1"; the README's zdr-contract-sync script is missing [SRC]. Do the five hardening steps above; add a sandbox-manager flag only if leases become reachable by ZDR callers.

**Dependencies and risks.** A missing claim already fails closed. Applying per-Space ZDR needs a Control decision and is a separate M or L item; under ZDR, approvals cannot resume by design.

## 6. Nice to have

These 31 items add polish, reach or assurance once the table-stakes and recommended work is in place. None of them blocks the Space vision; several are gated on a product decision (calls), on substrates that do not exist yet (push transport, a desktop shell, attachments in rooms), or on a single reference source. Each entry states what the item is, which reference does it best, and the verified Verevon gap or approach; the status, effort, plane and wave come from the verified data and supersede earlier estimates.

### 6.1 Agent lifecycle and governance

- <a id="F-t1-071"></a>**F-t1-071 · Shareable, versioned agent templates with an export policy, and an internal catalogue of org agents and skills** (Partial · M · Application Plane with Control Plane · W5). User-published templates with immutable versions, an active-version pointer, org or team visibility and an export policy that never copies credentials, memory or grants; plus an admin-approved catalogue of org agents. It is polish because the registry already spreads agents inside an org. Reference: Grok Bot templates with PUBLIC or TEAM visibility, an active version and a secret-stripping export policy, 2 of 5 references [SRC]. Verevon has three hard-coded client-side templates that only copy text into the form, and the org registry plus installable-agents bind flow already deliver most of the catalogue (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceAgents.ts:486-515), so templates become a new Convex agentTemplates table and the catalogue an extension of that read model.

- <a id="F-t1-072"></a>**F-t1-072 · Scoped agent context and promotion path: team layer, setup manifests, publish to team, service accounts** (Partial · XL · Model Plane with Control Plane decisions · W5). A team layer in the authored-instruction hierarchy, scoped setup manifests for agent computers, a publish-to-team flow with an item-by-item carry-over proposal, and agent-owned service credentials per binding. Only nice to have because the stricter half (owner-plane ACL, authored instruction hierarchy) exists. Reference: QM mounts org and team layers read-only and the active scope read-write; Grok Bot's PublishToTeam confirms carry-over item by item and never copies chats, each 1 of 5 [SRC]. There is no ResolvedSpaceContext struct to extend; the real seam is the gateway's inject_authored_instructions (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4225-4300), and the team layer waits for Control Teams ([F-t1-054](#F-t1-054)) being linked to Spaces.

- <a id="F-t1-081"></a>**F-t1-081 · Long-tail polish: role-aware agent templates and persona library, owner-reviewed config proposals, catch-up summaries, external MCP surface** (Partial · L · Frontend Plane and Application Plane · W5). Role and tool-tagged templates with a self-introduction, a persona library with an allowlisted export, agent-proposed configuration changes an owner reviews in a prefilled dialog, time-windowed catch-up summaries, and a hosted Model Context Protocol (MCP) surface. Each appears in only 1 or 2 of 5 references and adds modest value. Reference: Grok Bot ships 74 eligibility-tagged templates [REA]; Buzz copies only explicit manifest fields, asserted by tests [SRC]. Verevon has three static templates with no role or tool tags (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceCreateAgentDialog.tsx:15-75), a Meeting Scribe catch-up and re-bindable org definitions; split the bundle and keep summaries under the room read decision and Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR).

- <a id="F-t1-088"></a>**F-t1-088 · Versioned publish lifecycle for agent-built apps and shared agent definitions** (Planned · XL · Application Plane with Model, Infra and Control · W5). Governed build and publish of agent-built internal apps (immutable artifact, manifest, pointer-flip rollback, private until shared) and draft or published revisions for shared agent definitions, pinned by Space bindings. Expensive and not core to the chat room. Reference: QM deployments point at a current_version and roll back by a compare-and-swap pointer flip; Grok Bot keeps agent draft and published revisions, 2 of 5 and 1 of 5 [SRC]. Verevon's app lifecycle exists only as plan slice S5.4 (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/docs/VEREVON_QM_COMPARISON_AND_ADOPTION_PLAN_2026-08-13.md:2263-2268) and agent definitions mutate in place in Convex, so put revisions in Application Plane, not a Model Plane registry, and split the separable M revision feature from the XL apps lifecycle.

### 6.2 Room polish and client state

- <a id="F-t1-070"></a>**F-t1-070 · Room header and pulse rail extras: live counts, pinned posts first, user-pinned resource tabs and a budget meter; PWA share target and MCP apps later** (Partial · M · Frontend Plane, meter data from Model Plane · W5). Header counts from existing projections, pinned posts rendered first, user-pinnable link and document tabs stored as Space metadata, and later a usage meter, a Progressive Web App (PWA) share target and MCP apps. Polish that waits on canvas, cost attribution and the PWA. Reference: QM's top bar shows tool buttons with counts cached per scope for 60 s, 2 of 5 for the header shelf; Grok Bot's usage meter is unique [REA]. Pinned-first is a client sort defect: Session Core lists pinned threads first but SpaceRoomTimeline re-sorts by updated_at (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:137-141), so ship pinned-first and header counts now and split the rest.

- <a id="F-t1-074"></a>**F-t1-074 · Provenance footer on relayed and automated messages and notifications; optional system voice** (Partial · S · Model Plane with Application Plane; system voice later · W5). A footer such as "scheduled run: schedule name for creator" on posts and notifications, and optionally one system sender for platform notices with a per-user cap and opt-out. Useful once rooms fill with cron runs, but not blocking. Reference: QM sets a relaySender field when attribution differs from the sender and records the trigger in delivery provenance, 1 of 5 [SRC]; Slack's single Slackbot voice is 2 of 5 for system voice [DOC]. The provenance is already recorded server-side (run metadata source, schedule_id, creator subject; message author_subject_id and agent_name, apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/migrations/0035_message_author_attribution.sql:1-20) but SpaceThread exposes no origin or schedule field, so expose it on the thread summary and render it in the timeline.

- <a id="F-t1-080"></a>**F-t1-080 · ZDR-safe client persistence: Space drafts, per-user Space state synced across devices, cache hygiene, and an optional intent-only offline queue** (Partial · M · Frontend Plane with Application Plane · W5). Session-only composer drafts for non-ZDR orgs, server-side per-user Space state (mutes, sidebar sections) with last-writer-wins, and later a versioned head-page cache and an intent-only offline queue. Push plus paging already gives most of the speed benefit. Reference: Buzz keeps a bounded, versioned paint-accelerator cache always replaced by authoritative data; a local replica ships in 4 of 5 references [SRC]. Verevon already has room-level read markers, user-core preferences and a complete ZDR-gated session-only Home draft pattern (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/session/home-draft-storage.ts:9-77); SpaceRoomComposer persists nothing, so re-key that pattern by org, user and Space, gate on the org posture and the Space's own ZDR flag, and keep per-thread markers out, as a recorded decision rejects them.

- <a id="F-t1-082"></a>**F-t1-082 · Long-tail polish: voice and video clips with transcripts, image markup, per-viewer translation, room lenses** (Partial · L · Frontend Plane with Model Plane inference · W5). Short clips with transcripts, a markup step before sending an image, on-demand per-viewer translation that is not stored, and a per-room threads-versus-posts lens with an announcement mode. None blocks the core room. Reference: Grok Bot voice notes with streaming speech-to-text and word-level transcript jump; clips ship in 4 of 5 references, translation and lenses in 2 of 5 [REA]. The Space composer has no attachment, voice or image control (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:108-140), but the recorder, batch transcription and model-gateway /v1/ai/translate exist, so do translation first (only the BFF proxy and UI are missing, with the ZDR posture raise), then dictation into the composer, then clips after [F-t1-013](#F-t1-013).

- <a id="F-t1-089"></a>**F-t1-089 · Multi-window coordination: pop-out run console, cross-org unread counts, idle presence signal** (Absent · M · Frontend Plane, projections in Application Plane · W5). Compact pop-out routes for the run console and a thread, an active or away presence signal from visibility plus input idle, and a per-org unread-count endpoint for the org switcher. Nice to have because one window covers the core flow. Reference: Teams has a window manager with tagged windows, pop-out chats and shared notifications across tenant instances, pop-out 2 of 5 and multi-workspace unread 3 of 5 [REA]. Presence is already gated on tab visibility with a 6 s beat (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpacePage.tsx:200-250) but has no away state, unread counts cover the active org only, and a pop-out cannot show another org because active org is session state; defer the shared-connection multiplexer until a push stream exists.

- <a id="F-t1-095"></a>**F-t1-095 · Long-tail Space UX, build on demand: multiview tiling, agent-led first-run welcome, What's New and admin notices, non-web link interstitial** (Absent · L · Frontend Plane · W5). Tiling several rooms or agent sessions, a seeded agent introduction in the org room, a What's New panel with admin notices, and a confirmation gate for non-http(s) links in agent-posted content. Build on demand, except the link slice. Reference: QM multiview with dockview tiles capped at 16 and per-user layout, 1 of 5 [SRC]; Slack intercepts non-web schemes with a per-host allowlist, 1 of 5 [REA]. SpacePage mounts one room per route and AgentRunConsole is a prop-less page, not a tile (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/agents/components/AgentRunConsole.tsx:168-186); ship a scheme allowlist (http, https, mailto) in parseInline now as a safeguard and leave the other three slices until asked for.

### 6.3 Desktop shell and local reach

- <a id="F-t1-073"></a>**F-t1-073 · Tauri desktop shell around the gateway origin: narrow bridge, system-browser sign-in, protocol links, signed updater** (Absent · XL · Frontend Plane, sign-in handoff in Control Plane · W5). A Tauri 2 shell loading the gateway origin with a locked content security policy, a bridge of at most about 15 capability commands, system-browser sign-in via a single-use Proof Key for Code Exchange (PKCE) handoff, a native protocol handler and a signed updater. The PWA ([F-t1-035](#F-t1-035)) captures most value first; enterprise rollout needs this later. Reference: all 5 of 5 ship a native shell and protocol handler; Teams wraps a web UI in a thin shell with a versioned RPC over capability modules [REA]; Buzz's Tauri 2 shell is the closest stack fit [SRC]. Verevon has a plain Vite entry and no shell, PKCE endpoint or updater (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/index.html:1-16); the installer and signing pipeline is greenfield and the PKCE handoff needs a new auth-core endpoint.

- <a id="F-t1-090"></a>**F-t1-090 · Shell-only conveniences: quick composer, global hotkey and push-to-talk, window state, close guard** (Absent · M · Frontend Plane · W5). An always-on-top mini composer posting into the personal Space, a validated global shortcut with key-up for push-to-talk, window-state memory, and a web-first beforeunload guard for unsent text or a run awaiting approval. The shell parts need OS hooks a tab cannot provide. Reference: Grok Bot's transparent always-on-top mini composer sends to the selected bot, 2 of 5 for a quick composer; Slack's global-shortcut API validates bindings and emits key-up [REA]. Verevon has no shell and no beforeunload or pagehide handler anywhere (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/verevon-web/plans/system-audits/COPILOT_APP_PARITY_2026-09-28.md:192); ship the S close guard now and route the quick composer through the Space-injection chat stream path, never a run API with client-asserted authority, after [F-t1-073](#F-t1-073).

- <a id="F-t1-096"></a>**F-t1-096 · Reverse channel so a user's own computer can run agent tool requests without inbound ports (later; cloud browser first)** (Partial · XL · Endpoint Plane, driven by Control grants · W5). A local executor that keeps an outbound stream open, receives Model-proposed tool requests, checks them against local policy, executes and posts results back, with heartbeat, polling fallback and supervised restart. Distinctive but a large local-execution attack surface, and the cloud browser path covers most needs. Reference: Grok Bot's daemon opens an SSE request stream, posts results with the request id and is adopted via a discovery record; 1 of 5 [SRC]. Verevon already has a native host agent (Verevon Agent, Rust) that registers outbound and gates control with a consent prompt (apps/Endpoint Plane/docs/architecture.md:1-20) but no tool-request channel; register Endpoint Plane ownership and extend that agent rather than build a Quarry-style executor in Ingestion.

### 6.4 Calls and meetings (decision-gated)

- <a id="F-t1-084"></a>**F-t1-084 · Meeting recording to governed object storage with a state machine, a mandatory recording indicator and expiry (defer behind transcripts)** (Absent · L · Data Plane · W5). Optional recording via Selective Forwarding Unit (SFU) egress to object storage, a record with started, processing, saved, failed and expired states, a mandatory indicator, an expiry job and meeting-record access. Transcripts and recaps carry most value with far less sensitive data. Reference: Teams has the complete state machine with cloud storage, expiry in N days and upload errors by cause, 2 of 5 [REA]. Verevon has no call, SFU or meeting-record code; the only raw audio-video storage is the docs-only Channel Plane plan (apps/Channel Plane/docs/meeting-intelligence.md:40-66), so the only aligned near-term shape is uploaded recordings registered as Data Plane documents, refused for ZDR orgs.

- <a id="F-t1-085"></a>**F-t1-085 · Live in-meeting AI layer: captions, an optional interpreter agent and a facilitator agent (agenda, timers, live context)** (Absent · L · Application Plane with Model Plane inference · W5). Caption events with language auto-detect, an interpreter agent publishing a text-to-speech (TTS) track per language, and a Facilitator template that tracks an agenda, nudges on time and surfaces Data Plane context. A visible agent-in-the-room moment, but blocked behind calls, live automatic speech recognition (ASR) and agent participants. Reference: Teams ships captions with spoken-language auto-detect, an interpreter with a roster identity and agenda smart actions, 1 of 5 [REA]. Verevon has only batch speech-to-text, governed TTS and a text-only Meeting Scribe template (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/ai.rs:30-80); the cheap first step is a post-hoc facilitator over a transcribed meeting, opt-in and refused for ZDR orgs.

- <a id="F-t1-086"></a>**F-t1-086 · Call polish and operability: call-health feedback, browser audio and video processing, in-tab push-to-talk and mute keys, wake lock and busy guard, call sounds and Join notifications** (Partial · M · Frontend Plane · W5). Web-native follow-ups to calls: browser noise constraints, a getStats health panel with reason-code feedback, hold-to-talk keys, Screen Wake Lock, a busy-reason registry guarding beforeunload, sounds and Join notifications. Most of it presupposes calls that do not exist. Reference: Slack ships push-to-talk with separate press and release, a restart veto with reason codes and a keep-awake lease; Teams triggers feedback from metrics, each 1 to 2 of 5 [SRC]. Verevon enables echo cancellation and noise suppression only for the dictation recorder (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/voice/voice-recorder.ts:36-41) and has no wakeLock or beforeunload code; keep the call-independent slices (busy guard, run-rating reason codes, hold-to-talk, Wake Lock during long runs) and drop the rest until a call decision.

- <a id="F-t1-029"></a>**F-t1-029 · Room calls on a bought SFU with Control-authorized join and a navigation-persistent call bar, modelled as a meeting record that belongs to the Space** (Absent · L · Application Plane, Frontend Plane, Control Plane, Infra Plane · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. A Call button that starts or joins the room's single live call on a self-hostable SFU plus TURN, with a Convex call record, a gateway-minted short-lived join token after a Control decision for a new space.call.join effect, and a persistent call bar. Table stakes in human chat, but the Space definition lists huddles as a non-goal, so a product decision comes first. Reference: calls started from a room ship in 3 of 5 (Slack, Teams, Buzz); Slack huddles give the product model [DOC], Buzz the data model [SRC], Teams the UX [REA]. Nothing exists: the Space header has no call affordance (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpacePage.tsx:358-377), so sequence it after the human-only post path and push presence, accept a 6 s poll-based call card first, and keep Verevon code to authority and signalling.

- <a id="F-t1-066"></a>**F-t1-066 · Agents attend Zoom, Meet and Teams meetings as visible guests through an Ingestion capture worker, with approval-gated chat drafts** (Planned · L · Ingestion Plane, ownership to be decided by ADR · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. A capture worker joins an external meeting as a labelled guest after consent, feeds the transcript into the [F-t1-050](#F-t1-050) pipeline and leaves; the agent may post only human-approved drafts. Meeting coverage without a conferencing stack, but dependent on unbuilt ASR, consent policy and an ownership decision, plus platform terms-of-service risk. Reference: Grok Bot exposes JoinMeeting, LeaveMeeting, ReadMeetingTranscript and a MeetingChatDraft that a human approves, 1 of 5, medium confidence from RPC names and strings [SRC]. Verevon has docs only (apps/Channel Plane/docs/meeting-intelligence.md:38-58) and calendar reads already surface meeting links that nothing consumes; prefer a vendor meeting-bot API or the platforms' own transcript export via integration-corev2 as the first adapter, and treat a headless-browser guest as a research spike.

- <a id="F-t1-068"></a>**F-t1-068 · Screen sharing in calls with a sharing indicator, sensitive-surface redaction, and the same cue for agents acting in a room** (Absent · XL · Frontend Plane, media service owner to be decided · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. Publish a getDisplayMedia track to the call, show a persistent sharing bar with Stop, mask approval cards and secrets while sharing, and reuse the border design to show when an agent is acting. About M as an increment once a call stack exists, but hard-dependent on [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029). Reference: Slack's native border overlay follows the shared window and the picker excludes the app's own sensitive windows; 2 of 5 [SRC]. Verevon's only getDisplayMedia use is a one-frame screenshot in the Home composer (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/DashboardComposer.tsx:1915-1950), and the agent-acting cue exists only as an "is working" row; keep the agent cue as its own S item on the live-run projection and move human screen share behind the call decision.

### 6.5 Search, APIs and external reach

- <a id="F-t1-075"></a>**F-t1-075 · Agent external channels: per-agent email first, then Slack and Teams apps** (Stub · XL · Ingestion Plane, Application Plane, Control Plane, Channel Plane · W5). Inbound mail verified and deduped in Ingestion wakes an agent as an untrusted unattended turn, outbound send is an approval-gated action, and later an agent publishes into Slack or Teams as its own app with an install-state machine. Meets users where they chat, but a per-agent address is a new authority design. Reference: Grok Bot creates a Slack app per bot through the manifest API with typed install outcomes and gives each bot a dedicated mail domain, 2 of 5 for chat apps and unique for email [SRC]. The self-forward mail-task pipeline is built but unwired, answering 503 mail_task_pipeline_not_configured (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/mail_task.rs:395-440); wiring it is a finishing job, while a per-agent address reverses the owner-only sender ruling and opens a prompt-injection surface, so design its authority separately.

- <a id="F-t1-076"></a>**F-t1-076 · Public developer API with scoped tokens and rate limits (events, external bots and a curated catalogue later)** (Partial · L · Frontend Plane gateway, Control Plane tokens, Application Plane events · W5). An OpenAPI description generated from the typed action registry, user-bound scoped revocable tokens (action ids plus an optional Space allowlist, never wider than the user's ACL), per-token token-bucket limits with Retry-After, and generated clients; events and external bots only on demand. Table stakes in Teams, Slack and Buzz but invisible to most users at launch. Reference: Teams Graph is the broadest parity surface with OAuth2 scopes and SDKs; Slack gives the clearest rate-limit contract, 3 of 5 for a public API [DOC]. Org-scoped keys are already minted, listed and revoked end to end and the registry builds a deterministic JSON-Schema catalog (gateway apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/settings/api_keys.rs, the auth-core validate RPC at apps/Control Plane/auth-core/src/auth/orpc-router.ts:2717-2790, and src/shared/actions/catalog-manifest.ts), so the real gap is gateway-side token authentication with Space-safe scopes: extend, do not build.

- <a id="F-t1-083"></a>**F-t1-083 · Federated search across connected providers under the asker's authority** (Partial · L · Frontend Plane gateway with Ingestion Plane · W5). Extend the [F-t1-069](#F-t1-069) fan-out with an integration-corev2 backend so a Space searches connected systems as the asking user, each backend under its own Control decision, results never cached, and failed or limited sources shown. An "ask the whole company" surface that needs message search and populated Space knowledge first. Reference: QM runs a SearchBackend registry concurrently with per-backend timeouts and pushes failures into failed[] and limited[] arrays, 2 of 5 [SRC]; Slack's Enterprise search over Google, Microsoft and Salesforce is docs only [DOC]. The pieces exist but are not joined: provider search-shaped reads ship in integration-corev2 (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/README.md:262-297) and the gateway has a read-only per-connection browse proxy used only by the home composer, so build only the orchestrator in order of readiness.

- <a id="F-t1-087"></a>**F-t1-087 · Read-only snapshot share of a room or thread with an allowlisted projection** (Absent · L · Application Plane record, Session Core projection, Control decision · W5). An immutable snapshot of a room outcome (org or link scope) holding only user messages, final replies and copied attachments, never reasoning or tool output, behind an unguessable token on a session-less, no-store, noindex route; the creator's access is re-checked on every view; disallowed under ZDR. A safe hand-off to a customer or auditor, mainly for case Spaces with external parties. Reference: QM's share_snapshots store an allowlisted projection and share-specific file copies, and every read re-checks that the creator can still see the source, else 404; 1 of 5, unique [SRC]. Verevon's gateway shares only grant per-user document views and the Space route table has no share or export route (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:652-732); put the snapshot record in Application Plane with a new Control decision class for the audience, and start with org scope, not link scope.

- <a id="F-t1-093"></a>**F-t1-093 · Multi-org and external collaboration horizon: org archive and transfer, shared cross-org rooms, managed external chat connections** (Partial · XL · Control Plane · W5). Org lifecycle states (archived, owner transfer) first; later org-to-org links with host-owned shared rooms, external trust cues and QM's rule that an external audience restricts agents to a context without org memory or keys. Enterprise differentiators, but Verevon's customers are single companies today. Reference: Slack Enterprise Grid and Slack Connect give two-level tenancy and shared channels, 3 of 5 for shared channels [DOC]; Buzz runs an operator lifecycle state machine on the tenant row [SRC]. org-core knows only active versus soft-deleted (apps/Control Plane/org-core/migrations/001_init.up.sql:8-12), but auth-core already has a host-anchored org_group with per-org grants, so reuse it with UI and lifecycle instead of a parent-org table, and split into org lifecycle (M), org_group UI (S to M) and shared rooms after guests ([F-t1-053](#F-t1-053)).

- <a id="F-t1-069"></a>**F-t1-069 · Search operators with Verevon-native filters, typed result tabs and recent-search history** (Absent · L · Frontend Plane; owner planes expose per-domain search · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. Port Buzz's operator parser as a pure TypeScript module, add agent:, has:approval, has:run, has:artifact and is:thread, render typed result tabs with counts, and fan out to owner planes returning {hits, counts, failed[], limited[]}. A refinement of search, so it follows [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018) rather than leads. Reference: operators ship in 3 of 5; Buzz's parseSearchOperators recognises an operator only at token start and leaves malformed values in free text [SRC]; Teams fans one query out to per-domain resolvers [REA]. No search over Space messages, runs or approvals exists; the gateway search routes cover only web, similar, suggest, images, videos and answer (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/search.rs:39-45), so land the S parser with the search UI and start run and approval tabs as a client filter over the per-room activity projection.

### 6.6 Operability, assurance and compliance

- <a id="F-t1-077"></a>**F-t1-077 · Compliance export, custodian legal hold, information barriers and audited admin break-glass** (Partial · XL · Control Plane with Model, Application and Data Planes · W5). A compliance principal whose org-wide read rests on signed, audited decisions: export by custodian, Space, time or keyword; custodian holds that pin items from retention; pairwise information barriers; and admin break-glass for agent computers. Regulated buyers need it, but it follows retention and audit export. Reference: Teams and Slack ship eDiscovery, hold and barriers, 2 of 5 [DOC]; Grok Bot exposes 41 team-scoped admin RPCs including break-glass delete, 2 of 5 for break-glass [SRC]. Control already has Space-keyed legal holds that only block the deletion saga (apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/022_space_deletion_authority.up.sql:14-19) and no retention sweeper exists for a hold to pin; move room export to Session Core behind a new Control-signed compliance-export decision, since Application holds only membership metadata, and state that ZDR rooms have nothing to export.

- <a id="F-t1-078"></a>**F-t1-078 · Perceived-latency observability: W3C traceparent on every send and stream, and performance budgets under simulated agent load** (Partial · M · Frontend Plane · W5). A traceparent header and client marks (enter, ack, first frame, missing row, resync) on Space sends and streams, propagated through the gateway, plus a Playwright performance project that floods a room with agent output and asserts typing and scroll budgets via the Event Timing API. Nobody measures this today, yet it is observability rather than a feature. Reference: Grok Bot adds traceparent and enter timestamps to every send with 114 named perceived-quality events; Buzz tests typing and scroll budgets under simulated agent load, 3 of 5 [SRC]. The server half ships: model-gateway emits a content-free turn_timing event that chat-client parses (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/chat-client.ts:390-420); add the client half with performance.now marks rather than Grok's wall-clock subtraction, which conflicts with the repo's same-clock rule.

- <a id="F-t1-079"></a>**F-t1-079 · Ids-only "report this" flow and hardened error-code registry** (Partial · M · Frontend Plane with Application Plane intake · W5). A Report action in the Space, the run console and the error card that sends only ids (Space, run, thread, request, build, error code) and an optional note, plus domain and retryable fields in the error catalogue with a gateway test that every emitted code has an entry. Helps support reconstruct runs without content; not a user-facing capability. Reference: Slack ships a self-service diagnostics kit with zipped rotating logs; Teams a report-a-problem with chunked upload, 2 of 5 [REA]; Grok Bot's 108 coded errors carry domain and retryable flags, unique [REA]. A shell FeedbackWidget already posts free text to /api/v1/inbox/feedback (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/core/components/FeedbackWidget.tsx:17-120) with no ids and no ZDR check; extend it and SubmitFeedback with a structured diagnostics object instead of a new Support Plane route, which owns no tickets.

- <a id="F-t1-091"></a>**F-t1-091 · Realtime and media operability after push ships: shared connection per browser, drain on deploy, capability discovery, stream-stall self-test, perf marks, media cache policy** (Partial · M · Frontend Plane gateway and SPA with Application Plane · W5). One connection per browser via a leader tab, drain with a close frame and client resume, capabilities and limits in the connected frame, a stall watchdog that detects proxy buffering, web vitals and a space_open mark, and a media cache policy. Hardening that matters only after the push substrate ([F-t1-003](#F-t1-003)). Reference: Buzz shares one socket with frame coalescing and autoscales on an active-websocket gauge, 3 of 5 for shared sync and scale-out [SRC]; Slack drains cells in about 5 minutes [DOC]. model-gateway already drains on SIGTERM, exports an active-streams gauge and sends 15 s keep-alive comment frames, but the SPA reader skips comment frames (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/sse.ts:106), so build the watchdog and the Frontend gateway's graceful shutdown now and the leader tab after push.

- <a id="F-t1-092"></a>**F-t1-092 · Delivery health: channel failover and a "why am I not notified" diagnosis** (Absent · M · Application Plane with Frontend Plane banner · W5). Per-device and per-channel health in the delivery ledger with fallback from push to in-app to email digest, paired with a Settings and bell banner listing typed reasons notifications are not arriving (DND, permission denied, subscription expired, room muted, channel failing). Worthwhile only after push and email exist. Reference: Slack keeps a consecutive-failure counter, records a week-long failover timestamp and returns typed warning reason codes cached 30 s; 1 of 5, unique [SRC]. notification-core's ledger tracks Novu submissions per notification, not per-channel health, and its routes expose only feed, preferences and channel configs (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/http/server.go:55-100); when push exists, ship the diagnosis banner first and treat automatic failover as a Novu workflow concern (inference).

- <a id="F-t1-094"></a>**F-t1-094 · Assurance long tail: hash-chained audit, formal decision model, supply-chain gates, EU-posture pinning, credential expiry states** (Partial · L · Control Plane audit-core with Support, Model and Ingestion Planes · W5). Five independent facets: chained audit rows with a signed daily head checkpoint and verify endpoint; a TLA+ model of Space decision issuance checking "never widens an owner ACL"; dependency cooldown, cargo-deny and govulncheck gates; per-org residency pinning; credential expiry states. They answer security questionnaires rather than block the Space. Reference: Buzz hashes each audit entry over the previous hash with an external signed anchor and mutates its formal spec to prove invariants are not vacuous, 1 of 5 each [SRC]; residency ships in 2 of 5 (Slack, Teams) [DOC]. audit-core keeps only a per-event payload_hash for idempotent dedupe with no prev_hash or checkpoint (apps/Control Plane/audit-core/internal/store/schema.sql:11-33), while supply-chain checks, an EU residency label and credential status are further along than assumed; treat it as five rows and start by tightening the existing CI gates.

## 7. What each reference does uniquely well, and how that helps Verevon

This section reads each reference for what it does better than the others, not for the table stakes. Evidence quality differs: buzz and QM are read from source [SRC]; Grok Bot from shipped, often minified, bundles [SRC]; Slack mostly from public documentation [DOC] plus the desktop shell [SRC]; Teams mostly from reverse-engineered strings [REA] plus documentation [DOC], which shows that a feature exists rather than how deep it goes. Each bullet links to the recommendation that carries the capability.

### 7.1 Microsoft Teams (new Teams), MSTeams 26260.1701.5139.3736

Teams is the enterprise incumbent. It is best at governance that an administrator can actually operate: policy precedence, locked settings, consent on AI in meetings, and a typed attention model that scales to many rooms. Its agent model is cautious and well-labelled rather than capable, and it shows no agent computer, browser or sandbox surface at all [REA].

- **Meeting AI governance.** Per-meeting AI on or off, a privacy mode that blocks Copilot and every agent, ephemeral versus retained answers by org policy, only selected people may change AI settings, and a recap visible only to people who can see the transcript [REA]. This is the consent model [F-t1-050](#F-t1-050) should adopt once calls exist, mapped onto Verevon's Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) flag.
- **Review-then-share.** When the agent's answer rests on data not every member may open, Teams shows it as a private draft with a warning, an Allow-to-send step and an expiry, and a hard "only you can see it" block for sensitivity-restricted content [REA, DOC]. It is the clearest user-facing expression of Verevon's rule that Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL, and it is the UX half of [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012) and [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011). The escalate-to-group dialog is meeting-scoped in Teams, so copy the pattern, not the exact flow [REA].
- **Explicit per-room knowledge list.** An agent's knowledge is a visible list of conversations; removing the agent deletes what it collected, and re-adding starts from zero [DOC]. This is the simplest honest answer to "what does the agent remember about this room", and the first Space memory scope in [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012) should look like it.
- **A typed activity feed.** The UI exposes about 60 titled activity types with inbox slices such as approvals waiting, failed runs and followed threads [REA]. It is the catalogue shape for Home "needs you" in [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009); the number is a count of UI string keys, not a verified server catalogue.
- **About 87 typed system notices,** including agent added and removed, and Loop live components where the message holds a pointer and the component keeps its own ACL [REA]. Both feed [F-t1-052](#F-t1-052): governance events should land in the room timeline, and artifact embeds should be pointers with their own authorization.
- **Policy administration.** Precedence direct over group over global, policy packages, admin allow or block per agent app, and four distinct refusal reasons shown as tooltips [DOC, REA]. This is the admin model for [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006), where Verevon today has deny-by-default policy rows but no writer except an operator script.
- **Strict content security.** Content Security Policy (CSP) with Trusted Types and Subresource Integrity (SRI) [REA]. Carried by [F-t1-030](#F-t1-030); Verevon's room markdown can load images from any https host today, which is an exfiltration channel for a prompt-injected agent.

### 7.2 Slack desktop, 4.52.178

Slack is the human-chat incumbent and the best reference for notification engineering, search under the asker's authority and the room's working documents. Most of its agent and server-side behaviour is documented rather than observed, so it shows the product shape, not the mechanism [DOC].

- **Search as the asking human.** Real-time Search uses a user token or an action token bound to the triggering event, scopes per content type, excludes guests and forbids storing results [DOC]. It is the template for [F-t1-038](#F-t1-038); Verevon's signed, audience-bound Control decisions are a stronger token than Slack's, so the mechanism is already in hand.
- **One validated notification payload** that passes through display and Do-Not-Disturb (DND) gates in the desktop shell with a trace id, DND with VIP break-through, and toasts with inline reply [SRC shell, DOC]. The server-side triggering is documentation only [DOC]. This shapes [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008), [F-t1-059](#F-t1-059) and the toast half of [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035). Slack is also the only reference with a "why am I not notified" self-diagnosis, carried by [F-t1-092](#F-t1-092) [SRC shell].
- **Huddles belong to the room,** with notes and a canvas attached to the conversation [DOC]. This is the product model for [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029), which now waits on a product decision because Verevon's own docs list huddles as a non-goal.
- **Canvas and Lists as the room's working documents** [DOC]. The user model for [F-t1-015](#F-t1-015) and [F-t1-058](#F-t1-058); buzz supplies the revision mechanism underneath.
- **Granular retention** at org, workspace, channel and DM level with an org lock, audit-log export to a SIEM, and posting permissions [DOC]. Carried by [F-t1-051](#F-t1-051), [F-t1-031](#F-t1-031) and the posting rules in [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006).
- **The operator grammar and keyboard model:** search modifiers, quick switcher, unread navigation, F6 region cycling [DOC, SRC shell]. Carried by [F-t1-069](#F-t1-069) (demoted to nice-to-have because no message search exists yet) and [F-t1-041](#F-t1-041).
- Not recommended: Slack's app-level approvals. Slack publishes design guidance for human-in-the-loop but ships no approval protocol [DOC], so it must not be counted as a shipped approval card; Verevon's durable approvals already go further.

### 7.3 Grok Bot desktop agent, 0.70.2

Grok Bot is the only reference that treats a personal agent with its own computer and browser as the product. It is best at agent lifecycle, live supervision and honest client engineering, and it offers nothing for human-to-human collaboration, so it should never be the model for the chat half [SRC].

- **Personal and team agents.** A primary "chief of staff" bot, Personal and Team visibility, an access list and publishing to the team [SRC]. Wire roles are USER and MANAGER; Owner and Collaborator are UI labels [SRC]. This is the Grok-Bot half of the vision, carried by [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011) and [F-t1-072](#F-t1-072).
- **The agent's computer and browser as a product.** Hibernate and pre-thaw, a live preview card that expands to an interactive view, a hand-off request with an instruction, Take control and an explicit hand-back [SRC]. Live view with takeover is 2 of 5 references (QM hands the person a provider live-view link), with Grok Bot best in class [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-020](#F-t1-020) and [F-t1-028](#F-t1-028), always with Quarry as the executor. The link from bot-wall classification to the hand-off flow was not traced and is inference [INF].
- **Multi-agent turn protocol.** A per-member turn request with nonce and deadline, speak, pass, skip and timeout outcomes, lineage ids and a loop guard in shadow mode [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-025](#F-t1-025); Verevon's UI-2c plan is one hop in one room and lacks the pass and skip protocol.
- **Approval UX.** Scope ladder, "card catching up" reconciliation, addressed approvals and Esc-to-deny; effective permission is the minimum of the user setting and the team ceiling [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) as the UX layer over QM's durable mechanism.
- **A multiplexed, sequenced transcript stream** with updated_seq cursors, cursor_too_old and generation frames [SRC]. It is flag-gated and mid-migration in the shipped build, not the default path [SRC]. It is still the best frame design for [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003).
- **Honest client engineering.** A send journal with stages queued, prepared, dispatching and accepted-awaiting-echo; resource state machines with stale-while-revalidate; typed previews with retired-card fallbacks; a coded error registry [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-033](#F-t1-033), [F-t1-052](#F-t1-052) and [F-t1-079](#F-t1-079). The notification rate limiter and the "notify only the active device" rule are shipped as a disabled config and a default-off flag respectively, so neither counts as implemented [SRC].
- **Cost by flow and by bot with hard limits** [SRC], carried by [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027); and a zero-retention mode that blocks the persistent sandbox with a typed no-storage error [SRC], which is one feature, not a general rule, and informs [F-t1-005](#F-t1-005).

### 7.4 Buzz, commit 799cfd705 (2026-10-12)

Buzz is a Slack-like room where agents are ordinary members, read from source. It is the best reference for human-first rooms, delivery-time authorization, mention integrity and the small, testable invariants a push substrate needs. Its agent supervision is good; its approval and credential story is not [SRC].

- **Human-only posts as a first-class event,** with agents that wake only on mention or subscription, and thread counters kept atomically at ingest [SRC]. This closes Verevon's largest gap and is the core of [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001). Note that QM has no native human-to-human room, so human-first rooms are 3 of 5 references natively.
- **Addressing and trigger policy.** A binding has respond_to and subscribe fields that fail closed, rule triggers and an address lock in the composer; mentioning an owned agent that is not in the room offers to invite it [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002) and [F-t1-007](#F-t1-007). Mention resolution and ambiguity rejection run client-side in buzz; a server-side check against the roster is Verevon's addition [INF].
- **Authorization at every delivery, and a content-free push wake.** One fail-closed fan-out chokepoint shared by local and cross-node paths, subscribe-then-register ordering, refcounted per-tenant topics, and a push that is only a wake signal while the client fetches the content [SRC]. This lets [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) push without a side door around Control and gives [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035) a ZDR-safe push.
- **Owner-of-agent retract, soft deletes kept in audit, and moderation that cascades from a banned person to that person's agents** [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017) and [F-t1-043](#F-t1-043); Verevon agents carry no owner link in Control today, which the ADR on service subjects must add.
- **Permission-checked message search.** A generated tsvector, NULL vectors for sensitive kinds, ranking with ts_rank_cd and per-hit re-authorization [SRC]. The index model for [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018).
- **Server timeline windows** with reply summaries and exact composite cursors, a tested scroll-anchoring policy, performance budgets measured while simulated agents flood the room, and room-behaviour eval suites that grade thread placement and silence [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-034](#F-t1-034), [F-t1-078](#F-t1-078) and [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027).
- **Selection-to-context hand-off** capped at 100 items and 16,000 characters, control characters collapsed, a fixed treat-as-data notice and a byte-identical pre-send preview [SRC]. The safest "discuss this with an agent" design, carried by [F-t1-032](#F-t1-032).
- Not recommended: buzz's audience-bound agent memory exists only in a draft design document and its ifc-core crate is unused [DOC], so [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012) treats QM as the shipped reference. Nostr as the wire, agents on user machines and auto-approved tool permissions are listed as patterns to avoid.

### 7.5 QM, commit 1e975255 (2026-10-12)

QM is a Teams-like agent harness for companies, read from source. It is the best reference for the mechanisms that make agents safe in shared rooms: audience folds, durable approvals, credential custody, egress tokens and fire-time re-authorization. It has no human messaging, presence, notifications, calls or i18n of its own, so it is the agent layer only [SRC].

- **Audience fold with write-time labels and a context cut when the audience changes** [SRC]. The only shipped audience intersection among the references; carried by [F-t1-012](#F-t1-012). Verevon already binds audience hash and revision into decisions and message rows, so the gap is memory, not transport.
- **Durable approvals with once, session and always scopes, requester-only decisions and a tighten-only tool policy** [SRC]. The card goes to the requester's DM when the conversation is thread-only [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010). Untrusted-content quarantine is unique to QM (1 of 5 references).
- **Credential broker and one-time secret drop.** The secret is bound to https, host and path, redirects are refused, and the transcript records only that a value was supplied [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-044](#F-t1-044) and [F-t1-026](#F-t1-026); Verevon's S3.4 plan matches it exactly.
- **Per-turn capability tokens and an authorizing egress proxy** with provider readback [SRC]. Egress is fail-open when the backend proxy URL is unset [SRC], which Verevon must not copy. Carried by [F-t1-021](#F-t1-021) and [F-t1-028](#F-t1-028).
- **Agent self-API.** A discoverable API catalogue with per-turn tokens whose claims are actor, scope and org [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-019](#F-t1-019); Verevon has 14 spaces.* actions and a governed pipeline for exactly one action.
- **Ambient participation.** A cheap judge returns act or not with a reason, standing orders, a bot ledger, and an explicit room I/O tool where silence is allowed [SRC]. The judge is unique to QM; Teams has only a string for "offer help proactively" [REA]. Carried by [F-t1-002](#F-t1-002) as the optional second step.
- **Live run stream any viewer can join** (AG-UI-shaped SSE with character-offset deltas and a snapshot), a cross-room session-state stream, and a per-session serialized run queue with SKIP LOCKED claims and leases [SRC]. Authorization is run visibility for the actor, not a room decision [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004), [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) and [F-t1-040](#F-t1-040).
- **Automation depth.** Fire keys, outcomes, loops with gates, recipient consent, pause-while-unread, goals with an independent governor, and webhook wake with per-provider verifiers [SRC]. Carried by [F-t1-036](#F-t1-036) and [F-t1-047](#F-t1-047). Smaller QM mechanisms carried elsewhere: reactions reach the agent as a reply directive parsed by regex and capped at 5 [SRC], so [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017) recommends a typed tool instead; a visibility-preserving fork and a versioned app publish with pointer-flip rollback [SRC] go to [F-t1-039](#F-t1-039) and [F-t1-088](#F-t1-088).

## 8. What Verevon already does well (protect it)

Verevon's authority model is stronger than any reference's: signed, short-lived, audience-bound decisions, deny-by-default policies, ZDR that fails closed, and agents as governed service subjects; its gaps are in the human chat layer and in wiring what exists into the room. Every item in this document must reuse the following, not route around it. Paths are relative to the CoresSystem repo at commit afb83928 [SRC].

- **Signed, effect-bound authority.** Ed25519-signed, two-minute Control decisions bind subject, org, Space, audience (ref, hash, revision), authority revision, privacy policy, action, schema hash, payload digest and idempotency key; the BFF strips 12 forged authority fields; Session Core verifies signature, expiry and privacy completeness (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/spaces/decision.go:4-60,145-222; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:34-47,60-98; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:1521-1790). Do not regress: every new transport (push, web push, CLI, webhooks, external channels) passes this one chokepoint; no plane caches a decision past its lifetime.
- **Deny-by-default effect policies.** space_effect_policies has no default row and absence denies, with separate bits for thread create, read, retrieval, import, schedule fire, agent action, sandbox capability and process registry (apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/018_space_effect_policies.up.sql). Do not regress: admin tooling in [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) adds writers and presets; it never adds a permissive default.
- **Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL.** Immutable audience snapshots (set, hash, revision) are delivered to Control through an outbox, messages carry audience columns at append, and reads drop rows newer than the decision revision; revoked members are denied through space_membership_revocations (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:853-903; apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceAudience.ts:32-76; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:2565-2580,5455-5461). Do not regress: search, file library, canvas, sharing and guests keep the intersection of Space decision, audience and owner-plane grant.
- **ZDR that fails closed in Session Core.** Verification found more than the baseline assumed: authorize_operation refuses every non-read scope on CreateThread, AppendMessage and StartRun for a ZDR caller, MemoryRetention gates durable and Letta memory, run output is refused, and a typed refusal catalogue plus SPA presentation module exist but are untracked in git (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/auth.rs:262-285; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/grpc.rs:1735-1775). Only the Space decision's own zero_data_retention flag is still decoded and discarded (grpc.rs:1749-1755). Do not regress: commit the catalogue; any push, cache, index, preview or digest carries references, not content, for ZDR orgs.
- **Agents as Control service subjects on the same roster as people.** Agents join Control-first, the UI shows the honest state "access granted, identity not published", and managed_subject_types stops a human roster edit from removing an agent (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceMembers.ts:31-44; apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:821-836; apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/017_space_authority.up.sql). Do not regress: personal agents in [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011) are still service subjects with owner-derived admission, never client-asserted members.
- **Binding policy enforced at the gateway with safe defaults.** trigger_modes, approval_mode and allowed_tools are applied server-side; an agent created in a room is born mention-only, with no tools and requiring confirmation; the instruction hierarchy platform, org, Space, user, agent is composed on the server and client-supplied values are stripped (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:4500-4573,3720-3888; apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:620-634; apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaces.ts:1503-1555). Do not regress: new binding fields (respond_to, subscribe, access list) extend this pipeline; allowed_tools stays a filter, never a grant.
- **Durable approvals that really resume.** Session Core stores an encrypted, action-bound continuation descriptor and a leaseable approval delivery outbox; an execution-core worker resumes three idempotent action kinds and fails closed with invalid_continuation for shell, browser and MCP tools (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/orchestration_grpc.rs:755-775; approval_delivery.rs and continuation_crypto.rs in the same service). This settles the "does a granted approval resume" dispute in the baseline: it does, for those three kinds. Do not regress: [F-t1-010](#F-t1-010) adds scopes and further action kinds on this worker; no approval path may suspend without persisting a continuation first.
- **Per-fire re-authorization of scheduled work.** capability-core's Sweeper re-authorizes every slot through Control with a fire key and idempotency key, and the Watch primitive is bound to one resource and one member with Control watch decisions (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/cron/sweeper.go:148-160; apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/watch/routes.go:15-33). This is stronger than QM. Do not regress: [F-t1-036](#F-t1-036) builds the room UI on it; no trigger fires on a stale decision.
- **The model-executable admission bar.** The tickets.create pipeline proves the shape: a Control owner-grant decision, an owner effect reservation, capability advertising and an execution-core adapter, with the decision forwarded to Conversation Core (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:743-937; apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/http/server.go:375). Do not regress: [F-t1-019](#F-t1-019) opens spaces.* actions through this bar, not around it.
- **Honest-state UX enforced in code.** Every composed Space response carries unavailable[] with section, code and reason, so "nothing here" and "could not ask" stay distinct; the room context is rechecked every 30 s and hidden on error; destructive actions arm in place and disarm after 6 s; personal-Space deletion shows per-plane receipts (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:1205-1222; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpacePage.tsx:317-353,1198-1248; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceConfirmButton.tsx:24-104). Do not regress: every new chip, count and card follows the contract, and the one known exception, the swallowed 403 at apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomTimeline.tsx:201, is fixed in [F-t1-030](#F-t1-030).
- **One event log with several lenses.** The Activity tab reads five owner sources (threads, runs with steps, approvals, owner receipts, authority grants) through one salience-ordered verb-object-outcome grammar with Everything, Needs attention, Work and effects, Conversations and Authority lenses, while the BFF stays a proxy (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceActivityPanel.tsx:56-83,145-259; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/lib/activity-grammar.ts:733-753). Equal to or ahead of buzz and QM. Do not regress: [F-t1-004](#F-t1-004) and [F-t1-009](#F-t1-009) extend the grammar; they do not add a second activity model.
- **Policy-gated grounding.** Room turns retrieve Space-bound documents under a separate retrieval_read_entitled bit, and a 403 degrades the turn to ungrounded instead of failing it; the knowledge_search tool maps to a capability and takes the org from the verified run (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/spaces.rs:225-280; apps/Control Plane/user-core/migrations/019_space_retrieval_effect_policy.up.sql; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/capability_policy.rs:780-800). Do not regress: [F-t1-038](#F-t1-038) and [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018) reuse this degrade-not-fail rule and re-authorize per hit.
- **Plane isolation, a credential-free BFF and a sandbox that fails closed.** The browser holds no upstream credential; the sandbox uses a read-only bubblewrap root, a cleared environment and namespace unshare, refuses unsandboxed spawn for restricted modes, and runs a seccomp deny-list; risky browser actions have per-action human-in-the-loop gates, and Quarry owns the DNS-pinned egress proxy (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/sandbox.rs:1-60,150-230; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/executor.rs:60-200; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/browser_agent/browser_risk.rs:1-60). Do not regress: the Model proposes and Quarry executes; [F-t1-021](#F-t1-021) closes the advisory-egress gap without a second network path.
- **Infrastructure to reuse rather than rebuild.** Transactional outboxes with leases (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/schema.ts:680-820); run-event SSE with after_event_id replay (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/orchestration_grpc.rs:3253-3268); durable read markers (apps/Application Plane/convex-core/convex/spaceReadMarkers.ts:1-153); notification-core's delivery state machine (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/notification/delivery_state.go:1-60); mid-run queued input under a fresh append decision (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/queued_input.rs:1-37); Space-turn artifacts persisted unless ZDR (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/sse.rs:4040-4060); the CAS-backed workspace promote (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/workspace_promote.rs:1-20); wiki-store's append-only versions (apps/Data Plane v2/services/wiki-store-go/internal/repo/wiki_repo.go:359-430); the verified webhook receiver (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/api/server.go:981-1100); and the typed action registry (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/actions/action-registry.ts:1991-2134). Do not regress: W1 and W2 are mostly wiring these into the room; a parallel store in the gateway or Convex for messages, reactions or canvases would break plane ownership.
- **Accessibility, skills and the six-tab cockpit.** aria-live rows for streaming activity, colour-independent status marks, a roving-focus tablist and reduced-motion queries are at or above buzz and QM; the skill picker sends structured skill ids resolved server-side rather than inserted text; the cockpit (Chat, Work, Knowledge, Activity, Agent, Members) shows more agent working context than Slack or Teams (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpacePage.tsx:1387-1397; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceCockpit.tsx:61-124,170-179; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/spaces/components/SpaceRoomComposer.tsx:375-470). The bilingual claim is partial: activity labels and dates are hard-coded Norwegian (activity-grammar.ts:118-137), which [F-t1-062](#F-t1-062) fixes. Do not regress: keep the six tabs as the default when [F-t1-070](#F-t1-070) adds extras, and keep every new row narrated.

## 9. Roadmap and decisions

This section turns the 96 recommendations into an order of work. Tiers and efforts are the verified values: 23 priority, 42 recommended and 31 nice-to-have items after verification, with 29 effort estimates revised and 5 tier changes (F-t1-005 moved from priority to recommended; F-t1-029, F-t1-066, F-t1-068 and F-t1-069 moved from recommended to nice-to-have). Where verification changed an item's effort (25 up, 4 down), the table shows the revised value with the original in brackets. "Plane" lists the owning planes in the order the verification named them. "Depends on" lists only the sequencing dependencies that matter for planning; fuller lists are in the item cards (the Recommended cards carry a Dependencies and risks paragraph); Appendix B does not list dependencies.

### 9.1 Implementation waves

#### Wave 1: foundations and quick wins

Goal: people can post and reply without a model turn, on a substrate that pushes, with ZDR (Zero-Data-Retention) hardening, an admin surface for the deny-by-default policies, and cheap fixes that users notice at once.

| ID | Title | Tier | Effort | Plane | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-t1-001](#F-t1-001) | Human-first room: human-only posts, shared threads, thread pane | priority | L | Model Plane, Control Plane, Frontend Plane | ADR 1 (message ownership); the existing member-capable Control thread:append decision (no new decision type); [F-t1-007](#F-t1-007) so agents are summoned explicitly |
| [F-t1-003](#F-t1-003) | Realtime substrate: event projection, resumable SSE, delivery-time authorization | priority | XL | Model Plane, Application Plane, Frontend Plane, Control Plane | ADR 1 and ADR 2; a new Control stream audience; [F-t1-033](#F-t1-033) client store |
