### [F-t1-015](#F-t1-015): Room canvas: a shared, revisioned Space document that humans and agents co-author
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** 1) Buzz CAS is not a single 'INSERT ... WHERE current_head = expected_head': it is a write transaction that takes a per-channel advisory lock, reads the head, compares it with the expected revision and only then inserts; same semantics, different mechanism. 2) 'The losing text stays in history' is overstated: a rejected save is rolled back and not stored; only accepted (later superseded) revisions remain in history. 3) GrokBot room canvases are written by agents through a WriteCanvas tool (full contents, no expected revision) and read by humans as a rendered bundle; human co-authoring is not evidenced. The lock/renew/release and tombstone/undelete RPCs belong to the agent file store, not room canvases, and locks return a lease lock_token with TTL; calling them 'fencing tokens' is an over-reach (FENCED is a sandbox manifest commit status). 4) 'CRDT co-edit' for Slack and Teams is inference: Slack's sync engine is undocumented in the evidence and Teams Loop uses the Fluid Framework (ordered ops); say 'real-time co-editing'. 'Block-anchored comments' for Slack is not in the cited evidence (comments behave like thread replies with resolve). 5) Teams evidence is meeting/chat notes (Loo…

**Caveats:** Slack canvas is DOC-only; channel canvases were retired in favour of a canvas tab (2025); free plan one canvas per conversation; Teams notes are REA from locale strings plus DOC; GrokBot canvases: agent-written, human read-only as far as observed (SRC proto names); Write-safety 2 of 5 holds only if GrokBot agent-store locks count; for room canvases specifically Buzz is the only one with CAS

### [F-t1-016](#F-t1-016): Direct messages and group DMs on Space authority, with conversation kind deciding agent scope and history window
Reference-side check: **confirmed** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** Minor: in Buzz, un-hiding on new activity is driven by the desktop client re-issuing the open-DM command (which clears hidden_at server-side), plus a server-side unhide for moderation notices; it is not an automatic server projection rule. Add that Buzz DM participant sets are immutable: 'adding' someone creates a different DM with no history, the opposite of Teams' add-with-history choice.

**Caveats:** Teams history choice is REA from locale strings (plus DOC limits: 250 per chat); Teams also requires full history access to add an agent to a chat; Buzz DMs are server-managed channels, not end-to-end encrypted; QM DMs come through Slack; QM ScopeId is a string 'kind:ref' encoding, copy the semantics not the encoding

### [F-t1-017](#F-t1-017): Message lifecycle and reactions: edit and delete as revision entries (owner-of-agent retract, revisions reach the agent), emoji reactions and a per-message action toolbar
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** Two mechanisms are wrongly attributed. (1) It says qm agents 'emit reactions through a typed tool capped at 5 per turn'. In fact qm uses a text directive in the model reply ([[react: name @ ~id]]) that the Slack adapter parses with a regex and caps at 5. The cluster itself recommended a typed tool as an improvement over qm. Rephrase as 'qm: reply directive, max 5; recommend a typed tool for Verevon'. Inbound reactions reach the agent as a rendered '[Slack reaction] ...' text turn, not a typed {reaction, target_seq, by} object. qm revision entries target the Slack message ts, not a seq. (2) It says 'slack: role=toolbar per message with roving focus and a more-menu'. The role=toolbar evidence is GrokBot (index-Bo3NzkuN.js sand-message-hover-actions). Slack's records cover the rich clipboard and the native context menu only, and roving focus was not verified anywhere. Also: buzz 'soft-deleted events kept in audit' comes from VISION.md (DOC), not code. buzz's client-side permission has no admin branch; admin delete comes only from the Slack/Teams policy docs. qm has no edit or delete UI of its own; it ingests Slack edit/delete events. So qm's place in the 4-of-5 edit/delete count mean…

**Caveats:** qm reactions and edits are Slack-surface features (qm reuses Slack's reactions and edit events); there is no qm-native reactions table; Slack and Teams edit/delete and admin policy flags are DOC (help center, learn.microsoft.com) plus Teams locale strings; buzz audit retention of soft-deleted events is DOC (VISION.md:147), not verified in code; GrokBot reactions and feedback votes are proto/RPC-schema level (SRC from bundle); wake-on-reaction behaviour not re-verified; Per-message action toolbar evidence is GrokBot; the more-menu content differs, and GrokBot has no edit/delete

### [F-t1-018](#F-t1-018): Permission-checked message search: Session Core index with per-hit re-authorization and ZDR exclusion, in-room scoped search on Ctrl/Cmd+F and open-in-context
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** The buzz mechanism is accurate. Small fixes: ranking uses ts_rank_cd, not ts_rank. Full-text mode uses websearch_to_tsquery, and prefix mode builds a to_tsquery with :* on the last token only. In R-t1-04-02, 'Esc or backspace widens' is buzz Backspace-on-empty-query only; I did not find an Esc handler. Prevalence for in-room scoped search with Ctrl/Cmd+F is overstated. GrokBot is in sourcesWith, but its search is agent-side (searching its own conversations and memory), not user-facing in-room scoped search, so drop it: 3 of 5 (buzz, teams, slack). Slack is only partial: its shell routes Find to in-app search (START_SEARCH), but the shipped shell does not show whether that search is pre-scoped to the room. For R-t1-04-01 (full-text search, 4 of 5), qm is confirmed by its own tsvector search over session entries. Slack has no member record and rests on product knowledge. Per-hit re-authorization is verified only in buzz. Teams search runs on a backend service (Substrate) we cannot see, so its permission model cannot be observed and Teams must not be cited for per-hit re-authorization. Teams being best for scope UX is justified, but only at the level of UI strings.

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** Teams scope list is REA/locale-string evidence only (UI copy), not observed behaviour; could be ring- or license-gated; Teams search engine is Substrate server-side; suggestions/init/events endpoints observed only as URLs in the shell's CORS preflight handler; Slack evidence is the desktop shell only (Find to START_SEARCH); scoped search semantics are web-client/server-side and not observed; qm full-text search exists (SRC), but I did not verify per-hit re-authorization in qm; Per-hit re-authorization is SRC-verified only in buzz; REA analyze_javascript_application used only on grokbot node-agent-coordinator (heap-limited partial); the rest is direct source/bundle grep

### [F-t1-019](#F-t1-019): Let room agents call Space actions themselves (agent self-API with run-bound grants)
Reference-side check: **confirmed** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** Say the token is minted per turn (sandbox turn token, 48 h default, configurable including no-expiry) and only optionally carries run id/attempt/lease; swarm operations are the ones that require a real durable run. Claims are actorId/scopeId/orgId (+ optional session/run fields), not literally 'principal'. Buzz: say 'about 25 CLI command groups covering most platform actions' rather than 'every action'; the CLI authenticates with NIP-98 signed HTTP auth events.

**Caveats:** qm feature-gated families (admin-only, swarmsEnabled); token compression is off by default (env switch); Buzz 'everything a human can do' comes from VISION.md intent; code shows about 25 command groups; Admin authority recheck inferred from the admin tool routing through the /v1/admin handlers (SRC), not separately tested here

### [F-t1-020](#F-t1-020): Room-scoped agent browser: space_ref on browser runs, a live preview card that expands to an interactive view, hand-off and take-over with a control indicator
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence medium).

**Corrections:** (1) Live view with human takeover is not unique to Grok Bot. QM's browse skill hands the person the provider's LIVE_VIEW link to finish sign-in walls, then re-runs. That makes 2 of 5, with Grok Bot best-in-class (embedded noVNC, explicit hand-back RPC). (2) 'Grok Bot hands off instead of retrying on bot walls' is partly INF. Only the non-retryable botBlock classification is SRC; the link from botBlock to the handoff flow was not traced. (3) A preview that is a periodic frame or low-rate VNC stream was not verified. The preview chunk only shows live/stream/asleep/wake/retry states. (4) QM sign-in profiles are used only in DMs; 'shown only in DMs' should say 'used only in DMs'. (5) space_ref on browser runs and a room-scoped preview card are Verevon proposals (INF). No reference ties browser runs to a shared multi-human room.

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** QM's agent browser is a prompt-level skill that depends on an org provider key (Kernel, Anchor or Browserbase). It is not core product UI, and its availability is configuration-dependent.; Grok Bot is built on the Cursor sand platform (Cursor backend error text, /opt/cursor recording staging). The Box is a cloud VM, and local/mac computer-use flags default to false.; Grok Bot teach mode evidence is protos, strings and chunk names (SRC strings). End-to-end skill authoring was not traced.; Control-owner badge evidence is the You're in control / Take control strings. An agent-side badge string was not separately confirmed.; No REA tool was used for this item. Direct reads of the pre-extracted Grok Bot bundle and the QM repo.

### [F-t1-021](#F-t1-021): Agent computer confinement: real network enforcement and resource caps, audience-bound egress, one SSRF-safe connector
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** Remove 'bubblewrap, Landlock and seccomp': no such strings exist in the shipped Grok Bot code and the sandbox helper binary is not shipped, so the enforcement mechanism is unknown (only the policy JSON contract, a Linux kernel preflight and macOS support are visible). State that on Windows the helper is network-proxy-only with no filesystem isolation. The cgroup step only attaches spawned processes to an externally provided workload cgroup (env-gated, Linux Box) and resets oom_score_adj; it does not itself set resource caps. 'Sandbox confinement 1 of 5' undercounts: QM also runs agents in hosted sandbox backends (E2B, Modal, Sprites, DO, smolmachines) with an optional egress proxy. QM egress is fail-open when the backend proxy URL is unset, and auditing exists only where the backend supports it. SSRF: Grok Bot link previews pin IP + SNI and re-validate up to 5 redirects; Buzz pins IP but disables redirects for webhooks; do not merge these into one source's mechanism.

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** Grok Bot sandbox enforcement binary not shipped; only the client contract is observable (contract SRC, enforcement INF); Grok Bot filesystem sandbox unsupported on Windows (proxy-only); QM egress enforcement conditional per backend, fail-open if proxy URL missing; Buzz SSRF guard is enumerated-deny (unlisted special ranges pass)

### [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022): Room administration basics: change and choose member roles with an owner guard, rename, topic, archive and restore, and keep the organization room roster in sync
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** 1) Buzz has no 'actor rank >= target rank' rule: is_elevated() is Owner|Admin, so an Admin may grant Owner; elevated grants on ADD are checked only in private channels (role CHANGES of existing members are checked on every visibility). Say 'elevated roles (owner/admin) can only be granted or changed by an owner/admin; the sole owner cannot be demoted, removed or leave'. 2) The TOCTOU guard is a per-channel Postgres advisory lock held across the transactional check-then-write, not a 'membership-revision check'. 3) Starter rooms and the welcome agent are client-orchestrated in the Buzz desktop app during onboarding (Tauri command ensure_starter_channels creates-or-joins #general/#welcome-everyone; the kickoff starts locally managed agents), not a server-side 'on member join' hook; present server-side ensure_starter_rooms(org)+auto-join as Verevon's design, not as what Buzz does. 4) room.state {active, archived, deleted(restore_until)} is a synthesis: Buzz has archive/unarchive/delete and TTL auto-archive; the restore window comes from Teams (DOC, 30 days). 5) Lifecycle prevalence 2 of 5 (buzz, teams) undercounts: Slack supports archive, rename and topic (public product behaviour, IN…

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** Slack role set is DOC-only (help center), not REA; Teams 30-day restore is DOC (Learn limits note) plus locale-string REA; Buzz onboarding/starter rooms are desktop-client choreography; the Buzz doc itself lists open failure classes; TTL rooms (sliding deadline + reaper) are SRC-confirmed and Buzz-unique

### [F-t1-023](#F-t1-023): Enterprise identity: stop the fake-success SCIM stub, real SCIM 2.0 with deprovisioning that suspends a person's agents, per-org SSO enforcement and domain verification; linked identities later
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence medium).

**Corrections:** (1) Teams is not a SCIM reference: Teams relies on Entra ID provisioning; its best-in-class evidence is the native OneAuth/WAM client broker and HRD against Microsoft's own identity cloud, not a per-org IdP service-provider pattern. Name Slack (DOC) as the SCIM reference and say SCIM is Business+/Enterprise plan-gated. (2) The Slack help article does not state the SCIM version; 'SCIM 2.0' needs Slack API docs or should be phrased as Verevon's choice. (3) 'unredeemed_invite' is an interpretation of Teams' UserAuthState UnRedeemed (INF). (4) Allowlist 'checked at sign-up, invite claim and agent invocation' is a design extrapolation: QM's actor gate only filters Slack ingress (mentions, DMs, reactions) and Buzz's is a relay pubkey allowlist. (5) SSO 4/5 counts Buzz, whose federated identity is relay-side only with shadow/enforce modes and a client flow marked planned, and no SAML; count Buzz as partial. (6) Deprovisioning that suspends a person's agents is Verevon design, not a reference behaviour.

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** Slack SCIM and SSO are DOC and Business+/Enterprise plan-gated; Slack direct-SAML sign-in method is a darwin-only managed preference (SRC); Teams identity facts are native strings/RPC names only; QM principal links are SRC+DOC (verified docs and file)

### [F-t1-024](#F-t1-024): Client resilience: root and per-region error boundaries, lazy-chunk recovery, a boot watchdog, and a build id with stale-tab reload and contract guard
Reference-side check: **partial** (confidence high).

**Corrections:** (1) Prevalence of crash/render recovery is 4 of 5, not 3: buzz also has a root React error boundary that shows a 'Buzz failed to start' screen with a Reload button (desktop/src/app/RootErrorBoundary.tsx). That makes it table stakes. QM: none found. (2) The Teams hang toast has Wait / Trace / Restart. desktop.json has no Reload button string. (3) The Teams error curtain is a native-shell screen for when the web client fails to start. It is not a render/chunk error boundary. No reference showed per-region boundaries, lazy-chunk recovery, or a build-id stale-tab reload: those parts of the title are Verevon proposals and must be labelled INF. (4) Grok Bot has no generic feature_min_versions map. Remote config sand_min_client_version has fixed fields: min_version, backend_min_version, novnc_port_token_min_version, hosted_mcp_oauth_callback_min_version, max_age_days (default 14). Only min_version and max_age_days have client-side consumers. The per-feature fields appear only in the schema and fallback, so their enforcement is presumably server-side (INF). (5) SAND_CLIENT_UPDATE_REQUIRED arrives as ConnectError metadata and starts an auto-updater check (trigger backend_gate). A 'banner o…

**Prevalence in the first draft was wrong; the corrected figure is given in the card.**

**Caveats:** Teams staged nag-then-block staleness policy is DOC-only (Learn teams-client-update). The shell strings only show an update toast.; Teams curtain and repair are native (work when WebView2 fails). Many Teams recovery behaviors are ECS-flag controlled (enableTeamsRepair, enableUserInitiatedRepair, ...).; Slack crash-integrity restart path is behind the crash_integrity_check flag. The unresponsive timeout is 5 s, or 15 s with the extended_unresponsive_timeout flag.; Grok Bot per-feature version floors: schema-level evidence only, enforcement not observed client-side.; No REA tool was used for this item. Evidence was re-read directly from the installed Teams locale JSON and the pre-extracted Slack and Grok Bot bundles.

## Appendix D. What code verification changed

Code verification read the Verevon implementation for every recommendation. Outcome: 96 recommendations kept in the list, 5 moved down one tier, 0 dropped as already built; 78 were reworded because the code showed a more precise gap, a better place to build it, or reusable parts.

### Tier changes after code verification

- [F-t1-005](#F-t1-005) Make zero-data-retention binding in every plane, with typed refusals and content-free telemetry: Priority to Recommended. Largely exists in the code, and the item's central premise (Session Core ignores ZDR and must be made to refuse durable thread, message and memory writes) misdescribes the state. Keep only the residual hardening above and move it out of the W1 priority slot.
- [F-t1-029](#F-t1-029) Room calls on a bought SFU with Control-authorized join and a navigation-persistent call bar, modelled as a meeting record that belongs to the Space: Recommended to Nice to have. Nothing exists today, the product doc lists huddles as a non-goal, and the feature rests on three absent substrates, so it should not sit in the top ranks; keep it as a later-wave, decision-gated item rather than drop it, since calls are table stakes in the Teams and Slack references.
- [F-t1-066](#F-t1-066) Agents attend Zoom, Meet and Teams meetings as visible guests through an Ingestion capture worker, with approval-gated chat drafts: Recommended to Nice to have. Correctly described as absent, but it depends on F-t1-050 (which itself lacks a shared-room authority path), on an unresolved Channel versus Ingestion ownership decision and on external ToS and consent risk. It belongs after the upload-based meeting MVP.
- [F-t1-068](#F-t1-068) Screen sharing in calls with a sharing indicator, sensitive-surface redaction, and the same cue for agents acting in a room: Recommended to Nice to have. Absent, hard-dependent on a call stack that also does not exist, and contrary to the documented Space non-goal on huddles; only the agent-acting cue half deserves to stay as a small separate item.
- [F-t1-069](#F-t1-069) Search operators with Verevon-native filters, typed result tabs and recent-search history: Recommended to Nice to have. Nothing exists and the item is blocked on message search that does not exist; operators are a refinement of search, not a first-order Space need, so it should follow F-t1-018 rather than lead.

### Effort estimates changed by verification

- [F-t1-005](#F-t1-005) Make zero-data-retention binding in every plane, with typed refusals and content-free telemetry: M to S. M to S: the enforcement matrix, typed catalogue and cross-plane refusals are built; what is left is committing work in progress, typing two refusal sites, one cross-check and a product note. Applying per-Space ZDR would be a separate M or L item.
- [F-t1-006](#F-t1-006) Space governance admin: effect-policy admin UI with safe presets and typed denials first, then posting rules, agent/model allowlist, locked switches, autonomy posture and rollout flags: L to XL. Raised from L as one item: phase 1 alone is L (new Control route, typed denial codes across the Space decision endpoints, admin UI), and phases 2 and 3 add an owner for model allowlists, human-first posting rules, a posture and a flag registry.
- [F-t1-008](#F-t1-008) Space notifications: typed signals for mentions, replies, approvals and agent completions, refs-only payload, rate limits, per-room levels and thread follow/mute: L to XL. Raised from L: signals originate in three planes, structured mentions do not exist on the wire, and in-app delivery under ZDR needs a new rule; slice one (taxonomy, allowlist, mention/approval/agent_done producers, deep link) is L and per-room levels plus follow/mute add M.
- [F-t1-011](#F-t1-011) Personal agents with an owner-controlled access list and add policy, a private assistant beside the room, and configurable room bindings: L to XL. Raised from L as one item: slice one is S to M, the Control access list and add policy is M to L, and personal agents with the private panel is XL.
- [F-t1-019](#F-t1-019) Let room agents call Space actions themselves (agent self-API with run-bound grants): M to L. Raised from M. Each action needs an owner endpoint, a Control decision target, a pinned schema and an execution-core adapter, and the single-action machinery must first be generalized.
- [F-t1-021](#F-t1-021) Agent computer confinement: real network enforcement and resource caps, audience-bound egress, one SSRF-safe connector: L to M. L to M for what is worth doing now (resource caps, one cross-plane lease rule). The netns-with-proxy and audience-intersection egress remain L and should be deferred behind a concrete need.
- [F-t1-022](#F-t1-022) Room administration basics: change and choose member roles with an owner guard, rename, topic, archive and restore, and keep the organization room roster in sync: M to L. Raised from M: role change and archive each touch Control's contract and state machine; only the reconcile and rename or topic remain quick wins (S each).
- [F-t1-023](#F-t1-023) Enterprise identity: stop the fake-success SCIM stub, real SCIM 2.0 with deprovisioning that suspends a person's agents, per-org SSO enforcement and domain verification; linked identities later: L to XL. Raised from L as one item: the 501 fix is S, per-org SSO with domain verification is M, SCIM Users is L, Groups and agent suspension add L; recommend splitting.
- [F-t1-024](#F-t1-024) Client resilience: root and per-region error boundaries, lazy-chunk recovery, a boot watchdog, and a build id with stale-tab reload and contract guard: S to M. Raised from S. Boundaries plus chunk recovery plus a build-id banner is S; the gateway contract-version header, a client-diagnostics route and draft-safe forced reload make the full item M.
- [F-t1-027](#F-t1-027) Agent operations: per-room and per-agent cost and budgets, admin allow/block, answer-quality surface, and room-behaviour evals: L to XL. Four separable work items are bundled; cost attribution alone is L because cost-core has no space or agent dimension, session-core token fields are 0 and several producers change.
- [F-t1-034](#F-t1-034) Scalable room timeline: server-computed windows with exact cursors and reply summaries, virtualised rows with scroll anchoring, jump-to-latest and history paging: M to L. Raised from M: the change spans a Session Core migration and query, model-gateway, the Rust gateway, the SPA windowing and an anchoring module with tests. The client-only slice stays S and is the real quick win.
- [F-t1-035](#F-t1-035) Attention outside the open tab: installable PWA, content-free Web Push with service-worker Open/Reply, in-tab toasts, tab-title and app badge: L to XL. As written (PWA plus Web Push plus toasts plus badge, with five dependencies) it is more than L; sliced, A is S, B is M and C is L, and C is blocked on notification-core event producers and the delivery ledger.
- [F-t1-036](#F-t1-036) Room automations: create schedules, watches, calendar and event triggers from the room, with fire outcomes, recipient consent, goals and run traces: L to XL. As bundled it spans Control, capability-core, gateway, integration-corev2 and the Work tab and contains two L slices (shared-audience Control contracts, calendar trigger plus write) before goals; the fire-log and pause slice alone is S-M.
- [F-t1-043](#F-t1-043) Room moderation: report, queue, tombstones, user block, and timeout or ban that also stops the person's agents: L to XL. Raised from L: it touches Control (schema, decision issuance), Model Plane (Session Core moderation write and purge), Application (reports, sanctions), Data Plane purge, a durable revocation event and the UI.
- [F-t1-045](#F-t1-045) Agent runtime guardrails for shared rooms: member-text screening, shell command policy, room approvals, secret masking, DLP: L to M. M for the live slice (a) plus (e); the deferred (b), (c), (d) are S-M each but blocked on S3.2, S3.4 and room agentic access, so the full bundle stays L.
- [F-t1-047](#F-t1-047) Webhooks that wake room agents (Space-bound inbound endpoints): M to L. Was M and marked a quick win. A Control decision kind for shared rooms, an endpoint record, a Model-side trigger, payload trust handling and UI make it L; it is not a quick win.
- [F-t1-050](#F-t1-050) Meeting intelligence with consent: long-form transcription and an authority-scoped recap saved as a Space document, under a deny-by-default per-meeting AI policy: L to XL. Was L. Long-form ASR with diarisation, a shared-room Control decision and policy, Data Plane ingestion with speaker metadata, a recap writer, a Meetings tab and a manager role path together are XL; split into the ASR and recap MVP (L) and the policy and authority work (M plus M).
- [F-t1-051](#F-t1-051) Retention policies for rooms, files and agent sessions, plus shared-Space deletion on the existing saga: L to XL. L to XL: six owner planes need new time-bounded purge contracts and a new authority class, Session Core purge is owner-bound and must be redesigned for shared rooms, and shared-Space deletion adds approval and write-fence work. Phase 1 alone is L.
- [F-t1-053](#F-t1-053) Guests and link invitations with mandatory expiry: L to XL. L to XL: no guest primitive exists in any plane, and the work spans an identity-model decision, auth-core, a Control membership schema and sync change, Data Plane visibility treatment, billing seats and agent-audience fencing.
- [F-t1-056](#F-t1-056) Agent task tracker: Needs-input state, per-task threads and unread in the Work tab: M to L. Was M and a quick win. There is no agent producer, no Space authority on tasks and no clarification pause, so L.
- [F-t1-057](#F-t1-057) Artifact sharing: edit role, Space-as-subject grants, general access and access requests: M to L. M to L: the grant write surface is fenced off in Control and needs a new reviewed delegation mode, the taxonomy has a Go/Rust parity lint, and Space-subject evaluation needs a Control read-time decision. Drop the quick-win flag.
- [F-t1-065](#F-t1-065) Governed Space skills and plugin bundles (ecosystem-compatible manifests): L to XL. Raised from L: the plugin half needs a plugin host that does not exist. Governed Space skills alone are L; static-skill bundle import adds M.
- [F-t1-068](#F-t1-068) Screen sharing in calls with a sharing indicator, sensitive-surface redaction, and the same cue for agents acting in a room: M to XL. M holds only for the incremental share track after a call stack exists; none exists, and an SFU plus authorization and signalling is an XL by itself. The agent-cue half alone is S.
- [F-t1-074](#F-t1-074) Provenance footer on relayed and automated messages and notifications; optional system voice: M to S. Lowered from M for the recommended slice (room-post footer for scheduled runs) because the provenance is already stored; the notification-payload part waits on F-t1-008 and system voice is dropped.
- [F-t1-081](#F-t1-081) Long-tail polish: role-aware agent templates and persona library, owner-reviewed config proposals, catch-up summaries, external MCP surface: M to L. Raised from M: the first four slices are each S to M, but the MCP surface alone is XL (no inbound server, empty model-eligible Space action list, depends on external token auth), so the bundle as written is L or larger.
- [F-t1-082](#F-t1-082) Long-tail polish: voice and video clips with transcripts, image markup, per-viewer translation, room lenses: M to L. As one bundle M undercounts (four unrelated features, one needs a Control policy and one needs attachments). Split estimate: dictation S, translation S-M, voice clips M after attachments, announcement mode M, markup M.
- [F-t1-085](#F-t1-085) Live in-meeting AI layer: captions, an optional interpreter agent and a facilitator agent (agenda, timers, live context): M to L. M only counts the increment over the call stack and live ASR; streaming ASR with per-speaker captions, fan-out, translation and a published TTS track is L, and XL if the call infrastructure is included.
- [F-t1-087](#F-t1-087) Read-only snapshot share of a room or thread with an allowlisted projection: M to L. M undercounts: a Control decision class, an Application snapshot store, the deletion and erase fan-out, a hardened session-less route and the creator re-check on every view.
- [F-t1-092](#F-t1-092) Delivery health: channel failover and a 'why am I not notified' diagnosis: S to M. Raised from S: S assumed push, preferences and a per-channel ledger exist; the ledger is per-attempt for Novu and push is absent. The diagnosis banner alone is S once push exists; failover is the M part.

## Appendix E. Glossary

### Verevon terms

- **ADR (Architecture Decision Record).** A short record of one architecture decision and its trade-offs. "ADR needed" means leadership must decide before the affected items are built.
- **Plane.** A service group in the CoresSystem monorepo that owns one domain. Control Plane: identity, users, organisations, billing, sessions, audit, quotas, entitlements. Data Plane v2: documents, chunks, embeddings, retrieval, graph, wiki. Ingestion Plane: captures evidence and persists durable knowledge through Data Plane contracts (it holds Quarry v2 and integration-corev2). Model Plane: reasoning, sessions and runs, inference, execution loops, capabilities, sandboxes, browser grants, cost. Application Plane: collaborative and realtime workspace projections and notifications. Frontend Plane: the Verevon v3 user interface and the same-origin gateway. Support Plane: the RustDesk-compatible remote-support rendezvous and relay. Channel Plane: future, documentation only. Planes do not read each other's databases.
- **Space.** Verevon's governed room shared by people and agents.
- **Quarry (Quarry v2).** Verevon's web acquisition engine in the Ingestion Plane: crawl, scrape, extract and browser automation. The Model Plane proposes browser actions; Quarry executes or rejects them.
- **BrowserBroker.** The Model Plane service that issues browser grants, which are the permission for one run to act in a browser.
- **integration-corev2 (IC).** The Ingestion Plane service that executes provider actions and connector calls.
- **ZDR (Zero Data Retention).** A setting under which content must not be persisted or logged. In Verevon it has to hold in every plane that could store content.
- **W1 to W5.** The implementation waves in section 9.
- **OD n, D n, H n, E n, I n, S n.m.** Identifiers of open decisions, decisions and plan rows in Verevon's own planning documents (for example "open decision OD19"). They are kept as written so the team can look them up.

### General terms

- **ACL.** Access control list: the rules saying who may do what to a resource.
- **API / SDK / CLI.** Application programming interface; software development kit; command-line interface.
- **AX (accessibility tree).** The structured description of a page that screen readers use; browser agents read it to find elements.
- **BFF.** Backend for frontend: a server layer that serves one frontend and calls the other services on its behalf.
- **BYO.** Bring your own (for example your own keys or models).
- **C2PA.** A standard for attaching provenance information to generated media.
- **CAPTCHA.** A challenge meant to tell humans from bots.
- **CAS.** Content-addressed storage: files are stored and found by a hash of their content.
- **CDP.** Chrome DevTools Protocol: the remote-control interface of Chromium browsers.
- **CEL.** Common Expression Language: a small language for writing policy conditions.
- **CI.** Continuous integration (automated build and test runs).
- **CSP.** Content Security Policy: a browser header that limits what a page may load or run.
- **DLP.** Data loss prevention.
- **DM.** Direct message.
- **DND.** Do not disturb.
- **DOM.** Document Object Model: the live tree of a web page.
- **DOCX / OOXML.** Microsoft Office's zipped XML document formats.
- **GDPR / HIPAA.** European and United States data-protection laws.
- **HITL.** Human in the loop: a person approves or steers an agent step.
- **HMAC.** A keyed hash used to sign data so tampering is detectable.
- **IDE.** Integrated development environment (a code editor).
- **IPC.** Inter-process communication.
- **JSONL.** JSON Lines: one JSON object per line.
- **JWT.** JSON Web Token: a signed token that carries claims.
- **LLM.** Large language model.
- **MCP.** Model Context Protocol: an open protocol for giving agents tools and data.
- **MSIX.** The Windows app packaging format used by Teams, Slack, Claude, Codex and Manus on this audit's machine.
- **NATS.** A messaging system used between Verevon services.
- **NIP.** Nostr Implementation Possibility: a numbered specification of the Nostr protocol (used by Buzz).
- **OCR.** Optical character recognition (reading text from images).
- **OTLP / OTel.** OpenTelemetry protocol and project, a standard for traces, metrics and logs.
- **OTP.** One-time password (the code sent by email or an authenticator app).
- **PII.** Personally identifiable information.
- **PTY.** A pseudo-terminal, used to run an interactive shell for an agent.
- **PWA.** Progressive web app (a website that installs and works like an app).
- **REA.** The reverse-engineering toolkit used in this audit to analyse the installed apps. In provenance tags, [REA] means a fact observed through REA on a shipped app.
- **RPC.** Remote procedure call.
- **S3.** Amazon's object storage API, also offered by compatible stores such as MinIO.
- **SCIM.** A standard for provisioning users and groups from an identity provider.
- **SEA.** Node.js single executable application: a Node program packaged as one binary.
- **SFU.** Selective forwarding unit: a media server that relays audio and video in group calls.
- **SIEM.** Security information and event management (a central security log and alerting system).
- **SPA.** Single-page application.
- **SRI.** Subresource integrity: a hash that lets a browser verify a loaded script.
- **SSE.** Server-sent events: a one-way stream from server to browser over HTTP.
- **SSO.** Single sign-on.
- **SSRF.** Server-side request forgery: tricking a server into fetching an internal address.
- **STT / ASR / TTS.** Speech to text; automatic speech recognition; text to speech.
- **TLS.** Transport Layer Security (encryption of network connections).
- **TTL.** Time to live: how long something is kept or valid.
- **UI / UX.** User interface; user experience.
- **VM / microVM.** Virtual machine; a very small, fast-starting virtual machine used for sandboxing.
- **WASM.** WebAssembly, a portable binary format that runs in browsers and sandboxes.
- **WS.** WebSocket, a two-way connection between browser and server.
